# Periya Tiruvandhadi

## 1. Muyarri sumandu

*muyaṟṟi sumandu eḻundu munduṟṟa nenjē iyaṟṟuvāy emmodo nī kūdi nayappudaiya nāvīn todaikkiḷavi utpodivōm naṟpūvaip pūvīnṟa vaṇṇan pugaḻ*

**Пословный перевод:**

muyaṟṟi sumandu – being involved enthusiastically (in carrying out service to emperumān); eḻundu – rising up intensely; muduṟṟa nenjē – Oh heart who has gone ahead (of me)!; nal – superior; pūvaip pū īnṟa – in the flower kāyāmbū (a type of flower which has indigo like colour); vaṇṇan – sarvēśvaran, who has that colour, his; pugaḻ – auspicious qualities; nayappudaiya – having grace; nā In – being created by tongue; todaikkiḷaviyuḷ – words with beautiful sounds, strung beautifully; podivōm – we will keep constantly; emmodu – along with me; nī kūdi – you too join; iyaṟṟuvāy – praise

**Перевод:**

**muyaṟṟi sumandu – being involved enthusiastically in the kainkaryam to be carried out by the faculty of speech, which is being mentioned in the latter part of the pāsuram. The word muyaṟṟi indicates enthusiasm. The way āḻvār’s heart is proceeding ahead of āḻvār is analogous to the way iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) proceeded ahead of perumāḷ (śrī rāma), as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 33-28 “prāgēva tu mahābāga: saumitrir mithra nandana: I pūrvajasyānuyātrārtē drumasīrair alankruta: īī” (in order to go behind his elder brother, lakśmaṇa, who is fortunate, who makes the favourable people happy and who is sumitrā’s son, dressed himself with the wooden barks, ahead of śrī rāma). Did not kaikēyi give the dress made of wooden bark to śrī rāma first and then only to lakśmaṇa? How then can it be said that lakśmaṇa wore the bark ahead of śrī rāma? śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 21-17 says “dīptamagnimaraṇyam vā yadi rāma: pravēkśayati I praviśtam tatra mām dēvi tvam pūrvamadāraya īī” ((lakśmaṇa tells his mother sumitrā) Oh mother! If śrī rāma enters a raging fire or a forest, know that I would have entered it ahead of him). Thus, knowing the basic nature of lakśmaṇa, it is not a fault to say that lakśmaṇa wore the dress made of wooden bark ahead of śrī rāma. The opinion here is that unlike śrī rāma, who was wearing the bark in order to fulfil the words of his father, lakśmaṇa wore it because śrī rāma was going to wear it and hence this is more significant.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As soon as āḻvār told his heart that one should carry out vācika kainkaryam (carrying out service to emperumān through the faculty of speech), the heart set out alone, in search of emperumān, without taking āḻvār with it. āḻvār tells his heart to take him along. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**munduṟṟa nenjē** – Just as lakśmaṇa told śrī rāma as in the śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam “*yaditvam prastidōdurkam vanamadyaiva rāghava* I *agratastē gamiśyāmi mrutnatī kuśakaṇṭakān* īī” (Oh rāghava! Even if you are proceeding to the difficult-to-go-to forest today itself, I will go ahead of you, stamping on the grass and thorns and destroying them \[so that it will be easier for you to walk on the cleared path\]), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart “Oh heart, who is ahead of me!”

**iyaṟṟuvāy emmodu nī kūdi** – \[āḻvār tells his heart\] Wasn’t it I who instructed you about service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? Could you go, leaving me behind, forgetting that gratitude? It is indeed good that you are enthusiastic about carrying out service ahead of me. Take me too, along with you. What will the two of us do, together, asks the heart. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) states further “We are going to carry out kainkaryam to emperumān, together”

**nayappudaiya**  – having superiority. Just as nammāḻvār has mercifully mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 7-9-10 “*ennāl tannaip padaviya inkavi pādiya*” (he \[emperumān\] composed beautiful songs on himself, using me as a tool), the poem will add superiority to the one about whom it speaks. Adding superiority to the lead character is creating greatness, which was not there earlier.

**nāvīn** – a poem created through the tongue. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart “Oh heart! Neither you nor I would be needed to create this poem. Tongue itself is enough for this”. āḻvār says that his heart and he are required only to retain this poem in the heart and to enjoy it. Is it possible to create a poem without the presence of heart? The meaning implied is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will remain in the heart and make it to compose the poem. ḍidn’t āḻvār say in tiruvāimoḻi “*ennāl tannaip pādiya*”! (emperumān who sang about himself through me)

**todaikkiḷavi** – words which have been strung beautifully. The meaning implied here is that since the poem was not composed troubling the heart, there is no harshness in the words.

**utpodivōm** – taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli says “*yatō vācō nivartantē aprāpya manasā saha* I *ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān na bibhēti kutścanēti*  īī” (The heart, with the words, set out to measure the happiness of emperumān and returned, being unable to accomplish the task. One who knows the happiness of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will not fear anything). vēdānta could not measure the happiness of emperumān and returned empty handed. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is full of zeal to contain the auspicious qualities of that emperumān inside his words.

**naṟpūvai pūvīnṟa pugaḻ** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that the subject matter \[emperumān\] cannot be contained inside words.

**naṟpūvai** – While the colour of the flower *pūvaippū* (indigo coloured flower) may, to an extent, match the complexion of  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form, his fragrance and gentleness, the simile would end just there as the flower will wither away in a while. Since the normal *pūvaippū* will not match the perennial greatness of emperumān, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is qualifying the flower by calling it as *narpūvai*  (good flower).

**pūvīnṟa vaṇṇan** – it could be taken as either the flower which manifests the bluish colour of emperumān or emperumān who reveals the colour of the flower.  This is similar to the saying “*gō sadruśō gavaya:  gavaya sadruśō gau:*” (the animal *gavaya* is looking like cow and the cow is looking like the animal *gavaya*) in which both the simile and the object have got interchanged.

**pugaḻ** – the qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form, not made of primordial matter, and qualities of emperumān’s basic nature, full of knowledge and happiness.

We will move on to the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram, next.

## 2. Pugalvom palippom

*pugaḻvōm paḻippōm pugaḻōm paḻiyōm igaḻvōm madippōm igaḻōm madiyōm maṟṟu engaṇmāl sengaṇmāl seeṟal nee tīvinaiyōm engaḷ māl kaṇḍāy ivai*

**Пословный перевод:**

pugaḻvōm – if we are to praise (you); paḻippōm – (since it won’t be apt for your auspicious qualities) we will end up ridiculing you; pugaḻōm – if we do not praise; paḻiyōm – we will refrain from ridiculing (since my faulty words will not come anywhere near your auspicious qualities); maṟṟu – moreover; madippōm – if we think (about you); igaḻvōm – we will end up insulting you; madiyōm – if we do not think (of you); igaḻōm – we will not end up insulting you; engaḷ māl – having affection towards us; sem kaṇ māl – having reddish, divine eyes; nee – you (who cannot be estimated); seeṟal – do not be angry; ivai – engaging in praising you and refraining from praising you; tīvinaiyōm – horrible sinners; engaḷ – our; māl kaṇḍāy – bewilderment, please see

**Перевод:**

**pugaḻvōm paḻippōm – If we praise matters related to emperumān, we will actually end up ridiculing him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram, āḻvār said that he will speak about the auspicious qualities of emperumān with the words “nāvīn todaikkiḷavi utpodivōm”. However, just as vēdas came back empty handed when they set out to measure the qualities of emperumān as mentioned in the taittirīya upaniśat ślōkam “yatō vācō nivartantē …  ” āḻvār too steps back saying that this is not a matter that he could speak about \[describing the auspicious qualities of emperumān\]. This is similar to āḷavandār (one of our principal preceptors) saying in his stōtra ratnam 7 “tatvēna yasya mahimārṇavaśīkarāṇu: śakyō na mātumapi śarvapitāmahādyai: I kartum tadīya mahimastutimudyatāya mahyam namō’stu kavayē nirapatrapāya īī ” (I started praising about emperumān, in whose ocean of greatness, even a drop could not be estimated by Siva, brahmā et al; I must salute myself, who am shameless and who earned the name of being a poet). The opinion indicated here for stepping back from praising emperumān is – singing paeans about a matter is to mention about all the greatness regarding that matter and to further praise the matter with greatness which it does not possess. In the case of emperumān, even listing out his greatness in the first place is an impossible task. This is why śrī āḷavandār spoke disparagingly of himself when he started singing the greatness of emperumān. What is the reason for nammāḻvār to praise emperumān in the first pāsuram and to step back in the second pāsuram by saying that praising emperumān is similar to ridiculing him? Both acts (of praising and stepping back) are due to the greatness of matter related to emperumān. When one sees something great, it is apt to enjoy it for himself. In the same way, when one starts praising emperumān and finds that it impossible to do so as the greatness of emperumān is beyond one’s faculties of mind and speech, it is apt to step back, thinking “my attempt to praise emperumān and create a greatness for him is ending up as a ridicule for him” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pugaḻōm paḻiyōm** – If we refrain from praising him, that itself will be equivalent to not ridiculing  him. But will this meaning not be derived in the earlier verse itself (*pugaḻvōm paḻippōm*)? It is more strongly emphasised in this negative assertion (*vyatirēkam*) than in the positive assertion (*anvayam*) there.  Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has shown that it is better not to indulge with the faculty of speech, in matter related to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Next, he shows the same with the faculty of mind.

**igaḻvōm madippōm** – We should change the order of the verse to *madippōm igaḻvōm*. The opinion here is that if we think of emperumān through our heart, we will end up insulting him. Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya samhitā 7-5-3 “*mana: pūrvō vaguttara:*” (it is the mind which precedes speech), we speak through our faculty of speech whatever the faculty of mind thinks. But, will it not be construed here that whatever has been mentioned in *pugaḻvōm paḻippōm* is being repeated here? This is not correct since even though we have to carry out atonement for the sins committed through the faculty of thinking, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is bringing this up here in order to show that we would have committed the sin of insulting matters relating to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) even if we think of them.

**madiyōm igaḻōm** – If we do not think of matters relating to emperumān we would not have insulted them.

**maṟṟu** – this shows that the activity of saying something through the faculty of speech is different from thinking of the same through the faculty of mind. Thus the word *maṟṟu* should be brought forward to get the verse *maṟṟu igaḻvōm madippōm* and the meaning should be taken accordingly. It is by combining the meanings in these two lines and the meaning in *kēnōpaniśat* which starts with the verse *yasyā matam* that [śrī parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) mercifully composed in his śrī rangarāja stavam 1-13 the ślōkam “*amatam matam matamatāmatam stutam parininditam bhavati ninditam stutam* I *iti rangarājamudajughuśat trayī stumahē vayam nimiti tam na śaknuma*: īī ” (The tamiḻ and samskruta vēdas say “when it is thought that the supreme being cannot be known, it becomes known. And, if it is thought that the supreme being is known, it becomes unknown. When it is thought that the supreme being is praised, it becomes insulted. If it is thought that it cannot be praised, it becomes praised”. How are we, the ignorant people, engaging in praising that supreme being, śrī ranganāta? We are not capable of praising him).

**engaḷ māl** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is very affectionate towards us so that we do not ridicule him.

**sengaṇ māl engaḷ māl** – one who is proclaimed by vēdas as the supreme being because he has eyes described as in cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7 “*tasya yatā puṇḍarīkam ēvamakśiṇī* ” (that supreme being (who is at the centre of the solar system) has two eyes which are like lotus blossomed by sun) and one who cannot be estimated by anyone, developed deep affection for me. The word *māl* which occurs at the beginning denotes one who is a great person and the second *māl* denotes one who has affection.

**nee seeṟal** – do not get angry with us and shower your mercy on us.

**sengaṇmāl seeṟal** – thinking that emperumān’s eyes have become reddish because he is angry on account of his praising him without knowing his greatness, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not to get angry. Alternatively, it can be construed that after hearing āḻvār’s first pāsuram when he set out to praise emperumān, like a famished person taking a large leaf to take the food offered which will satiate his hunger, emperumān was very anxious to hear what āḻvār had to say. When āḻvār stepped back in the second pāsuram since he would end up ridiculing emperumān if he set out to praise him, emperumān might have become angry and hence āḻvār is requesting him not to get angry.

**tīvinaiyōm** – While you are anxious to hear being praised, we are not fortunate to praise you due to our sins. This meaning would gel with the second meaning for the world *seeṟal* ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) was waiting to hear [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) praise when he stated as much in the first pāsuram and became angry when āḻvār stepped back since he would end up ridiculing him). Alternatively, this can be construed as referring to the sins due to which, without knowing emperumān’s greatness, āḻvār set out to start praising emperumān.

**engaḷ māl kaṇḍāy ivai** – setting out to praise you in the first place after seeing your greatness and stepping back from that as it would be inappropriate, are both borne out of our bewilderment. *māl* here refers to bewilderment.

We will take up the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 3. Ivaiyanre nalla

*ivaiyanṟē nalla ivaiyanṟē tīya ivaiyenṟu ivai aṟiyanēlum ivaiyellām ennāl adaippu nīkku oṇṇādu iṟaiyavanē ennāl seyaṟpāladu en*

**Пословный перевод:**

ivaiyanṟē nalla – isn’t not thinking of emperumān good?; ivaiyanṟē tīya – isn’t thinking of emperumān bad?; enṟu – saying so; ivai ivai – these qualities; aṟivanēlum – even though I know; ivaiyellām – all these entities which are bad and good; ennāl – by me; adaippu nīkku – to hold on to or to reject; oṇṇādu – is not possible; iṟaiyavanē – Oh emperumān!; ennāl – by me, this servitor; seyaṟpāladu en – what is there to carry out

**Перевод:**

**ivaiyanṟē nalla – It is good not to praise through mind or speech as said in “pugaḻōm paḻiyōm, madiyōm igaḻōm” (2nd pāsuram)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the first pāsuram, āḻvār started praising emperumān after seeing the greatness of matters related to him. In the second pāsuram, seeing the inestimable nature of these matters, he stepped back from praising saying that he is not qualified to start praising such matters. Seeing the anger that this caused in emperumān, he said that his acts of praising and stepping back from praising are due to his bewilderment. In the first pāsuram, he started to praise emperumān after seeing his greatness and not because of any bewilderment. ṣouldn’t he have stepped back from praising emperumān at that time itself saying “It is not proper on my part, who is a lowly person, to praise such a great entity”? The response for this would be – since emperumān has given me \[reference here is to āḻvār\] the knowledge which the insentient do not possess and since I am capable of analysing virtues and vices, I know that “it is better for us, who are very lowly, not to praise emperumān both in terms of mind and in terms of speech. Praising him would end up as insulting him and is bad”. However, since I \[āḻvār\] have a connection with physical form, and taking or rejecting something is not under my control since I am totally subservient to emperumān, since I cannot sustain without praising matters relating to emperumān, I am incapable of remaining aloof from praising emperumān. Thus, āḻvār resumes praising emperumān in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ivaiyanṟē tīya** – praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) wholeheartedly through mind or speech is bad, as said in the verse “*pugaḻvōm paḻippōm madippōm igaḻvōm*” (2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram)

**ivai enṟu ivai aṟivanēlum** – even though I know that these are good and these are bad, by analysing.

**ivai ellām** – all these, by which I give up praising and accept not praising

**ennāl adaippu nīkku oṇṇādu** – it is not possible for me to accept not praising which I know to be correct and to give up praising which I know to be bad.

**iṟaiyavanē** – Do I have the independence to accept something and to give up something? ṣouldn’t you, who are my lord, do this?

**ennāl seyaṟpāladu en** – Is it possible for me to give up praising you when you have mercifully said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 2-47](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-47/) “*karmaṇyēvādikārastē mā palēśu kadācana*” (you, who are a mukukśu (one who is desirous of attaining mōkśam) have a right over carrying out daily and ceremonial deeds and never over the results) and in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-65](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-65/) “*manmanā bhava madbhaktō madyājī mām namaskuru* I *māmēvaiśyasi satyam tē pratijānēpriyō’si mē* īī” (you be one who keeps his mind on me, one who is my devotee and one who worships me; you offer your salutations to me; you will attain me; this is the truth; I make a vow to you; are you not dear to me!)

We will move on to the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 4. Ennil migu pugalar

*ennil migu pugaḻār yāvarē pinnaiyum maṟṟu eṇṇil migu pugaḻēn yān allāl enna karunjōdik kaṇṇan kadal puraiyum seela perunjōtikku ennenju ātpeṟṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

enna – being mine; karum sōdi kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa with black complexion; kadal puraiyum seelam – having the inherent quality of ocean; perum sōdikku – to you who are having great radiance; en nenju – my mind; āḷ peṟṟu – became a servitor; maṟṟu eṇṇil – if one were to analyse; migu pugaḻēn yān allāl – only I have great fame and none else; pinnaiyum – more than that; ennil – more than I; migu pugaḻār – having more fame; yāvarē – who else is there?

**Перевод:**

**ennil migu pugaḻār yāvarē – this could be construed to mean (1) who else is there who has more fame than I do? (2) Just as bhūtattāḻvār mercifully said in the 100th pāsuram of iraṇḍām tiruvandādi “enṟanaḷavanṟāl yānudaiya anbu” (my affection \[for emperumān\] became much larger than I), my fame has grown bulky, much larger than my basic form which is atomic. Who else  has this much of fame?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān mercifully tells āḻvār “You have not noticed an important aspect when you say ‘If I start praising emperumān, it becomes a fault. If I stay away from praising him, it has a special distinction’. While looking at what you said superficially may appear correct, if one were to analyse deeply at what you said, it will be clear that one should follow the exact opposite of what you had said. If you stay away from praising me, others will think ‘only those who are greater than āḻvār can attain matters related to emperumān’ and will not come anywhere near me. If you praise me, others will think ‘everyone can hold on to the matter that āḻvār has held on to’ and come to me. Hence, I will attain greatness only if you praise me”. Hearing this, āḻvār becomes very pleased and starts praising emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) further affirms the meaning brought out by both these meanings.

**pinnaiyum maṟṟeṇṇil migu pugaḻēn yān allāl** – if one were to analyse, there is none who has fame which is not limited by the basic form. How is it that this āḻvār, who praises himself to this extent in this pāsuram, said in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-7-1-seelamillach-chiriyan/) “*seelamillāc ciṟiyanēlum seyvinaiyō peridāl*” (even though I am lowly person without any knowledge, my sins are huge)? When he does not attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that there is none who has sinned more than he. In the same way, when he attains emperumān and he says that there is none who is his equal, there is no shortcoming in that.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) now says the reason for this.

**enna karunjōdikkaṇṇan kadal puraiyum seelan perunjōdikku ennenju ātpeṟṟu** – praising kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who has black complexion, who gave his divine form to me for me to enjoy completely as my right and thus showed his simplicity, who has the inherent quality of ocean and who is supremely radiant, being the [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), added to his greatness. This verse should be read along with the previous part “*ennil migu pugaḻār yāvarē* ”. By referring to *ennudaiya karunjōdik kaṇṇan,* [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s quality of simplicity by which he gives his divine form for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) to enjoy has been brought out. *seelan* refers to the quality of great person mingling with lowly person without any inhibition. Such a person is referred to in vēdas (sacred texts) as *paranjōdi* (supremely radiant), the sarvēśvaran and āḻvār refers to him here as perunjōdi (great radiance). Alternatively, it can be construed that he is referring to emperumān as *perumjōdi* because of the deep affection that emperumān has for āḻvār as mentioned by āḻvār himself in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-4-isan-vanavarkku/) “*īsan vānavarkkenban enṟal adu tēsamō tiruvēngadattānukku nīsanēn niṟaivonṟumilēn enkaṇ pāsam vaitta paranjudar sōdikke*” (Is it a great quality for tiruvēngadattān  (emperumān) to be the controller of nityasūris? He showed affection towards me, who is lowly and who has no great quality in him and he is supremely radiant).

We will move on to the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 5. Perra tay

*peṟṟa tāy nīyē piṟappitta tandai nī maṟṟaiyār āvārum nī pēsil eṟṟēyō māya mā māyavaḷai māya mulai vāy vaitta nīyammā kāttum neṟi*

**Пословный перевод:**

māya – Oh one who has amazing activities!; ammā – Oh my Lord!; pēsil – if we have to speak (about the beneficial activities that you carry out); peṟṟa tāy nīyē – you are the only one who carries out sweet activities for me, like a mother; piṟappitta tandai nī – you are the only one who carries out beneficial activities like the father who created; maṟṟaiyār āvārum nī – you are the other ācāryas (teachers) who do good by the ātmā (soul); mā māyavaḷai māya – ensuring that the huge demon pūtanā perishes; mulai vāy vaitta – taking (her) bosom in your mouth; nī – you, the lord of all; kāttum neṟi – the path that you showed (to remove my enemy); eṟṟēyō – how wonderful it is!

**Перевод:**

**peṟṟa tāy nīyē – only you are the mother who, even before the child is conceived, observes fast, \[and as the child grows inside the womb\] adjusts her intake of food and sleeping hours according to the needs of the foetus when carrying it for ten months in her womb, takes care of cleaning the child, after it is born and discharges waste fluids, and keeps doing only sweet things for the child always.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tried to move away from emperumān after thinking that he does not have the qualification to approach him. Just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 “asannēva bhavati I asat brahmēti vēdehacēt I asti brahmēti cēd vēda I santamēnam tatō vidu: īī” (when he (a sentient being) realises that brahmam (emperumān) does not exist, he (the sentient entity) does not exist; when he realises that brahmam exists, for that reason alone, (good people) know that he (sentient entity) exists), āḻvār was about to lose his svarūpam (basic nature) when he tried to move away from emperumān. However, emperumān showers his mercy on āḻvār and gives him his svarūpam. In this pāsuram, āḻvār expresses his gratitude to emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**piṟappitta tandai nī** – you are the father who, just as it is mentioned in śāstras (religious treatises) “*mātā bhastrā pitu: putra:*” (mother is like a vessel holding the child for ten months, the son belongs to the father), has rights over the child in many ways, one who creates the child, doing what is beneficial for the child unlike the mother who does only sweet things for the child.

**maṟṟaiyāvārum nī** – just as it is mentioned in āpastamba sūtra 1-1-6 “*śarīramēva māta pitā janataya:* I *sa hi vidyātas tam janayati tac crēśtam janma*  īī” (mother and father create only the physical form for the child; it is ācāryan alone who creates ātmā through knowledge; it is this ātmā which is the superior birth), the parents create only the physical body for the child. They do not carry out any beneficial activity for the ātmā. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that it is emperumān who is his ācāryan, always thinking of doing beneficial acts for the ātmā. The term *marraiyār* refers to great ācāryars (teachers) who are different from mother and father.

**pēsil** – is it not impossible to speak about the benefits that you carry out? If one attempts to speak about these to an extent . . .

**eṟṟeyō** – All that one can say is “How amazing!”

**māya** – Oh one who has amazing power! The opinion is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) can carry out what others cannot. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is speaking about how emperumān accepted him after he decided to leave emperumān.

**māyavaḷai māya mulai vāy vaitta** – keeping his ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s) mouth on the poisoned bosom of the huge demon pūtana to kill her when she came deceptively in order to kill him \[during his incarnation as kriṣṇa\]. By the term *māyavaḷ* āḻvār shows how the demon pūtanā came deceptively, in the guise of kriṣṇa’s mother, in an amazing way. The word *māya* refers to her perishing in the process.

**nīyammā kāttum neṟi eṟṟēyō** – When one looks at the way you removed my hurdle when I decided to move away from you feeling unqualified to be with you, does it not look similar to the way you removed pūtanā! The usage of the word *ammā* emphasises that you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] did this since you possess me.

We will move on to the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 6. Neri katti

*neṟi kātti nīkkudiyō ninpāl karumā muṟi mēni kāttudiyō mēnāḷ aṟiyōmai en seyvān eṇṇināy kaṇṇanē īduraiyāy en seydāl enpadōm yām*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇanē – Oh my svāmy (lord) who incarnated as kriṣṇa!; neṟi kātti – showing (other) means; nin pāl nīkkudiyō – will you remove (me) from you?; (nin pāl) karu mā muṟi mēni – your blackish divine form which is like a tender mango leaf; kāttudiyō – will you show?; mēl nāḷ – since time immemorial; aṟiyōmai – I, who am ignorant; en seyvāṇ eṇṇināy – what do you propose to do?; īdu uraiyāy – tell us the words (mā śuca:); en seydāl – whatever beneficial acts which you do; yām en padōm – (if you leave the act of protecting us, to us) what troubles will we not create?

**Перевод:**

**neṟi kāttu nīkkudiyō – will you banish me from you by showing me other means in which I have to make the efforts (to attain you)? āḻvār says that showing him, who is totally dependent on emperumān, other means by which āḻvār has to make efforts himself, which are contrary to his svarūpam (basic nature), is equivalent to banishing him. When kriṣṇa instructed in śrī bhagavat gītā 18.65 to arjuna “man manā bhava madbhaktō ….” arjuna felt sad that emperumān was showing him means in which he has to make efforts. That he felt sad is reflected in the very next \[carama\] ślōkam 18.66 when kriṣṇa says mā śuca: (do not grieve). Is it not clear that if a person is told “you take care of your needs” it means that it has been said with the intention of giving him up? Did not naḷa tell his wife damayanti “this is the way to vidarbha and this is the way to kōsala” and leave her later when they were sleeping, by cutting the cloth that they were wearing as said in “ēśa pantā vidarbhāṇām ēśa yāti hi kōsalān”? In the same way, is it not to get rid of a person \[āḻvār\] who has attained him \[emperumān\] and who has given him \[emperumān\]  the responsibility of protecting him, that emperumān says “you have to look after what is good for you”?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tried to go away from emperumān thinking that he is not qualified to be with emperumān even after emperumān had created an interest in āḻvār towards him. emperumān had got him into his fold \[as we had seen in the previous pāsuram\] . āḻvār now thinks that emperumān has decided that āḻvār could now take care of himself and says in frustration “If you think in your divine mind about me this way, I will surely perish” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ninpāl karu mā muṟi mēni kāttudiyō** – ōr else, will you show me, through your causeless mercy, your divine form which is blackish and which is like a tender mango leaf? The word *ninpāl* is added to both the previous verse and this verse to get the meaning. Did not upaniśat too mention that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will show his divine form to those who are dear to him, as mentioned in kata upaniśat 1-2-23?  “*nāyamātmā pravacanēna labhyō na mēdayā na bahunā śrutēna* I *yamēvaiśa vruṇutē tēna labhyā: tasyaiśa ātmā vivruṇutē tanūm svām*  īī” {(this paramātmā) cannot be attained through hearing, committing to memory and meditating, without devotion.  Whoever is chosen by the paramātmā only will attain him. This paramātmā reveals his divine form to him}.

**karumā muṟi mēni** – The word *muṟi* refers to a tender leaf. *māmuṟi* – tender mango leaf. Thus, the softness of emperumān’s divine form is spoken of by this word. Since the adjective *karu* (black) has been added, this refers to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form being darker than the tender mango leaf.  The commentator for this prabandam, śrī periyavāccān piḷḷai would say that *karumāmuṟi* would refer to the glossy black pigment from which the superficial layer of dust has been removed. Thus, this refers to the glossy state of emperumān’s divine form.

The next word *mēnāḷ* can be added to the preceding and succeeding words to get the meanings..

**mēnāḷ karumā muṟi mēni kāttudiyō** – Will you show your divine form which has been existing from time immemorial?

**mēnāḷ aṟiyōmai** – I, who have been ignorant from time immemorial.

**aṟiyōmai en seyvāy eṇṇināy** – what are you proposing to do with us who have been caught up in the whirl of ignorance since time immemorial? Is it proper on a person who knows the correct path, to tell another person who has lost his way “You find the way yourself”? ōr is it proper on his part to show the lost man, the correct path? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s situation is similar to the one expressed by śrī āḷavandār in his [stōtra ratnam 49](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-49/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-49/) [ślōkam](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-49/) “*avivēkaghanānta dingmukē bahudā santata du:khavarśiṇi* I *bhagavan bhavadurdinē pada: skalitam mām avalōkayācyuta* īī ” (Oh one who is full of knowledge, power and auspicious qualities! Oh one who does not let go of your followers! Shower your mercy on me who has lost the correct way and is caught in the dark times of samsāram (materialistic realm) which is having darkened directions due to the clouds called as indiscretion and which keeps pouring continuous troubles).

**en seyvāy eṇṇināy** – did you try to push me into the path of devotion as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18-65](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-65/) “*man manā bhava mad bhakta ….*” or did you propose to show yourself as the means, as said in [śrī bhagavat gītā 18.66](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/) “*sarva darmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja* I *aham tvā sarva pāpebhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca:* īī” (leave aside all the righteous paths (as means of attaining mōkśam) with their traces and hold on only to me as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins. Do not grieve)?

**kaṇṇanē īdu uraiyāy** – the responsibility of telling me “Do not worry” (which you said in the just quoted carama ślōkam) and giving me refuge, rests only with you.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says with his divine mind “But I have given you deep knowledge and devotion such that  ignorance will be got rid of”……

**en seydāl en padōm yām** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) responds to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “It is true that you created in me the ability to be free from malevolence towards you; you made me to be favourably disposed towards you;  you also gave me deep knowledge and devotion such that I can get rid of my ignorance. Apart from \[providing me with\] these, even if you carry out more benefits towards me and take me to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam), if you leave it to me to take care of myself, will I not get rid of the first mentioned quality of being without malevolence towards you and return to samsāram to become your enemy?”

We will move on to the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 7. Yame aruvinaiyom

*yāmē aruvinaiyōm sēyōm en nenjinār tāmē aṇukkarāyc cārndoḻindār pūmēya semmādai nin mārbil sērvittup pāridanda ammā nin pādaddarugu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pū mēya sem mādai – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who was born in a lotus flower and who has a reddish complexion; nin mārbil – on your divine chest; sērivittu – making her unite (during the time of churning of milky ocean for nectar); pār idanda – digging out earth (during the time of deluge); ammā – Oh my svāmy!; nin – your, being the lord; pādattu arugu – near the divine feet; en nenjinār – my mind; tāmē – on his own (without my permission); aṇukkarāy – being a confidante (to emperumān); sārndu oḻindār – attained; aruvinaiyōm – having cruel sins; yāmē – we; sēyōm – are at a far distance

**Перевод:**

**yāmē aruvinaiyōm sēyōm – I, having great sins such that I cannot see you and embrace you, am at a very far distance from you. Could emperumān be attained by a samsāri (dweller in this materialistic realm) like me that easily?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the fifth pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about how emperumān took him when he tried to move away from emperumān. In the sixth pāsuram he expressed his desire to see emperumān’s divine form by saying “karumāmuṟi mēni kāttudiyō”. As a means of fulfilling āḻvār’s desire, emperumān showed his divine form to āḻvār’s heart. Not satisfied with this mānasa sākśātkāram (seeing through the mind), āḻvār expresses his sorrow in this pāsuram saying “I am not fortunate to see his divine form and being embraced by him. The difference between what I desired and what I got is similar to that between a mountain and a molehill. I did not get the experience which my heart obtained”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**en nenjinār tāmē aṇuakkarāyc cārndoḻindār** – My mind, without my permission, had reached at close proximity with emperumān, on its own. Due to this, I too, though did not get what I desired, could get the benefit of enjoying \[emperumān\] with my heart. Unlike me who decided to go away from emperumān since I am not qualified to experience him, my heart, which went ahead of me towards emperumān, fell head over heels and enjoyed him, thus carrying out the benefit for me, of seeing emperumān through the mind. Since the heart carried out such benefit for him, āḻvār is referring to his heart as “*nenjinār*” (a respectable term).

Through the rest of the pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) gives the reason for his leaving [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and for his heart falling head over heels on emperumān.

**pū mēya sem mādai nin mārbil sērvittu** – having periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who is residing on a lotus flower, who is of reddish complexion and who has great qualities, to unite with his chest during the time of churning the milky ocean for obtaining nectar. The meaning for this could also be construed as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-1/) “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (not leaving [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) even for a moment), of having pirātti always on the divine chest. The reason for mentioning that emperumān is the consort of pirātti is to justify his leaving emperumān who is such a great entity and he, assuming of himself as a lowly entity. At the same time, his heart thinks “Should I lose out on emperumān when pirātti is always dwelling on his chest” and hence approaches emperumān.

**pāridanda** – this refers to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s taking the incarnation as a great boar during deluge and digging out the earth. The opinion here is that this activity is the reason for his leaving emperumān thinking “Is it appropriate on my part to approach someone who has carried out a super human task?” and, at the same time, for his heart to approach him saying “When he is there to uplift lowly people, should I lose out on him?”

**ammā** – this would refer to his being the supreme entity as well to his being the ordained lord. Here too, the opinion is that this is the reason for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) leaving emperumān since he is the supreme entity and for his heart to approach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as he is the ordained lord.

The lines should be rearranged to read *ammā nin pādattarugu – en nenjinār tāmē aṇukkarāyc cārndoḻindār – yāmē aruvinaiyōm sēyōm*.

We will take up the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 8. Arugum suvadum

*arugum suvadum terivuṇarōm anbē perugum migu idu en pēsīr parugalām paṇbudaiyīr pāraḷandīr pāviyēn kaṇ kāṇbariya nuṇbudaiyīr nummai numakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

parugaḷām paṇbu uadiyīr – Oh emperumān who has qualities which could be drunk (like a liquid)!; pār aḷandīr – Oh one who measured the worlds!; pāviyēn – we, who have lot of cruel sins; kaṇ kāṇbu ariya – not being able to see through eyes; nuṇbu udaiyīr – Oh one who has subtle form of quality !; nummai – you; arugum suvadum – approaching and the means (for approaching); terivu uṇarōm – we do not analyse and do not know; numakku – in your matter; anbu – affection (from us); miga perugum – will grow hugely; idu en – what is the reason for this; pēsīr – please tell (yourself)

**Перевод:**

**arugum suvadum terivuṇarōm  – The opinion here is that “we are not able to come near you. We are not able to enjoy well your sweetness too. We do not have the fortune of being near you for twelve years in ayōdyā, like sītāppirātti and know the means of enjoying your sweetness”. Alternatively, suvadu could be taken to refer to “the means” and hence the meaning would change to – we do not know getting near you and the means for achieving it.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār asks emperumān in this pāsuram, the reason for being close enough as seen through heart while at the same time being far enough that he could not be hugged when he tried to see him with his eyes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**anbē perugum miga idu en pēsīr** – however, affection in your matter kept growing in me. Please tell me the reason for this.

**parugalām paṇbudaiyīr** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/02/thiruvaimozhi-2-3-1-unil-vazh/) “*tēnum pālum neyyum* *kannalum amudumottē* ” (being equivalent to mixing honey, milk,  ghee , candy and nectar) since the resultant mix of liquid substances which are sweet by themselves, is very sweet, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s qualities can be consumed like a sweet juice. āḻvār tells emperumān “When looking at your sweetness, you appeared to be like a fluid which could be consumed by the mouth”. *paṇbu* refers to emperumān’s quality being simple to be tasted.

**pāraḷandīr** – by measuring the earth, you made everyone experience the quality mentioned above. By referring to these two calls (*paṇbudaiyīr* and *pāraḷandīr*) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) states the reason for affection growing in him so that his mind could experience [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**pāviyēm kaṇ kāṇbriya nuṇbudaiyīr** – you have such a subtle form that you cannot be seen with eyes by us, who have accumulated lot of sins. Since only his heart was able to see emperumān’s form, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says *kaṇ kāṇbariya nuṇbudaiyīr* ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is so subtle that he cannot be seen by the eyes). Through this call, āḻvār explains the reason for the first line *arugum suvadum terivuṇarōm*.

**nummai numakku** – once [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) (one of the preceptors in the lineage of our pūrvācāryas) asked piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar as to how the words *nummai* and *numakku* are connected. araiyar responded saying “*nummai arugum suvadum terivuṇarōm*” (we do not now approaching you and the means for carrying out this) and “*numakku anbē perugum miga; idu en pēsīr*” (lot of affection overflows for you; please explain how this happens).

We will take up the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9. Numakkadiyom

*numakkadiyōm enṟenṟu nondu uraittu en mālār tamakkavar tām sārvariyarānāl emakkini yādānum āgidukāṇ nenjē avar tiṟattē yādānum sindittiru*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh mind!; avar tām – he, the supreme entity; sārvu ariyar ānāl – if he is not attainable; “numakku adiyōm – are we not servitors to you, the lord?”; enṟu enṟu – saying this many times; nondu – feeling anguished; mālār tamakku – for such a great entity; uraittu en – what is the purpose in saying?; emakku – for us (who are his possession); ini – from now onvards; yādānum āgidu kāṇ – let whatever happen; avar tiṟattē – towards him; yādānum – something or the other; sindittu iru – keep meditating on

**Перевод:**

**numakkadiyōm enṟenṟu – It is enough to say only once “I am your servitor” to get his mercy, since he is naturally related to us. When this is the case, to say this many times will be a reason for hurting his divine mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since his desire to see emperumān directly was not realised, āḻvār tells his heart “Oh heart! What is the purpose in our showing twelve teeth to emperumān and beseeching him? It does not appear at all that he is going to show himself to us”. After analysing this a little, he feels that he cannot go away from emperumān due to this and tells his heart “Whether he showers his grace or despises us, keep thinking of something or the other about emperumān”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nonduraittu en** – due to the craving to see him directly, isn’t saying this many times, purposeless! Whatever [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had said in  śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-28 “*ārtō vā yadi vā drupta:parēśām śaraṇāgata:*  I *arī: prāṇān parityajya rakśitavya: krutātmanā* īī” (among the enemy warriors, one who has surrendered due to craving (due to feeling apologetic) or one who has surrendered due to patience (half-heartedly) should be protected by a person with well disciplined mind, even if he has to give up his life) has become false!

**mālār tamakku nondu uraittu en** – *mālār* – one who is great; the opinion here is that when we were together with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) he said that he is our servitor, he said a few words in anguish and showed his happiness, but now there is no point in saying these things to him, who is such a great person.

**avar tām sārvu ariyar ānāl** – when he was with us, he was obedient to us and showed that he was dependent on us, but now he is beyond approach for us. My desire to see him in person would have fructified on the day he had thought “we will do” \[show himself to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)\]; however, when he is not thinking of us now, there is no purpose in saying repeatedly in anguish, that “we are your servitors”.

This is the meaning of the first half of the pāsuram. In the second half of the pāsuram, āḻvār mercifully describes how he tells his heart “if this is the case, we can leave him”.

**emakku ini yādānum āgidukāṇ nenjē** – for me who had desired to embrace him with this physical form, once it is clear that it will not happen, there is no difference between attaining him and losing him. What is the point in getting food later when it was not available when one was hungry? This is similar to [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) saying in nācciyār tirumoḻi 13-9 “*kommai mulaigaḷ idar tīra gōvindaṟku ōr kuṟṟēval immaippiṟavi  seyyādē inippōycceyyum tavandānen*” (What is the purpose in carrying out service to gōvinda ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) later when I cannot carry it out for him in this birth?)

When asked, “Let him come when he wants to. Until then, can you not wait patiently?” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that it is possible for those who can sustain themselves in separation but not for him who cannot sustain himself in separation. Did not sītāppirātti tell that she cannot live if separated from emperumān, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 27-8 “*īrśyā rōśau bahiśkrutya bhuktaśēśam iva udakam* I *naya mām vīra visrabhda: pāpam mayi na vidyatē* īī ”  (Oh brave man! Getting rid of jealousy and anger, just as one gets rid of excess water after drinking what is required, take me, believing (my words); I do not have the sin (which is the reason for my  separation from you)). This ślōkam is explained in detail now, in the vyākhyānam. This ślōkam has been recited by sītāppirātti in anger which came out of affection, after śrī rāma had told her “There are wild animals and demons in the forest; I cannot take a gentle person such as you to the forest”.

*īrśyā rōśau bahiśkrutya* – (Leaving aside jealousy and anger) Do you need so much of jealousy and anger since I will get a name with people saying ‘when śrī rāma went to forest, sītā also followed him’. Just as you would get a name since ‘rāma took the words of his father \[emperor daśarata\] and went to the forest’ should I not get a name ‘sītā went to the forest following her husband’? Will the greatness ‘he followed the instructions of elders’ be appropriate only for you? Will it not be suitable for me?

*īrśyārōśau bahiśkrutya bhuktaśēśam ivōdakam* – the opinion here is ‘you are a disciple of vaśiśta. You are very knowledgeable. That being the case, just as excess water, after quenching thirst is got rid of, should you not get rid of your jealousy and anger?’

*naya mām* – (take me with you) Once you get rid of jealousy and anger, you would strive for the benefits of others. Hence, please take me too along with you.

*vīra* – You may hesitate to take me if there is any shortcoming in your valour, having taken the responsibility for others’ vellbeing. Since you are great warrior, there is no possibility for this doubt.

*visrabda* – (having faith in my words) You know yourself that you have the capability to protect me in the forest too. My words ‘take me along with you’ were not said for the sake of saying. These were said whole heartedly. Hence, keeping faith in my words, please take me.

sītāppirātti says further, thinking that perumāḷ (śrī rāma) may be of the opinion that ‘it would be better to live in the city \[of ayōdyā\] without any hardship than living in forest, facing many the hardships’

*pāpam mayi na vidyatē*  – Since I will not live if separated from you, there is no possibility that I will suffer in separation from you. The opinion is that due to this fact, there will be no sin for her, as a result of that.

Now, we get back to the main pāsuram.

**avar tiṟattē yādānum sindittu iru** – \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s heart is asking him\] ”Could you think that both attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and losing him are the same? He is very much required for our existence” āḻvār responds “It is not required to attain him for our existence. Since we are a part of his physical form, when he exists, we also will exist. Now, in order to spend time purposefully, keep thinking of something or the other about him”. The opinion here is that even if emperumān says “you are not needed for me”, āḻvār could sustain himself by thinking about this.

We will move on to the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 10. Iru nalvar

*iru nālvar īraindin mēl oruvar ettōdu oru nālvar ōriruvar allāl tirumāṟku yāmār vaṇakkamār ēpāvam nannenjē nāmā miga udaiyōm nāḻ*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh mind which has goodness; iru nālvar – the eight vasus; īraindin mēl oruvar – the eleven rudras; ettōdu oru nālvar – the twelve ādityas; ōriruvar – the two distinguished aśvinī dēvatās; allāl – apart from these entities; tirumāṟku – to attain the consort of mahālakśmi; nām Ar – in what classification do we belong?; vaṇakkam Ar – to which type will the attainment that we \[people with lowly power\] make, belong?; E pāvam – how are (our) sins?; nām – we; miga nāḻ udaiyōm – have lot of unnecessary desires; A – a sound made in wonder \[as in nāmā\]

**Перевод:**

**iru nālvar īraindin mēl oruvar ettōdu oru nālvar ōriruvar – the thirty three dēvas comprising 8 vasus, 11 rudras, 12 ādityas and 2 aśvinī dēvas. It is pertinent to recall here poygai āḻvār who says in his mudal tiruvandādi 52 “eṇmar padinoruvar īraṟuvar ōriruvar” and āṇḍāḷ who says in tiruppāvai 20 “muppattu mūvar”. vēda also says “trayas trimśadvai dēvā: ” (33 dēvas)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Three avatārikais were mentioned for this pāsuram \[by nanjīyar\]. The first avatārikai is that āḻvār is moving away from emperumān citing his unsuitability since he is trying to attain a means \[emperumān\] who is attainable only by the thirty three dēvas who are great in terms of knowledge, power etc. The second avatārikai relates to āḻvār saying disgustedly to his heart “emperumān will be attained only by dēvas who are interested in other benefits and not by us who are totally desirous of only him”. The third avatārikai is about āḻvār telling his heart in a dejected manner “emperumān will be attained only by dēvas who hold on to other means but not by us who hold on to only him as the means”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**allāl** – apart from these \[33 entities\] who are full of knowledge and power.

**tirumāṟku nām Ar vaṇakkam Ar** – do we have the qualification to attain the consort of [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/)? Couldn’t only great people attain him? Is our salutation etc, of any significance for him?

**E pāvam** – The reason for our losing him when he is at such close proximity is our sin only. One interpretation here is that we had to lose out on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) since we went away from emperumān considering our unsuitability. The second interpretation is that the reason for losing emperumān and not enjoying him despite our heart seeing him is due to our sin \[here, the term ‘our’ refers to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)\].

**nannenjē** – On my good heart who remains with me irrespective of whether I attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) or lose out on him.

**nām miga nāḻ udaiyōm** – As mentioned in the decad “*vaḷavēḻ ulagum*” ([tiruvāimoḻi 1-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-5-valavezh/)) , if we consider the unsuitability interpretation, it would mean “do we, the epitome of faults, have the qualification to approach the consort of śrī mahālakśmi? īsn’t moving away from him the proper course of action? īsn’t it due to our ignorance that we approached him despite our lowliness?” If we consider the second interpretation (losing him out due to our sin), it would mean “[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who carries out activities for those who have other means \[to attain him\] and who seek other benefits, would consider us, who have no other means and who seek no other benefit, as having sins”. The long syllable *A* in *nāmā* is indicative of the element of surprise.

We will move on to the 11<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 11. Nalal amar muyanra

*nāḻāl amarmuyanṟa vallarakkan innuyirai vāḻā vagai validal nin valiyē āḻāda pārunī vānunī kālunī tīyunī nīrunī āy ninṟa nī*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻāda pārum nī – you are the earth which does not submerge in water and which floats; vānum nī – you are the ethereal layer too; kālum nī – you are wind too; tīyum nī – you are fire too; nīrum nī – you are water too; āy ninṟa nī – you are the antarātmā (indvelling soul) for all these; nāḻāl – due to his pride; amar muyanṟa – one who fought with you; val arakkan – the powerful demon rāvaṇa; in uyirai – (his) dear life; vāḻā vagai validal – hurting (that rāvaṇa) such that he could not live; nin valiyē – is it any great skill (for you)?

**Перевод:**

**nāḻāl amar muyanṟa vallarakkan innuyirai vāḻā vagai validal nin valiyē – due to his arrogance that he is the king of all demons, rāvaṇa, ignorant of the fact that he should not stand in fight with śrī rāma, waged a war with him. śrī rāma destroyed him in such a way that he lost his dear life. āḻvār is asking whether this is such a great ability for emperumān. The opinion here is that for emperumān who was able to win over āḻvār who was about to leave his presence considering himself to be unsuitable to be with emperumān, the act of destruction of rāvaṇa does not appear to be a patch on the former act. Thus it is clear that those who move away from emperumān considering themselves to be ‘unsuitable’ will be on a par with rāvaṇa who separated sītāppirātti from emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Looking at the greatness of emperumān, when āḻvār tried to move away from him thinking that he is unsuitable to be with emperumān, emperumān removed that thought from āḻvār and made him to praise him again. After seeing this ability, āḻvār wonders “For you who won over me and made me unite with you, is winning over rāvaṇa a great deed?” In the interpretation where āḻvār said a few words in frustration after being unable to see emperumān directly, we can consider that emperumān showed his form of ruling all the universe and his activity of destroying the demon, thus bringing clarity in āḻvār and removing his frustration. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āḻāda pārum nī vānum nī kālum nī tīyum nī nīrum nī āy ninṟa nī** – Contrasting elements such as soil \[earth\] which will dissolve in water, water which will put out fire etc are all [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s wealth. You are the indvelling soul for all these five elements which are your wealth. You unite all these elements and rule over them. For a person with such great ability (to put together contrasting entities) is making me appropriate for you such a great skilful act? For you who are the *antaryāmi* for these elements, who are their controller and who are referred to by any word which refers to any of these five elements, is making us, who are under your control and who cannot sustain ourselves if we do not attain you, suitable for you, such a great deed? We can also consider this to mean that just as killing rāvaṇa is not a great deed, making us appropriate for you is also not a great deed.

We will move on to the 12<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 12. Niyanre altuyaril

*nīyanṟē āḻtuyaril vīḻvippān ninṟuḻanṟāy pōyonṟu solliyen pō nenjē nīyenṟum kāḻttubadēsam tarinum kaikkoḷḷāy kaṇṇan tāḷ vāḻttuvadē kaṇḍāy vaḻakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh \[my\] mind!; āḻ tuyaril – in deep sorrow; vīḻvippān – to push (me); ninṟu uḻanṟāy – one who made perennial efforts; nī anṟē – was it not you?; pōy – repeatedly; onṟu solli – by saying one word; en – what is the purpose?; pō – leave this; upadēsam tarinum – even if I instruct you (about emperumān’s simplicity); nī – you; kāḻttu – getting angry (with me); enṟum kaikkoḷḷāy – you do not recognise (that instruction); kaṇṇan tāḷ – the divine feet of kriṣṇa; vāḻttuvadē – to keep praising; vaḻakku kaṇḍāy – is appropriate for us, please see

**Перевод:**

**nīyanṟē āḻ tuyaril vīḻvippān ninṟu uḻanṟāy – the interpretation for āḻ tuyar (deep sorrow) could be taken as either \[āḻvār\] leaving the presence of emperumān considering himself as being “unsuitable” or losing out on emperumān on account of engaging with worldly matters for a very long time. āḻvār is making his heart as the reason for not stopping him when he was engaging with worldly matters or when he was leaving emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After approaching emperumān, āḻvār grieves for the time that he had lost \[in not being with emperumān earlier\] and loathes his heart which did not stop him \[when he moved away from emperumān\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

His heart, finding that all along [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) was praising it with words such as *munduṟṟa nenjē* (as in the 1st pāsuram), *yāmē sēyōm en nenjinār sārndoḻindār* (pāsuram 7) and *nal nenjē* (pāsuram 10), asks him as to why he was finding fault with it now for his wrong-doing. āḻvār says . . .

**pōy onṟu solli en pō nenjē** – I am telling you about your not stopping me when I was deep in sorrow. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart “What is the purpose in our blaming each other? Let us leave this argument at this”. The word pō gives the meaning of “Leave it aside”.

āḻvār tells his heart further that not listening to his instruction for a long time is also a reason for his present predicament.

**upadēsam tarinum nī enṟum kāḻttuk kaikkoḷḷāy** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says “Did you not remain aloof and become angry with me when I instructed you, for a long time, not to think that sarvēśvaran (supreme entity) has only greatness and that he has the quality of simplicity too? ”

His heart tells him “Now tell me as to what is to be done”. āḻvār says . . .

**kaṇṇan tāḷ vāḻttuvadē vaḻakku kaṇḍāy** – We should not stand aloof when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is ready to accept us. It is appropriate for you to keep praising the divine feet of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who has qualities such as simplicity, affability etc.

We will go on to the 13th pāsuram next.

## 13. Valakkodu maru

*vaḻakkodu māṟukolanṟu adiyār vēṇḍa iḻakkavum kāṇḍum iṟaiva iḻappuṇḍē emmātkoṇḍāgilum yān vēṇḍa en kaṇgaḷ tammāṟkāttu un mēniccāy*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṟaiva – Oh the supreme being!; odu – clinging (to samsāris); vaḻakku anṟu – is not appropriate (for you); māṟu koḷ vaḻakku anṟu – expecting something in return (from them) is also not appropriate; adiyār vēṇḍa – when followers beseeced; iḻakkavum kāṇḍum – we have seen (masters) losing (themselves and their possessions) while carrying out tasks (for their followers); iḻappu uṇḍē – could your followers lose?; āgilum – even if you have to obtain something from us; yān vēṇḍa – as I pray; em āḷ koṇḍu – making me as a servitor; en kaṇgaḷ tammāl – for these biological eyes of mine; un mēni cāy – the beauty of your divine form; kāttu – please manifest

**Перевод:**

**vaḻakkodu māṟukoḷanṟu – this should be split as odu vaḻakkanṟu and māṟukoḷ vaḻakkanṟu to get the correct meanings. odu refers to connection with samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm). odu vaḻakkanṟu – āḻvār tells emperumān that it is not appropriate for emperumān to approach a samsāri and stand in front of him. māṟukoḷ vaḻakkanṟu – unlike people (samsāris) who expect a return of compliment when one helps another, it is not appropriate for emperumān to expect any return of favour when he gives a benefit to a samsāri. māṟu – return of help (as an exchange).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 11th pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about how emperumān allowed him to unite with himself when āḻvār left him thinking that he is unsuitable for emperumān. In this pāsuram he requests emperumān “If you do not wish to allow me to leave, you must allow me to get near you, see you with my eyes and experience you”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

emperumān asks āḻvār to explain what he intends saying . . . .

**adiyār vēṇḍa iḻakkavum kāṇḍum** – in the world we see a leader with noble qualities losing himself and his possessions in order to fulfil the wishes of his followers when they approach him.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) counters saying that he will have such a compulsion only if [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is his (emperumān’s) follower.

**iṟaiva** – it does not matter whether I am a follower or not. The fact that you are the lord cannot be obliterated. When you are the lord, will it not follow as a natural corollary that I am the follower?

**iḻappuṇḍē** – While it may happen in the world that followers (of others) may not have their wishes fulfilled, is there any such loss for your followers?

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) “If you have to obtain whatever you desire without any loss, should you not have some connection with servitorśip?” āḻvār responds  . . .

**emmātkoṇḍāgilum yān vēnda** – this could be rearranged to get *āgilum yām vēṇḍa em ātkoṇḍu* – If I say “I too want such and such” you should consider my request \[my prayer to you\] itself as a *kainkaryam* (service) and fulfil my desire.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) further asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) “What is the desire that you wish to have fulfilled ”, āḻvār says …

**en kaṇgaḷ tammār kāttu un mēniccāy** – you must manifest the beauty of your divine form such that I could see that with these eyes of mine. It is not enough if you say “I will show you in *paramapadam*”. What is the purpose in showing it in some other place with some other form of mine when I want to see you here, with these eyes? Will a person’s hunger be sated if another person eats food? Did not sītāppirātti too desire to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the same physical form and in the same place, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 40-3 “*yatā tam puruśavyāgram gātrai: śōkābhikarśitai:* I *samspruśēyamsakāmāham tatā kuru dayām mayi* īī ” (you should shower your mercy on me such that I, having lot of affection, would be able to touch the greatest man \[śrī rāma\] with these limbs which have become wizened due to grief)? Just as āṇḍāḷ had desired to carry out service in this birth itself, as mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 13-9 “*gōvindaṟkōr kuṟṟēval immaippiṟavi seyyādē inippōycceyyum tavandānen?*” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too desires to see emperumān with these eyes.

We will take up the 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14. Sayal kariyanai

*sāyāl kariyānai uḷḷaṟiyārāy nenjē pēyār mulai koduttār pēyarāy nī yār pōyt tēmbūṇ suvaittu ūṇaṟindaṟindum tīvinaiyām pāmbār vāy kainīttal pārtti*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh my mind!; sāyāl kariyānai – kriṣṇa, who is dark in complexion; uḷ aṟiyārāy – not knowing how to enjoy, after entering; pēyār – pūtanā, the fiend; pēyarāy mulai koduttār – being a fiend, she offered her (poisoned) bosom; pōy – going repeatedly; tēmbu ūṇ suvaittu – enjoying śabda (sound) etc which keep eroding the ātmā (soul); ūṇ aṟindum aṟindum – even after knowing the result; tīvinaiyām pāmbār vāy – inside the mouth of the snake samsāram (materialistic realm) which nurtures only evil qualities; kai nīttal pārtti – you are trying to put out your hand; nī yār – how lowly are you (in comparison with pūtanā)?

**Перевод:**

**sāyāl kariyānai – sāyal refers to complexion. Thus, one who is black in complexion.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had requested emperumān to show him his divine physical form. Once he thinks of the divine physical form of emperumān, he starts moving away from emperumān again, saying “Seeing such a distinguished divine form with these eyes is an inappropriate activity”. He says that his approaching emperumān is much similar to the way pūtanā (the demon who tried to kill infant kriṣṇa) approached emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uḷ aṟiyārāy** – not knowing how to enjoy, after entering; not knowing that the supreme being has come down as an infant, crying for mother’s milk and, not knowing as to how to engage with that simplicity. We can also construe this as meaning that she (pūtanā) did not know that it is actually sarvēśvaran who has come down.

**nenjē pēyār mulai koduttār pēyarāy** – Oh my mind! pūtanā did not engage with deceit after taking birth as a human being. Did she not engage with that deceitful act because she was a fiend?

**nī yār** – when compared to her, what a lowly entity are you \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his mind\]! When looking at you, who should have been engaging with śāstras (sacred texts), hailing from the human birth but instead are harming [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), pūtanā appears to be fit for being considered as a nityasūris!

**tēmbūṇ suvaittu ūṇaṟindu aṟindum tīvinaiyām pāmbār vāy kai nīttal pārtti** – you are engaging  with worldly pursuits of śabda, sparśa, rūpa, rasa, ganda (the five senses of sound, touch, form, taste and smell) even after knowing very well that these pursuits will keep eroding the nature of ātmā. This is similar to a person putting out his hand inside the mouth of a (poisonous) snake. How are you related to pūtanā? In this interpretation, the word *nī yār* wonders whether one is related to pūtanā. The word *pōy* could be added to *suvaittu* or to *kai nīttal pārtti*. The opinion here is that despite knowing that the result of engaging repeatedly with worldly pursuits is only ruinous for ātmā, you \[reference here is to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s mind\] are repeatedly putting out your hand inside the mouth of the poisonous snake, which is what samsāram is. The word *tēmbūṇ* refers to worldly pursuits which cause ruin to ātmā’s nature. Alternatively, *tēmbūṇ* could refer to bringing a bad name to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) by the heart by its being unsuitable to approach emperumān and the heart repeatedly carrying out this. Thus this will mean that the heart, knowing well that its approaching emperumān will bring bad name to emperumān, is repeatedly doing this, just like one puts out his hand into the mouth of a poisonous snake. In both interpretations, it is clear that you are much lowlier than pūtanā. The end letter *Ar* in *pēyār, pāmbār* would indicate the nature of cruelty brought about by these entities.

We will move on to the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 15. Parttor edirida

*pārttōr ediridā nenjē padu tuyaram perttōdap pīdaḻivām pēccillai ārttōdam tam mēni(t)tāḷ tadavat tāngidandu tammudaiya semmēnik kaṇ vaḷarvār sīr*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh mind (which is leaving emperumān due to being unsuitable); ōdam – ocean; ārttu – agitating; tam mēni tāḷ tadava – rubbing his divine physical form and divine feet; tām kidandu – lying down; tammudaiya semmēni kaṇ vaḷarvār – one who is closing his reddish eyes and carrying out yōganiddirai (a meditative posture where the body is relaxed and mind is fully alert); sīr – his auspicious qualities; padu tuyaram pērttu – not thinking of our suffering; ōda – if we meditate; pīdu aḻivām pēccillai – that emperumān’s greatness will be destroyed will not happen; ediridā – at the very beginning; pārttu ōr – knowing (this), analyse

**Перевод:**

**nenjē ediridā pārttu ōr – Oh heart! ānalyse this at the very beginning, seeing it directly.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Thinking of the lowliness of his heart, āḻvār moved away from emperumān in the previous pāsuram. In this pāsuram, he tells his heart “If we think of your lowly activities, it is proper that we move away from emperumān. But if we think of emperumān’s activities, we can approach him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

But, what is to be analysed?

**ārttu ōdam tam mēni tāḷ tadava tām kidandu tammudaiya semmēnik kaṇ vaḷarvār sīr padu tuyaram pērttōda pīdaḻivām pēccillai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is lying in the roaring ocean which is gently rubbing against his divine form and divine feet. He is in *yōganidrā* posture (wherein the body if fully relaxed and the mind is fully alert). If we forget our sorrows and think of his auspicious qualities, there is no possibility that such thinking will lead to destruction of his greatness. The word *semmēnikkaṇ* would refer to reddish coloured eyes. For the words *tam mēnit tāḷ,* [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/), the commentator for this prabandam, considers the meaning as ‘his reddish divine feet’.

We will take up the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 16. Siral pirandu

*sīrāl piṟandu siṟappāl vaḷarādu pēr vāmanāgākkāl pērāḷā mārbārap pulgi nī uṇḍumiḻnda bhūmi nīr ēṟparidē sollu nī yāmaṟiyac cūḻndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pērāḷā – Oh supreme being!; sīrāl piṟandu – being born into a wealthy family; siṟappāl vaḷarādu – not growing up in a grand manner; pēr vāman āgākkāl – even if you had not borne the name of vāman; nī – you; mārbu Ara pulgi – (during your incarnation as varāha, the great boar) embracing with your chest; uṇḍu – swallowing (during the time of deluge); umiḻnda – spitting it out (during the time of creation); bhūmi – this world; nīr ēṟpu aridē – is not possible to take it in alms?; nī – you; sūḻndu – analyse; yām aṟiya – such that we know; sollu – please tell

**Перевод:**

**sīrāl piṟandu – Couldn’t you have been born similar to the divine son of cakravarty (emperor) daśarata who observed rituals such as putra kāmēśti yāga using his wealth and his penance, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 66-3 “mahatā tapasā rāma mahatā cāpi  karmaṇā I rājyā daśaratēnāsi labdō’mrutamivāmarai:  īī” (Oh śrī rāma! Just as dēvas obtained nectar, you were obtained by emperor daśarata through great penance and great deeds). Couldn’t you have been like kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who was obtained by vasudēva after observing ananta vrata  (ananta here could mean the name of a specific ritual or innumerable rituals)?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār experienced the reclining posture of emperumān in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean), wherein emperumān was waiting expectantly for someone (his devotees) to approach and attain him. In this pāsuram, he says “Is it only expectation that he has? Did he not incarnate as a mendicant too, in order to retrieve his possession?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**siṟappāl vaḷarādu** – Couldn’t you have been brought up by the great jñāni (one with lot of knowledge) śrī sumantra or vaśiśta who nurtured you thoroughly? Couldn’t you have grown up like kaṇṇa who ate up all the butter saved by his favourite devotees and who killed the unfavourable entities such as pūtanā?

**pēr vāman āgākkāl** – should you have assumed the most famous name of vāmana through which you made all the dwarfs, living then, to be referred to as trivikrama? Couldn’t you have assumed a great name such as *śriya:pati* (consort of śrī mahālakśmi)? It was inside the hut of atiti, the consort of sage kaśyapa that you were born; you grew up without having anyone to nurture you with care; your name was vāmana (dwarf).

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says further whether he couldn’t have obtained this earth from māvali (mahābali) if he had not lowered himself to such an extent.

**pērāḷā** – Oh one who has great affection! Just as this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said “*ettiṟam*” (in tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1) even before saying about his activity, he engages with the simplicity of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) by saying *pērāḷā*. He says that he cannot see the limit to emperumān’s partiality towards his followers.

**mārbārappulgi nī uṇḍumiḻnda bhūmi nīr ēṟparidē** – if you didn’t manifest such simplicity, is it impossible to obtain from mahābali this earth, which underwent as many transformations as cotton undergoes (twelve transformations, from flower stage to cloth stage) by way of being embraced by you tightly when you dug it out during your incarnation as varāha, being swallowed by you during deluge, being spat out during next cycle of creation etc?

**sollu nī yāmaṟīyac cūḻndu** – you must analyse the reason for incarnating with such simplicity and explain to us, so that we will know. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) cannot estimate such simplicity, he is asking emperumān himself to explain this. When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) speaks about his incarnations as in [śrī bhagavat gītā 4-9](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/4-9/) “*janma karma ca mē divyam ēvam yō vētti tatvata:* I *tyaktvā dēham punarjanma naiti māmēti sō’rjuna* īī” (Oh arjuna! One who knows truthfully my *aprākruta* (not related to primordial matter) birth and my activities, will not take birth again after relinquishing his body; he attains me), he only called his incarnation as divyam (divine) but did not elaborate on it. vēdam (sacred texts) too , when speaking about the mystery of his incarnation as in puruśa sūktam, mentioned that only intelligent people will know about it “*ajāyamānō bahudā vijāyatē tasya dīrā: parijānanti yōnim*” (even though he (emperumān)  is without any birth, he incarnates many times; only the knowledgeable ones know the mysteries of his incarnations) \[Hence āḻvār is requesting emperumān to state the reasons himself\].

We will go on to the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.
