## 17. Sulndadiyar

*āḻvār says tat vhen emperumān’s follovers are vorśipping him, and even if he does not manifest himself to tem, tey vill not despise him. ṭhey vill remain quiet, tinking “ḷet him do vhat his divine mind decides”*

**Пословный перевод:**

engum sūḻndu – surrounding on all sides; vāḷ varaigaḷ pōl – similar to shiny mountains; arakkan van talaigaḷ tām idiya – such that the strong heads of demon rāvaṇa are rent asunder; tāḷ varai vil – bow, like a mountain, reaching to his foot; ēndinār tām – emperumān who bore (the bow); sūḻndu – taking many incarnations; adiyār vēṇḍinakkāl – when searching for those who are willing to be his servitors; tōnṟādu vittālum – even if he does not get any follower; vāḻndiduvar – he will remain joyous; pinnum – even after (reaching paramapadam); tam vāy tiṟavār – he will not discuss this (with pirātti)

**Перевод:**

**sūḻndadiyār vēṇḍinakkāl tōnṟādu vittālum vāḻndiduvar – Just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-6 “edir sūḻal pukku”, emperumān will remain joyous even if he does not get desired welcome from samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm) when he takes incarnations repeatedly, expecting to get followers who will welcome him, thinking “We have taken all efforts for their sake”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that in order to cherish his followers, when emperumān gets close to them and even if they do not welcome him, he does  not take it hard on himself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings: sūḻndadiyār vēṇḍinakkāl tōnṟādu vittālum vāḻndiduvar pinnum tam vāy tiṟavār sūḻndengum vāḷvaraigaḷ pōlarakkan vantalaigaḷ tām idiyat tāḷvarai villēndinār tām*

**pinnum tam vāy tiṟavār** – when he is together with [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) (śrī mahālakśmi), he will not tell even her that these people did not welcome him. When children commit mistakes, it is normal for the father to discuss these with the mother and get comforted. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) \[the father\] will not tell even her \[the mother\]. The 364<sup>th</sup> *sūtram* (aphorism) in śrīvacana bhūśaṇam chapter 3 “*aṟivikka uriyavan agappada vāy tiṟavādē sarvajña viśayangaḷukkum māraikkum ennā ninṟadirē*” (when the apt person is available, not talking to him about this and hiding everything from him are considered as normal) and the explanation given by śrī maṇavāḷa māmunigaḷ for that *sūtram* are an explanation for this pāsuram.

**sūḻndengum vāḷvaraigaḷ pōlarakkan van talaigaḷ tām idiyat tāḷ varai villēndinār tām** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) held his bow (in his incarnation as śrī rāma) which was like a mountain reaching down to his foot and surrounded the demon rāvaṇa with his arrows which made people think that it was only śrī rāma’s arrows wherever they saw, and severed the heads of rāvaṇa which were like shiny mountains. Even though emperumān has such power (with his omnipotence), he appeared powerless when his followers are indifferent to him. He will not tell even [pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) that they are indifferent to him.

Let us now consider the pāsuram from the point of view of the followers of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and go through the 2<sup>nd</sup> avatārikai and vyākhyānam.

**adiyār sūḻndu vēṇḍinakkāl tōnṟādu vittālum vāḻndiduvar** – When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s followers pray to emperumān, with lot of sorrow, to manifest himself to them, and even if emperumān does not show himself before them, they will remain quiet, saying “ṣouldn’t we, his servitors, be patient with him, the Lord, and look at whatever he does?”

**pinnum tam vāy tiṟavār** – they will not discuss this with those who keep talking about the shortcomings in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s auspicious qualities.

**sūḻndengum vāḷvaraigaḷ . . . . tāḷ varai villēndinār tām** – they will not detest if śrī rāma, who armed himself with a bow to protect good people, did not manifest himself to them.

We will move on to the 18<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 18. Tambal appundalum

*tāmbāl āppuṇḍālum attaḻumbudān iḷaga pāmbāl āppuṇḍu pāduṟṟālum sōmbādu ip palluruvai ellām padarvitta vittā nin tolluruvai yār aṟivār sollu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāmbāl – with (yaśōdāppirātti’s) rope; āppuṇḍālum – even if tied down; attaḻumbudān iḷaga – to make even that scar to appear small; pāmbāl āppuṇḍu pāduṟṟālum – even if he were to suffer due to being tied down by the snake (kāḷiyan); sōmbādu – not backing down (due to these troubles); ippal uruvum – these cētanas (sentient entities) who have variegated forms such as dēva (celestial), manuśya (human) et al; padarvitta – creating them and spreading them out to many places; vittā – Oh, the causative factor for the world!; un tol uruvai – your form which is very old; yār aṟivār – who can know?; sollu – please say it yourself, …

**Перевод:**

**tāmbāl āppuṇḍālum – even if yaśōdāppirātti ties him with a rope saying “īsn’t he my son! To punish him when he does any mischief is only beneficial”. The scar created by her when she tied him with a rope is much sought after, similar to the mark which forms when the nāyaka (leader) unites with his beloved nāyaki (leading lady). Hence āḻvār is taking this as an example along with the next (kāḷia bandana or tying down by kāḷiya) :**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that emperumān will not feel disgusted if samsāris are indifferent to him. In this pāsuram, he says that he will not step back from protecting them even if they insult him either due to love or due to enmity. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**attaḻumbudān iḷaga pāmbāl āppuṇḍu pāduṟṟālum** – when he had to suffer so much at the hands of kāḷiya (a poisonous snake) when being tied down, that it will make the former scar appear insignificant.

**sōmbādu ippal uruvai ellām padarvitta vittā** – not stepping back thinking  “the samsāris have insulted me”, creating jīvātmās, who are worse than kāḷiya in their hatred towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), with the thought “is carrying out an apt deed,  in any way bad for my dignity?”, emperumān is the causative factor for all the worlds, creating jīvātmās from out of his divine form.

**un tol uruvai yār aṟivār sollu** – could a few people know about your divine form which has been there for a very long time? Please say this yourself. The opinion here is that his svarūpam (basic nature) which remains the same irrespective of whether someone insults him or not, cannot be known by others. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) requests [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to clarify this himself.

**tolluru** – here, the meaning for saying old svarūpam is the concern which the possessor \[emperumān\] has towards the possession \[all his creations\].

We will take up the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 19. Sollil kuraiyillai

*sollil kuṟaiyillai sūdaṟiyā nenjamē ellippagal ennādu eppōdum tollaikkaṇ māttānaikkellām ōr aivaraiyē māṟāgak kāttānaik kāṇḍu nī kāṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

sūdu aṟiyā nenjamē – Oh mind which does not know the distinction between good and bad; sollil – if one attempts to speak about emperumān’s \[auspicious\] qualities; kuṟaiyillai – there is no limit to such an activity; tollai kaṇ – on the earth, which is there for a very long time; māttānaikku ellām – for the huge armies (of duyōdana et al); ōr aivaraiyē – the five pāṇḍavas who had none to support; māṟāga – making them as enemies; elli pagal ennādu eppōdum – at all times, without thinking whether it is day or night; kāttānai – one who protected; kāṇḍum – we will see; nī kāṇ – you please see

**Перевод:**

**sollil kuṟaiyillai – if one tries to speak about matters related to emperumān, it cannot be done. The opinion is that however much one speaks, it is not possible to estimate emperumān \[his auspicious qualities\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that in his younger days, whether he was tied down by a rope or by a snake, he would protect his devotees. In this pāsuram, it mentions about the way he protected the pāṇḍavas after emperumān had grown up. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sūdaṟiyā nenjamē** – Oh heart which does not know the means! Not knowing that engaging with matters related to emperumān is good and that these should be held on to or that engaging with other matters is bad and that they should be desisted from.

**tollaikkaṇ māttānaikkellām ōr aivaraiyē mārāgak kāttānai** – Against huge armies which made the earth to suffer, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (as kriṣṇa) made the five pāṇḍavas as the opponents and protected them. If emperumān had himself destroyed duryōdana et al, the pāṇḍavas would not have recognised that as their victory. Hence, he put them up in the front and made them victorious, protecting them all the time.

**ellip pagal ennādu eppōdum kāttānai** – though the pāṇḍavas waged in battle during day hours and slept during the night hours, emperumān, without distinction between day and night, kept himself awake fully, massaging arjuna’s shoulders, devising the means which will enable pāṇḍavas to become victorious, putting them into practice and protecting them.

**kāṇḍum nī kāṇ** – if you desire to see ([āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart), you can see truthfully.

We will go on to the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 20. Kanappugil arivu

*kāṇappugil aṟivu kaikkoṇḍa nenjam nāṇappadum anṟē nām pēsil māṇi uruvāgikkoṇḍu ulagam nīrēṟṟa sīrān tiruvāgam tīṇḍiṟṟuc cenṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

māṇi uruvāgik koṇḍu – assuming the garb of a bachelor; ulagam – throughout the world; nīr ēṟṟa sīrān – one who has great auspicious qualities and who took as alms; tiru āgam – his beautiful, divine, auspicious form; tīṇḍiṟṟu – when it felt the entire world; nām kāṇappugil – even if we enquire; pēsil – even if we attempt to speak; aṟivu kaikkoṇḍa nal nenjam – the superior mind which has knowledge; nāṇap padumanṟē – should it not feel shy?

**Перевод:**

**kāṇap pugil nām pēsil aṟivu kaikkoṇḍa nal nenjam nāṇappadum anṟē – Even if we, lacking in knowledge, desire to see him and speak about his svarūpam (basic nature) as it is, śouldn’t the heart, which is the seat of knowledge and which thinks good for us, feel shy, after comparing our svarūpam and his activities? āḻvār says in the latter part of the pāsuram as to what is such an activity that emperumān carried out.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that even though he told his heart to see emperumān, considering our svarūpam (basic nature) and his activities, śouldn’t the heart feel shy to see him? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māṇi uruvāgi nīrēṟṟu ulagam koṇḍa sīrān tiruvāgam senṟu tīṇḍiṟṟu** – emperumān took the garb of a dwarf bachelor and went to mahābali to get as alms the three worlds. During this time, he went to feel the worlds \[touch them\] with his beautiful, divine form. If we desire to see that activity or to speak about that, śouldn’t the heart feel shy? In other words, does it make any meaning to see him falling head over heels to take possession of the three worlds? The opinion here is that even though [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had told his heart in the previous pāsuram “*kāṇ*” (to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), the good heart, which has knowledge, should have felt shy to see him and to speak about him. The opinion further here is that while emperumān has been working since time immemorial to get hold of us, it is not proper on our part to attain him on our own. This indicates the significant superiority of *paragata svīkāram* (where emperumān is engaged actively in trying to get hold of the jīvātmā) over *svagata svīkāram* (in which the jīvātmā is making efforts to attain emperumān).

**tiruvāgam tīṇḍiṟṟu nāṇappadumanṟē** – we can consider this as a re-emphasising of what he himself had mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-5-valavezh/) “*vaḷavēḻulagu*” since the heart, which has knowledge,  should have felt shy in going about to feel [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), considering its \[the heart’s\] lowliness.

We will take up 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 21. Senrangu vennaragil

*senṟangu vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku inṟingennenjāl idukkuṇḍa aṇrangup pār uruvum pār vaḷaitta nīr uruvum kaṇ pudaiyak kār uruvan tān nimirttak kāl*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār uruvan tān – emperumān who has the form of clouds; anṟu – during that time when mahābali conducted yajñam (ritual with agni, fire); angu – on his yajñabhūmi (place where he conducted the yajñam); pār uruvum – the form of earth; pār vaḷaitta nīr uruvum – the form of water \[ocean\] which is surrounding earth; kaṇ pudaiya – such that they \[the worlds\] are hidden; nimirtta – making them \[divine feet\] to grow; kāl – divine feet; angu senṟu – going to the respective world; vem naragil – in samsāram which is like a cruel hell; sērāmal kāppadaṟku – to protect so as not to get immersed; inṟu – now; ingu – in this world itself; en nenjāl – by my mind; idukkuṇḍa – hemmed in

**Перевод:**

**senṟu angu vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku inṟu ingu en nenjāl idukkuṇḍa – in order to make sure that we do not get caught in the hell called as samsāram (materialistic realm) and to protect us, the divine feet which went to various worlds and measured them, now, in this world, were hemmed in by my heart.  The words vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku could also be taken to mean that (the divine feet) would not allow us to go away from emperumān, thinking that we are ‘unsuitable’ and would protect us. In the first interpretation, the word vennaragu would mean samsāram and in the second interpretation, it would mean moving away from emperumān, considering ourselves as ‘unsuitable’. For those who have knowledge, vouldn’t both samsāram and moving away from emperumān be the same?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the divine foot \[of emperumān\] which measured the worlds, came on its own and took residence in his heart. He says further that if anyone tries to control it, it is not possible. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

In the rest of the pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) explains the nature of the divine feet which were hemmed in.

**anṟangup pāruruvum pār vaḷaitta nīr uruvum kaṇ pudaiyak kār uruvan nimirttak kāl** – The divine feet, which are hemmed in now, in this world, by my heart, are those which belong to him who, on that day \[when mahābali gave him consent to give land equivalent to vāman’s three steps\], took a huge, darkened cloud like form with which he hid all the worlds and the oceans surrounding the worlds.

**kāl idukkuṇḍa** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is amazed that the divine feet, which measured all the worlds, got hemmed in by his heart.

We will move on to the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 22. Kale podattirindu

*kālē podattirindu kattuvarām inanāḷ mālār kudi pugundār en manattē mēlāl tarukkum idam pāttinodum val vinaiyār tām vīṟṟu irukkumidam kāṇādiḷaittu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālār – sarvēśvaran (supreme being) who is very great; en manattē – in my mind; kudi pugundār – came with his entourage; mēlāl – in earlier times; tarukkum – tormenting; idam pāttinodum – with arrogance; val vinaiyār – cruel sins; tām vīṟṟirukkum idam kāṇādu – not finding a suitable place where then can live in comfort; iḷaittu – being without any strength; kālē poda tirindu – roaming all over the place such that their legs would split; ina nāḷ – in these times; kattuvarām – called out

**Перевод:**

**mālār kudi pugundār en manattē – Just as periyāḻvār had mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-10 “aravattamaḷiyinōdum aḻagiya pāṟkadalōdum aravindappāvaiyum tānum agambadi vandu pugundu” (emperumān entered my heart with the mattress of ādiśēśan, the beautiful milky ocean, pirātti who resides on a lotus), emperumān entered my mind along with his entourage.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that after emperumān took residence in his heart, his sins which were a hurdle for him in attaining emperumān ran away from his heart. Not finding a place to stay, roaming around till their legs split up, they are calling out now. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mēlāl tarukkum idampāttinodum valvinaiyār tām inanāl vīṟṟirukkum idam kāṇādu iḷaittu kālē podattirindu kattuvarām** – the cruel sins, which were tormenting us, with arrogance, from time immemorial, are now unable to find a place from where they can rule; they have lost all their strength; they are roaming all over the place \[in search of a place to rule over\] with their legs almost split and are calling out. The word *tarukkum* refers to tormenting. The word *idampādu* refers to arrogance. *vīṟṟirukkai* refers to ruling \[from the heart\]. Considering the cruelty of sins, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is referring to them as *valvinaiyār* in an honorific sense.

We will move on to the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 23. Ilaippay ilaiyappay

*iḷaippāy iḷaiyāppāy nenjamē sonnēn iḷaikka naman tamargaḷ paṟṟi iḷaippeyda nāy tandu mōdāmal nalguvān nalgāppān tāy tandai evvuyirkkum tān*

**Пословный перевод:**

naman tamargaḷ – the messengers of yama; iḷaikka paṟṟi – making (the cētanas) to be wizened even as they seize; iḷaippu eyda – to become weaker further; nāy tandu mōdāmal – preventing the dogs (in hell) from hurting us; nalguvān – whether he (emperumān) protects; nalgāppān – or does not protect; evvuyirkkum – for all creatures; tān tāy tandai – he himself is the mother and the father; iḷaippāy – whether you become infirm (by not attaining him); iḷaiyāppāy – or do not become infirm (by attaining him); sonnēn – I instructed you (this secretive message)

**Перевод:**

**iḷaippāy iḷaiyāppāy – it does not matter whether you become weak by not attaining him or not become weak by attaining him. Alternatively it could be construed as – it does not matter whether you become weak by engaging with worldly matters or do not become weak by attaining emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “It does not matter whether you attain him or do not. He will remain as the protector. In the same way, whether he protects or doesn’t, it is your duty to attain him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nenjamē sonnēn** – Listen to me, I am telling you a beneficial matter. That, emperumān is every relationship for us, is not something that can be instructed to anyone. It is a secretive matter which could be instructed only to qualified people. I am telling you such a matter; listen to me.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart further “Whether he protects us or not, no one can deny the fact that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is related to us by his nature, is the protector of all”, considering that his \[āḻvār’s\] heart is thinking “he is remaining without protecting us”

**iḷaikka naman tamargaḷ paṟṟi** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that when the messengers come to take hold of this [jīvātmā (soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), at the time of seizing him, he becomes very weak.

**iḷaippeyda nāy tandu mōdāmal** – to prevent further weakening of the jīvātmā after he is taken to hell and letting the dogs on him.

**nalguvān nalgāppān** – whether [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protects us or not

**tāy tandai evvuyirkkum tān** – He is like mother and father and every other relationship, in all states, and protects. When he, who is there to protect, does not protect at any point of time, could we go to someone else for protection! Did [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) not say mercifully in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-8-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvaimozhi-5-8-8-kalaivay-thunbam/) “*kaḷaivāy tunbam kaḷaiyādoḻivāy kaḷaigaṇ maṟṟilēn*” (it does not matter whether you get rid of my hurdles or not, I do not have anyone else to protect me)! The opinion here is that “It does not matter whether you attain him or not, he is always protecting you. In the same way, it does not matter whether he protects you or not, you must always consider him as your refuge”.

We will take up the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 24. Tane tanittonral

*tānē tanittōnṟal tannaḷapponṟillādān tānē piṟargatkum taṟṟōnṟal tānē iḷaikkiṟpār kīḻmēlām mīṇḍamaippānānāl aḷakkiṟpār pārin mēl ār*

**Пословный перевод:**

tanit tōnṟal tānē – he is the only one who has incomparable incarnations; than aḷappukku – to compare with him; onṟum illādān tānē – he is the only one who does not have any entity; piṟargatkum taṟṟōṇṟal tānē – he is the most famous antaryāmi (indvelling soul) for all the others; tānē iḷaikkil – if such an emperumān drops down (from protecting); pār kīḻ mēlām – the earth will become topsy turvy and lose its balance; mīṇḍu amaippānānāl – if he starts protecting once again; aḷakkiṟpār – one who can estimate (the extensiveness that he has in protecting); pāril mēl Ar – who is there, on the earth?

**Перевод:**

**tānē tanittōnṟal – he is the only one who incarnates, without any comparison. Unlike jīvātmās who are born out of their karma (past deeds), emperumān takes incarnations on his own volition, in order to uplift the jīvātmās. Hence āḻvār refers to him as tanittōnṟal. The implied meaning is that this term, on its own, establishes the fact that he is the protector.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that whether emperumān protects or not, only he is to be attained since he is naturally related to us. In this pāsuram he proves through both anvayam and vyatirēkam (connection and difference or affirmative and negative aspects) that emperumān is the protector and he says that there is no question of emperumān not protecting us. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

To a question whether there is anyone else who does like this, āḻvār responds  . . .

**tannaḷapponṟillādān** – he is one who does not have any other entity (who or) which can be considered as comparison.  Hence the opinion is that there is no one else who can protect.

**tānē piṟargatkum taṟṟōnṟal** – Just as it is mentioned in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 5-7 “*ēśa ta ātmā antaryāmyamruta:*” (only this *paramapuruśa* (supreme entity) who is the *sarvāntaryāmi* (indvelling soul of all) has *amrutatvam* (does not have death) and remains as your indvelling soul), he remains as the indvelling soul for all, other than himself, and is the reason for their existence, behaviour and activities. It is implied through this that when all the others are under his control, there can be no place for doubting whether he is the protector for all.

**tānē iḷaikkil pār kīḻ mēlām** – If he momentarily steps back from protecting others, the earth will turn turtle. Since earth has not become topsy turvy, the implied meaning is that he protects at all times. Through *vyatirēkam* (negation) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) establishes, thus far, that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the protector.

Now, through *anvayam* (connection or assertion) he establishes the same fact.

**mīṇḍu amaippānānāl** – when all the sentient entities become identical to insentient entities, during the time of deluge, and merge with him, if emperumān decides in his divine mind that he should protect them and creates them (again)

**aḷakkiṟpār pārin mēl Ar** – who can estimate his qualities of being the indvelling soul of all entities, looking after their existence, providing them with śāstras (sacred texts), incarnating etc and protecting them?

We will move on to the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 25. Aranum adanum

*ārānum ādānum seyya agalidaittai ārāyndu adu tiruttal āvadē sīrār manattalaivan tunbattai māṟṟinēn vānōr inattalaivan kaṇṇanāl yān*

**Пословный перевод:**

ārānum – let it be anyone; ādānum seyya – let (him or her) do anything; agal idattai – in the expansive earth; ārāyndu – searching; adu – their activities; tiruttal āvadē – it is possible for us to rectify?; yān – I; vānōr inam talaivan – being the svāmy (lord) for the assembly of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); kaṇṇanāl – with the assistance of emperumān who incarnated as kriṣṇa; sīr Ar – full of benefit (of being loving towards emperumān); manam talai vantunbam – the terrible sorrow of samsāram which is uppermost on the mind; māṟṟinēn – removed

**Перевод:**

**ārānum ādānum seyya agalidattai ārāyndu adu tiruttal āvadē – let anyone do whatever (s)he wants.  Is it possible for me to examine the expansive earth and rectify each and every cētana (sentient entity)? āḻvār cries out here too, just as he had done earlier when he said in tiruviruttam 96 “ninkaṇ vētkai eḻuvippanē ” (I will create desire towards you) out of desire to spread devotion towards emperumān throughout the world and finding that the word was not correcting from the mistakes made, he had cried out in tiruvāsiriyam 6 “Oh Oh ulaginadin iyalvē” (Oh! How terrible is the nature of the world!) Just as he had said in the last pāsuram of tiruāsiriyam “naḷirmadic cadaiyanum” that he had somehow escaped, āḻvār consoles himself in this pāsuram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār was asked by some people “You had tried at several instances to correct the errant cētanas (sentient entities) such as in tiruviruttam 96th pāsuram ‘vaṇangum tuṟaigaḷ . . . . ninkaṇ vētkai eḻuvippanē’. To what extent were you successful in those efforts?” This pāsuram (25) is a response to that query. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that emperumān is the protector for all.  Though he is the protector, since he has given, to some extent, independence to the bound cētanas in this samsāram and allowed them to carry out their activities, his efforts at protecting them do not fructify in most of the cases \[due to the independence that they have\]. āḻvār says in this pāsuram “the cētanas do not listen to us if we try to correct them. We made our efforts and it is providence that we somehow escaped”. He feels as joyous as a person would feel who, having escaped from being robbed by thieves, finds in a safe place that the money that he had brought with him is safe. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**vānōr inat talaivan kaṇṇanāl manattalai vantunbattai yān māṟṟinēn** – With the help of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who came as simplicity personified as a human being though he is the leader of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam), I changed the terrible sorrow of samsāram (materialistic realm) which had occupied my mind. It is in this mind that I have [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) residing as my wealth. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says “is it possible for me to correct everyone?”

Let us take up the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 26. Yanum ennenjum

*yānum ennenjum isaindoḻindōm valvinaiyaik kānum malaiyum pugak kadivān tānōr iruḷanna māmēni emmiṟaiyār tanda aruḷennum taṇḍāl adittu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ōr iruḷ tān anna – it is as if darkness took a form; māmēni – having a great divine form; em iṟaiyār – emperumān; tanda – gave; aruḷ ennum taṇḍāl – the stick of (that emperumān’s) mercy; adittu – beating; val vinaiyai – (our) cruel sins; kānum malaiyum puga – to enter forest sand mountains; kadivān – to drive away; yānum en nenjum – my mind and I; isaindu oḻindōm – have agreed

**Перевод:**

**yānum en nunjum isaindu oḻindōm – We had to suffer all along only because my mind and I were not willing when emperumān tried to enter my mind in order to take hold of us. Since we have agreed now \[to have emperumān\], that shortcoming has disappeared. My mind and I, who were not harmonious to each other from time immemorial, have now become friendly to each other. When such is the case, do we need to talk about emperumān who had been making efforts to bring us together, to take hold of us now?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said “van tunbattai māṟṟinēn”, implying that he had done something on his own \[to remove his sorrow\]. In this pāsuram, he says “When emperumān came to get hold of me, by falling head over heal, all that I did was not to reject him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

When asked as to attain which benefit that they had agreed, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says . . . .

**valvainaiyaik kānum malaiyum pugakkadivān** – āḻvār says that he and his mind agreed to become harmonious in order to get the benefit of driving away their sins into the forests and mountains. We can consider that āḻvār is referring to his being *unsuitable* to be with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and deciding to move away from him as his cruel sin.

When asked whether he had anything in his hand to achieve this, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says . . .

**tānōr iruḷ anna māmēni em iṟaiyār tanda aruḷ ennum taṇḍāl adittu** – I did not have any implement in my hand \[to drive away my sins\]. There was no other means too, to get this benefit. He says that he drove away the cruel sins with the help of the grace which came in the form of stick from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has as his divine physical form, the epitome of darkness itself. The opinion implied here is that other than emperumān’s mercy there is no means \[for us\].

Let us move on to the 27<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 27. Adiyal padi kadanda

*adiyāl padi kadanda muttō adanṟēl mudiyāl visumbaḷanda muttō nediyāy seṟi kaḻalgaḷ tāḷ nimirttuc cenṟulagamellām aṟigilamāl nī aḷanda anṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nediyāy – Oh one who is superior to everyone else!; nī – you; seṟi kaḻalgaḷ – having anklets which are close to each other; tāḷ – divine feet; nimirttu – making them grow; ulagam eḷḷam senṟu – going to all the worlds; aḷanda anṟu – during that time when you divinely measured the worlds; padi kadanda muttō – is the happiness due to measuring all the worlds (which we see on your divine face)?; adu anṟēl – if it is not due to that; mudiyāḷ – with your divine head; visumbu aḷanda muttō – is it the happiness due to measuring the skies (which we see)?; aṟigilam Al – we do not know; how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**adiyāl padi kadanda muttō adanṟēl mudiyāl visumbaḷanda muttō – If we consider the samskruta word mugda: to have got changed to muttu in tamiḻ, then the meaning for the world mugda which is ‘infant’ would transform to ‘beauty’ in tamiḻ for muttu. Once āḻvār said “yānum ennenjum isaindoḻindōm” (once my heart and I decided to be in harmony), emperumān became very happy. āḻvār enjoys that beauty on emperumān’s face. He is asking whether the beauty that he is seeing on emperumān’s face is the beauty which was there on emperumān’s face when he measured the worlds with his divine foot or it is the beauty which was there on emperumān’s face when he measured the skies with his divine head. Alternatively, we can consider that āḻvār is referring to muttu (pearl) which is one of the nine gems. Later in the pāsuram, āḻvār is referring to seṟikaḻal which is anklet made with pearls. Also, in tiruviruttam 50 āḻvār mentions about ṇīḷ mudi veṇmutta vāsigaittāy (having his crown with white pearls). Hence we can consider that āḻvār is enjoying the beauty of divine ornaments on emperumān by asking whether they are the pearls when he measured the divine worlds or they are the pearls when he measured the skies. Since in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 90, pēyāḻvār mentions about silambum seṟi kaḻalum whereby the pearl which is inside the ornament is manifested outside or in this pāsuram itself, by referring to emperumān wearing anklets which have pearls packed closely, we can consider that pearls are there on the divine foot. Another interpretation for muttu is that in samskrutam, mut refers to happiness. Thus we can consider that āḻvār is asking emperumān whether the happiness on his divine face is due to his measuring the worlds with his divine foot or due to his divine face pervading the skies.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*By saying “isaindoḻindōm” in pāsuram 26, āḻvār said that he does not have any shortcoming and by saying “aruḷennum taṇḍāl” he said that whatever he gets is through emperumān only. What remains to be done now is to enjoy emperumān! He does that in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aṟigilam** – we are not able to determine this.

**Al** – How amazing is this!

**nediyāy** – āḻvār calls out to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as *Oh trivikrama*! ōr as one who is superior to everyone else!

The verse can be rearranged to read *nediyāy seṟi kaḻalgaḷ tāḷ nimirttuc cenṟulagamellām nī aḷanda anṟu adiyāl padi kadanda muttō adanṟēl mudiyāl visumbaḷanda muttō aṟigilam Al*.

We will take up the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 28. Anre nam kan

*anṟē nam kaṇ kāṇum āḻiyān kāruruvam inṟē nām kāṇādiruppaduvum enṟēnum katkaṇṇāl kāṇāda avvuruvai nenjennum utkaṇṇēl kāṇum uṇarndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇ kaṇṇāl – with the external eye which goes by the name of ‘eye’; enṟēnum kāṇāda – cannot be seen at any point of time; avvuruvai – that divine form; nenju ennum – what is called as mind; uḷ kaṇ – eye which is inside; uṇarndu kāṇumēl – if it can visualise; anṟē – at that time itself; āḻiyān kār uruvam – the dark divine form of one who holds the divine disc in his hand; nam kaṇ kāṇum – our external eye will also see; nām kāṇādiruppaduvum – our inability to see emperumān; inṟē – now (when we cannot visualise him with our heart)

**Перевод:**

**anṟē nam kaṇ kāṇum āḻiyān kāruruvam – our external eye will see the dark, divine form of the supreme being who holds the divine disc on his hand, only on that day.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār meditated on emperumān’s divine form when he measured the worlds, he desired to see that form with his eyes, which are external, on his face. He becomes disgusted when he is not able to see emperumān with his external eyes and after thinking about it, says “it is not emperumān’s fault that we are unable to see him. It is our fault only”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

When asked, “On which day?” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says

**enṟēnum kaṇ kaṇṇāl kāṇāda avvuruvai nenjennum uḷ kaṇ uṇarndu kāṇumēl** – our external eye is similar to the eye which is present on the feather of a peacock. On that day when our heart, which is also called as internal eye, is able to see the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), our external “peacock” eye, which is unable to see the divine form of emperumān, will be able to see.

**inṟē nām kāṇādiruppaduvum** – it is now when our internal eye of heart is unable to visualise [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) that our external eye is also not able to see. When the internal eye sees him, the external eye will also see him.

When [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram that he and his heart are now in harmony, why is it that his internal eye (heart) is not able to see [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? The opinion here is that only when one reaches the stage of *paramabhakti* in which the person cannot sustain himself if he separated from emperumān, could the heart be able to visualise him. Thus the implied meaning is that when one survives when separated from emperumān, he would not be able to see emperumān with his external eye.

We will move on to the 29<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 29. Unara oruvarkku

*uṇara oruvarkku eḷiyanē sevvē iṇarum tuḻāyalangal endai uṇarat tanakku eḷiyar evvaḷavar avvaḷavanānāl enakku eḷiyan emperumān ingu*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṇarum – blossomed; tuḻāy alangal endai – empirān who has tuḷasi garland; oruvarkku sevvē uṇara eḷiyanē – is he simple for anyone (of his own efforts) to know well? (no); tanakku eḷiyar – his devotees; evvaḷavar – the extent to which they have affection; avvaḷavan – he has affection to the same extent towards them; ānāl – hence; enakku – for me (who has huge affection for him); emperumān – my svāmy (lord); ingu uṇara eḷiyan – is simple for me to understand in this world itself

**Перевод:**

**sevvē uṇara oruvarkku eḷiyanē – it is not possible for anyone to say that emperumān is ‘this great’ by citing a simile or to say that he ’is like’ this by estimating him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that the parabhakti state \[sustain oneself if together with emperumān or perish otherwise\] that he has got is very rare for others to get. It is because I got this state of parabhakti that emperumān is very simple for me. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**oruvarkku** – even if the person is great in terms of knowledge, it is not possible for even such a person to know about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with his own efforts. ḍidn’t even upaniśat say this as mentioned in kēnōpaniśat 2-3 “*yasyāmatam tasya matam matam yasys na vēda sa:* I *avijñātam vijānatām vijñātam avijānatām* īī” (for whom brahmam (supreme being) is not known (as being this much), (it) is known to him; for whom it is considered as known, he does not know (it). For those who know (about its extent) it is unknown and for those who do not know (about its extent) it is known).

Which is that entity?

**iṇarum tuḻāy alangal endai** – The identity for the supreme being who cannot be estimated is the tuḷasi garland which is on his shoulders. The tamiḻ poets of *sanga kālam* (a period which is a few millennia ago) also sang in praise of such [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in their *paripādal* composition “*nāṟu iṇarttuḻāyōn nalgin alladai ēṟudal eḷidō vīṟupeṟutuṟakkam*”.

**iṇarum** – blossoming. Once the tuḷasi garland is applied on emperumān’s divine shoulders it will blossom more than it would on its own land. On the shoulders of dēvas (celestial entities) a garland will remain without withering. Since emperumān is the dēva (lord) for all the dēvas, the garland will blossom more than it would otherwise.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) was asked “If he cannot be estimated, then he could be non-existent”. He responds . . .

**uṇarat tanakku eḷiyavar evvaḷavar avvaḷavan** – we only said that he cannot be known to be ‘to this extent’. We did not say that he cannot be known at all. He will manifest himself to his followers with an affection which is to the same extent with which his followers have affection towards him. The word *uṇara* refers to the affection related to knowledge. Has it not been mercifully mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 7-17](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-17/) “*tēśām jñāni nitya yukta: ēkabhaktir viśiśyatē*: I I *priyō hi jñāninō’tyartamaham sa ca mama priya*: īī” (One who is always with (me) and who shows affection only for me is superior (among those four types of devotees). Am I not dear to that knowledgeable one? (In the same way) that knowledgeable person is also dear to me) and in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-10 “*tammaiyugappārait tām ugappar*” (he will show affection towards those who show affection towards him)!

**ānāl enakku eḷiyan emperumān ingu** – Hence, that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is my svāmy (lord) is simple enough for me to be seen by the internal eye of my mind since I have *parabhakti* due to which I can see him with my internal eye in this world itself.

In the previous pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had lamented “*inṟē nām kāṇādiruppaduvum*” since he had not attained the state of *parabhakti* then, while in this pāsuram since he has got *parabhakti* he says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is simple enough for him to see him with his internal eye. Thus there is no contradiction between the two pāsurams.

We will move on to the 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 30. Ingillai pandupol

*ingillai paṇḍupōl vīṟṟiruttal ennudaiya sengaṇmāl sīrkkum siṟiduḷḷam angē madiyadakki niṟpadanil valvinaiyār tām mīṇḍu adiyeduppadanṟō aḻagu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ingu – in my mind (for my karmas); paṇḍu pōl – like earlier times; vīṟṟiruttal illai – there is no place to sit expansively; ennudaiya sengaṇmāl – my svāmy (lord), having reddish eyes indicating affection, that sarvēśvaran’s; sīrkkum – auspicious qualities; (idamillādahapadi – without a place); uḷḷam siṟidu – my heart is very small; valvinaiyār tām – my cruel sins; angē – in that same place (vherefrom they were ruling); madi adakki niṟpadanil – instead of standing submissively, folding the end of their dress; mīṇḍu – moving away from there; adi eduppadanṟō aḻagu – isn’t stepping back, a thing of beauty?

**Перевод:**

**ingillai paṇḍu pōl vīṟṟiruttal – There is no place in my heart for my karmas (virtuous and sinful deeds) which were sitting expansively as if they were ruling my heart since time immemorial. vīṟṟiruttal – sitting in a way which displays their excellence.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “Let emperumān’s greatness and eminence remain. After we start meditating on his auspicious qualities, there is no place in our heart for our karmas (virtuous and sinful deeds) which have been troubling us and which were hurdles in attaining him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

What has happened now for them?

**ennudaiya sengaṇmāl sīrkkum siṟiduḷḷam** – my heart is so small that there is no place even for the auspicious qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is my svāmy (lord) and who has reddish eyes. The term *sengaṇmāl* indicates that he is supreme and that he has deep affection towards his followers. *māl* – he is a huge supreme being.

Next, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says something assuming that the sins had requested him “We will stand in a corner there itself”

**angē madi adakki niṟpadanil valvinaiyār tām mīṇḍu adi eduppadanṟō aḻagu** – āḻvār says that it will be respectable for the cruel sins to vacate their place instead of standing in a corner, shrinking themselves, in the place where they were ruling roost all along. āḻvār is using the term *valvinaiyār* in a derisive way \[to indicate as if he is giving them respect\]. *madi* – refers to the front portion of the dress worn by women which is tucked in the waist. m*adiyadakki* indicates that the cruel sins are willing to stand in a corner, by shrinking themselves.

The essence of this pāsuram is that sins will be removed if one meditates on [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s auspicious qualities.

We will take up the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 31. Alagum arivomay

*aḻagum aṟivōmāy valvinaiyāit (yum) tīrppān niḻalum aditāṟum ānōm suḻalak kudangaḷ talai mīdu eduttuk koṇḍādi anṟat tadam kadalai mēyār tamakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

val vinaiyai – cruel sins; tīrppān – to get rid of; aḻagum aṟivōmāy – having the beautiful knowledge (that emperumān is our means to attain him); kudangaḷ talai mīdu eduttukkoṇḍu suḻala ādi – keeping the pots on top of the head and dancing in such a way that they come down spinning (from the sky); anṟu – after that; a tadam kadalai – that expansive tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); mēyār tamakku – for emperumān, who attained (in order to take rest after having danced with the pots); niḻalum aditāṟum ānōm – we became his shadow and his pāduka (footwear)

**Перевод:**

**val vinaiyait tīrppān aḻagum aṟivōmāy niḻalum aditāṟum ānōm – we, who are like a rat which has weak bones, instead of removing our sins through our  virtuous deeds, similar to removing dirt from a new, dirtied dress, have the beautiful knowledge of removing our sins with the help of the supreme being. We also became his servitors by remaining as his shadow and as his footwear. The word aditāṟu refers to both the footwear which fits well with the foot and the lines which are on the sole of the foot.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that with the help of the supreme entity itself we got rid of our enemies and attained the benefit of carrying out kainkaryam to him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) explains next as to for whom we are carrying out service.

**suḻalak kudangal talai mīdu eduttukkoṇḍu ādi anṟu attadangadalai mēyār tamakku** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), in his incarnation as kriṣṇa, stood at the junction of four streets, manifesting the beauty of his divine form to all, and carried pots on his head, with which he danced. While dancing, he spun around in such a way that it appeared as if the pots were spinning from the sky. In order to take rest from the tiredness of such dancing, he reclined in the milky ocean. For such emperumān, we became his shadow by remaining with him always. In the same way, we also became his footwear and the lines on the sole of his foot by carrying out service to him. The opinion here is that we became focussed and carried out service to emperumān by remaining as his shadow during his incarnation as kriṣṇa as well as later when he ascended to tiruppāṟkadal, much similar to how lakśmaṇa had carried out service to śrī rāma, both at ayōdyā and in the forest, when śrī rāma was awake or asleep.

We will go on to the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 32. Tamakkadimai venduvar

*tamakkadimai vēṇḍuvār dāmōdaranār tamakkadimai seyyenṟāl seyyādu emakkenṟu tām seyyum tīvinaikkē tāḻvuṟuvar nenjinār yām seyvadu ivvidattu ingu yādu*

**Пословный перевод:**

adimai – being servile (towards those who are involved with him); tamakku vēṇḍuvār – one who desires it for himself; dāmōdaranār tamakku – for dāmaōdara (who had a scar to display to others, when he was tied down by yaśōdā); adimai sey enṟāl – if told to carry out service; seyyādu – not doing it; nenjinār – my mind, the elderly person \[āḻvār says this with derision\]; emakku enṟu – with stubbornness saying ‘I will do what I like’; tām seyyum – what it does often; tīvinaikkē – the activity of going away \[from emperumān\]; tāḻvuṟuvar – will be interested in; ivvidattu – when things are like this; ingu – in this world; yām seyvadu yādu – what is there for us to do?

**Перевод:**

**tamakkadimai vēnduvār – one who likes to be servile toward those who are engaged a little bit towards him. This implies that emperumān would like to be dependent on his followers by being servile to them, just as people say that they like something bitter or they like something sour.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that while he is decisive in carrying out service to emperumān his heart steps back, thinking “It is not proper on our part who have connection with this body to approach him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Where have we seen this behaviour in emperumān?

**dāmōdaranār** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that we have seen such a behaviour when he was tied down to a mortar by his mother yaśōdā through his stomach and beaten for stealing butter. The word *dāmōdara* means stomach being tied by a rope. The word *adimai* refers to being servile. Alternatively *adimai* could refer to followers \[of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] and the meaning would change to \[emperumān\] being affectionate towards his followers who are servile to him.

**dāmōdaranār tamakku adimai sey enṟāl seyyādu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that when he tells his heart to carry out service to kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who is the follower of his followers, his heart does not carry this out.

**emakkenṟu tām seyyem tīvinaikkē tāḻvuṟuvar** – he \[the heart\] will be stubborn saying “I will do whatever I want” and will carry out inappropriate acts only. Here the word *tām seyyum tīvinai* refers to the act of moving away from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) considering itself as ‘unsuitable’. The long syllable in *tīvinaikk****E*** implies that the heart is wont to doing this often.

**nenjinār** – the elderly person who goes in his own way instead of listening to me!

**yām seyvadu ivvidattu ingu yādu** – emperumān is keen on taking us as his follower. The heart is keen on moving away from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The opinion here is that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is indecisive on whether to listen to his possession \[heart\] or to carry out as per emperumān, who is the possessor.

We will take up the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 33. Yadanum onrariyil

*yādānum onṟaṟiyil tannugakkil enkolō yādānum nērndu aṇugā āṟudān yādānum tēṟumā seyyā asurargaḷai nēmiyāl pāṟupāṟākkinān pāl*

**Пословный перевод:**

yādānum – in any way; tēṟumā seyya – not carrying out activities which generate confidence (in emperumān); asurargaḷai – demons; nēmiyāl – with cakrāyuda (divine disc); pāṟu pāṟu ākkinān pāl – towards emperumān who cut them to pieces; yādānum nērndu – by giving something (which does not belong to self); aṇugā āṟudān en kolō – why is he not being attained?; than ugakkil (en kolō) – what if (the cētana) develops affection towards himself?

**Перевод:**

**yādānum onṟaṟiyil en kolō – Is it not proper to know any entity? Just as it has been mentioned in mudal tiruvandādi 67 “oṇ tāmaraiyāḷ oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu” the implied meaning is that if anything is known, then that knowledge will reach down to emperumān, the antaryāmi (indvelling soul) of that entity and hence it could be said that he is known.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār is asking whether emperumān should not be attained if one has knowledge. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tannugakkil en kolō** – even if one does not know an entity other than oneself, one can at the least like oneself. Could that not be done? The opinion is that since there is no benefit other than desiring emperumān, if one likes oneself, that would end up in desiring emperumān.

**enkolō yādānum nērndu anugāvāṟudān** – why is it that he is not attained by offering something? The implied meaning is that since every entity belongs to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), by offering something which belongs to emperumān, should he not be attained. What is the reason for using the term *nērndu* (bestowing something) which is actually carrying out a huge activity? The jīvātmā (soul) on seeing anything thinks that everything is his and (not emperumān’s) and gets bewildered. He thinks that he is independent. Since he is giving something which he thinks (mistakenly) as his, it could be said that he is bestowing something.

Would [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) be happy if the cētana offers something which is not his, but thinks that it is his? Just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 8-1-10 “*tālgaḷai enakkē talaittalaic ciṟappat tanda pērudavikkaimmāṟā tōḷgaḷai ārattaḻuvi ennuyirai aṟavilai seydanan sōdi*” – when [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) gave to emperumān as his gratitude his ātmā which is actually emperumān’s, emperumān was so overjoyed as if he had received something which was not his, that his shoulders, heads, eyes etc multiplied in thousands, as said in the same pāsuram 8-1-10 “*tōḷgaḷ āyirattāy mudigaḷ āyirattāy tuṇai malarkkangaḷ āyirattāy*”. Thus there is no doubt that emperumān becomes happy when cētana offers him anything. Even by offering one’s ātmā to emperumān, he should be approached is the underlying meaning here.

**yādānum tēṟumā seyyā asurargaḷai nēmiyāl pāṟu pāṟu ākkinān pāl** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) held the divine disc in his hand only to destroy the enemies of his followers and not for destroying his enemies.

**yādānum tēṟumā seyyā asurargaḷai** – if in the assembly of demons, if there is even one who is favourable, likeprahlāda, he will protect all the demons. The implied meaning is that he will destroy the demons with his divine disc only if they indulge in activities which do not create trust in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The words should be rearranged to read *pāṟu pāṟu ākkinān pāḷ yādānum onṟaṟiyil* to get the correct meaning. The larger meaning is that one should offer something to him and attain him who removes one’s enemies just as he had removed the demons with his divine disc. One who is powerless should attain the omnipotent. Hence only emperumān should be attained.

We will take up the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 34. Palali nikidakkum

*pālāḻi nī kidakkum paṇbai yām kēttēyum kālāḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇ suḻalum neelāḻic cōdiyāy ādiyāy tolvinai empāṟkadiyum nīdiyāy niṟcārndu ninṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

neel āḻi – like a dark ocean; sōdiyāy – having effulgence; ādiyāy – Oh one who is the cause for everything!; empāl – with me; tol vinai kadiyum – removing sins which have been there since time immemorial; nīdiyāy – one who has the nature!; nin sārndu ninṟu – holding on to you; nī pālāḻi kidakkum paṇbai – the beauty with which you are reclining in the milky ocean; yām kēttēyum – even as I hear; kālāḻum – my legs become weak; nenjaḻiyum – my mind will flutter; kaṇ suḻalum – eyes will spin (in bewilderment)

**Перевод:**

**neelāḻic cōdiyāy pālāḻi nī kidakkum – The way emperumān, with his dark, effulgent complexion is reclining on the milky ocean is similar to a black ocean reclining on a white ocean.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that one should attain emperumān by offering something or the other. In this pāsuram, he says that when he goes near emperumān in order to attain him, his limbs become very weak and he is unable to approach him. This pāsuram is an example of how āḻvārs become ecstatic when they are deep in devotion. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nī kidakkum** – Just as it is mentioned in [tirumālai 23](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thirumalai-23/) “*kidandadōr kidakkai*”, isn’t emperumān’s beautiful posture in the reclining state much superior to his beautiful states in standing or sitting postures!

**paṇbai yām kēttēyum** – The moment I hear about your reclining posture. How much will [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), who suffers so much on just hearing about the beauty of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s reclining posture in the milky ocean, suffer if he sees him directly? āḻvār next describes how he suffers on hearing about emperumān’s beauty.

**kālāḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇ suḻalum** – my legs will become weak. My mind will flutter. My eyes will spin around in bewilderment. The term *kāl* is representative of all the five organs of activity (hands, legs, mouth, reproductive and anal organs) while the term *kaṇ* is representative of all the five organs of senses (eyes, ears, nose, tongue and skin). [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) mercifully describes similar emotions in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-3-5 “*kālum eḻā kaṇṇa nīrum nillā udal sōrndu nadungi kural mēlumeḻā mayirkkūccumaṟā ena tōḷgaḷum vīḻvoḻiyā mālugaḷā niṟkum en mananē* ” (my legs do not move out from the place where they were; my tears are not retained inside; the body is in a shambles and I am not able to speak; gooseflesh happens continuously and the shoulders droop down; my mind is utterly bewildered). ḍidn’t [toṇḍaraippodi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) also mercifully say in [tirumālai 19](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-19/) “*aravaṇaittuyilumākaṇḍu udalenakku urugumālō*” (after seeing the reclining posture of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) on ādiśēśan, my body melts) and in [tirumālai 18](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-18/) “*kaṇṇanaikkaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ paniyarumbudirumālō*” (my eyes, after seeing the divine form of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) have not stopped shedding tears)!

Is it only the beauty of emperumān which is making [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) to behave like this? . . . .

**ādiyāy** – Oh the one who created me and who is the ordained lord for me!

**tolvinai em pāl kadiyum nīdiyāy** – Oh one who has the nature of removing my sins which have been there since time immemorial! The implied meaning is that apart from being beautiful and the ordained lord, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is also the benefactor who removes the enemies (in this case, sins).

**niṟcārndu ninṟu** – holding on to you.

We will move on to the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 35. Ninrum irundum

*ninṟum irundum kidandum tiridandum onṟumōvāṟṟān ennenjagalān anṟangai vanpudaiyāl pon peyarōn vāy tagarndu mārvidandān anbudaiyananṟē avan*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninṟum irundum kidandum tiridandum – standing, sitting, reclining and walking (in various divine abodes in order to attain me); onṟum āṟṟān – he remains as if he has not done anything; en nenju agalān – he will not leave my heart; anṟu – during that time when he incarnated as narasimha (half lion – half human); am kai van pudaiyāl – due to the hard blow (given) with the beautiful hand; pon peyarōn vāy tagarttu – crushed the mouth of demon hiraṇya kaśyap; mārvu idandān – tore the chest; avan – that emperumān; anbudaiyan anṟē – is he not loving (towards his followers)?; O – how amazing!

**Перевод:**

**ninṟum irundum kidandum tiridandum – the reason for emperumān taking residence in various abodes in standing, sitting, reclining and walking postures is only to attain me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about how he lent his ears for emperumān. In this pāsuram āḻvār explains as to what all emperumān undergoes at the hands of such a person. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**onṟum āṟṟān** – even though he does everything, he thinks that he is not doing anything. The long syllable O (in *onrumō*) denotes amazement.

**en nenju agalān** – he does not leave my heart as if thinks that it is all wild fire outside my heart.

Who is such an entity?

**anṟangai vanpudaiyāl ponpeyarōn vāy tagarndu mārvidandan** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has such soft hands that if he touches pirātti with his hands, there will be a scar on his hands. With such soft hands, when his follower prahlāda’s vow “*kaṇṇan is present everywhere*” was denied by the rude hiraṇya kaśyap, emperumān crushed the mouth of hiraṇya and tore his heart. īsn’t that emperumān, full of love? Does emperumān, who, when the father (hiraṇya) himself became inimical, came as a closer relative than that and helped prahlāda, have affection or do we, who tried to move away from him when he approached, saying that we are not suitable, have affection?

We will take up the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 36. Avanam ivanam

*avanām ivanām uvanām maṟṟumbar avanām avan enṟirādē avanām avanē enatteḷindu kaṇṇanukkē tīrndāl avanē evanēlum ām*

**Пословный перевод:**

avan – that emperumān; avanām – would he be difficult to attain?; ivanām – will he be easy to attain?; uvanām – would he be both?; umbar avanām – residing in some place (above all); enṟu irādē – not doubting; avan avanē Am enat teḷindu – having clarity that he is dependent on his followers; kaṇṇanukkē tīrndāl – if one is servile to him who incarnated as kriṣṇa; avanē evanēlum Am – that kaṇṇan will be protector in all ways

**Перевод:**

**avan avanām – would emperumān be the lord of tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) and be difficult to approach?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that when emperumān is so affectionate towards us, we should not have any doubt about him and step back. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ivanām** – would be in the state of arccai (in idol form) and be simple to approach?

**uvanām** – would he be in the form of *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul), being accessible easily for *yōgis* (ascetics) and difficult for the others? Since the world *avan* refers to someone at a distance, the meaning translates into one who is difficult to approach. In the same way *ivan* refers to someone who is close at hand and hence refers to one who is easy to approach, and *uvan* refers to someone who is in between the two and thus will be both easy and difficult to approach.

**maṟṟu umbar avanām** – will he be difficult to attain, being the lord of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)?

**enṟirādē** – instead of doubting him like this and moving away from him. Since āḻvār has mercifully added he word *enṟirādē* the words *avanām ivanām uvanām* are not assertive words (where they are statements) but interrogative words, with expression of doubt.

**avanām avanē enat teḷindu kaṇṇanukkē tīrndāl** – Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 111-7 “*bharatasya vaca: kurvan yācamānasya rāghava* I *ātmānam nātivartētā: satyadarma parākrama* īī” (Oh rāghava! Oh one who has true righteousness and valour! By doing as per bharata who is beseeching you, you may refrain from going past your basic nature of being dependent on your followers), being clear that dependence on followers is his basic nature which he cannot give up, if one were servile to kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who is easy to approach for followers…

**kaṇṇanukkē tīrndāl** – being servile to kaṇṇa who was such a simple person for a covherd lady (yaśōdā) whom he allowed to tie him up and to beat him.

**avanē evanēlum Am** – Just as it is mentioned in mahābhāratam vanaparvam 49-20 sanjaya vacanam “*yasya mantrī ca gōptā ca suhrudacaiva janārdana:* I *haristrailōkyanātassa kinnutasya na nirjitam* īī” (when that bhagavān, who is the lord of three worlds, who is called as janārdana and hari, is the minister, protector and friend of darmaputra, which is that which cannot be worn over by that darmaputra?) that emperumān will himself be all forms of relations and will protect his followers in all ways.

We will take up the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 37. Amaru arivudaiyar

*āmāṟu aṟivudaiyār āvadu aridanṟē nāmē aduvudaiyōm nannenjē pūmēy madu karamē taṇḍuḻāy mālārai vāḻttām adukaramē anbāl amai*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nenjē – Oh favourable heart!; āṟu Am aṟivu – intelligence which goes in the correct path; udaiyār āvadu aṟidnanṟē – is it not difficult to have \[such intelligence\]?; nāmē adu udaiyōm – we both \[āḻvār and his heart\] have that knowledge; pū mēy madu karam mē – having beetles which are engaging with flowers; taṇ tuḻāy mālārai – one who is superior to all and who has cool tuḷasi garland; anbāl – with affection; vāḻttu Am adu – singing praises on him, that itself; karamē amai – engage firmly

**Перевод:**

**āmāṟu aṟivudaiyār āvadu aridanṟē – The words should be rearranged as āṟu Am aṟivudaiyār to get the correct meaning. The meaning is – is it not difficult to get knowledge which goes in the correct path? The opinion is that it is very difficult to see people in the world with such knowledge.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “being servile only to emperumān is very difficult for others; it happened for us”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nāmē adu udaiyōm** – we have got such rare-to-get knowledge.

**nal nenjē** – are you not one with such greatness! (1. As a heart; 2. With the same opinion). Just as it is mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-28 “*mana Eva manuśyāṇām kāraṇam banda mōkśayō:* I   *bandāya viśayāsangi muktyai nirviśayam mana:* īī” (It is only the mind which is the reason for manuśyas (human beings) to be bound in samsāram or to be liberated from it. That mind which is engaged with worldly pursuits becomes the reason for bondage with samsāram and one which does not engage with worldly pursuits becomes the reason for being liberated from it), is it not mind which is the reason for both bondage with and liberation from samsāram?

**pū mēy madu karamē taṇ tuḻāy mālārai anbāl vāḻttu Am adu karamē amai** – \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart\] Be firmly engaged with affection, in praising the supreme entity who is having tuḷasi garland, on which beetles are swarming. The word *karam* is a variant of a samskruta word *kharam* and refers to being firm.

We will take up the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 38. Amaikkum poludunde

*amaikkum poḻuduṇḍē ārāyil nenjē imaikkum poḻudum idaicci kumaittiṟangaḷ ēsiyē āyinum īntuḻāy māyanaiyē pēsiyē pōkkāy piḻai*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh heart!; ārāyil – if we analyse; imaikkum poḻudum – even during the time of winking; amaikkum poḻuduṇḍē – do we have the time to keep quiet?; īdaicci – the covherd lady, yaśōdā; kumai tiṟangaḷ – the ways in which she punished (kriṣṇa); ēsiyē āyinum – even if you speak abusively; In tuḻāy māyanaiyē – the amazing entity having tuḷasi garland; pēsiyē pōkkāy – you are not passing your time by meditating on him; piḻai – (what you are doing is) incorrect

**Перевод:**

**imaikkum poḻudum amaikkum poḻuduṇḍē ārāyil nenjē – instead of looking superficially, if you were to analyse deeply, is there any place for not praising emperumān even for a moment?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, when āḻvār asked his heart to praise emperumān, it stepped back thinking of its lowliness. āḻvār tells it “What you are doing is wrong. You must spend your time, only speaking about him”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

When his heart tells him that only those who go to paramapadam and see him could enjoy him continuously, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) responds . . .

**idaicci kumaittiṟangaḷ ēsiyē āyinum In tuḻāy māyanaiyē pēsiyē pōkkāy** – there is no need to go to paramapadam. When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) incarnated as kriṣṇa, as one among cētanas, his mother yaśōdā, in her right that he is “my son”, had tied him to a mortar and had beaten him. By speaking about such activities, you could spend your time speaking about the amazing entity emperumān who wears sweet tuḷasi garland. But you are not doing that way.

**piḻai** – this is incorrect. Alternatively we can construe the meaning for this as – by speaking about the amazing entity with sweet tuḷasi garland, you can get rid of the wrongs, viz., sins. If we speak without love about the actions which yaśōdā carried out towards him with affection, will not become an abuse for him?

**ēsiyē āyinum pēsiyē pōkkāy piḻai** – even if it ends up as an abuse, not speaking about him is not correct.

We will take up the 39<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 39. Pilaikka muyanromo

*piḻaikka muyanṟōmō nenjamē pēsāi taḻaikkum tuḻāy mārvan tannai aḻaittorugāl pōyupagāram poliyakkoḷḷādu avan pugaḻē vāyupagāram koṇḍa vāyppu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjamē – Oh mind!; taḻaikkum – sprouting; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; mārvan tannai – one who has it on his divine chest; aḻaittu – calling (him); oru kāl – at a point of time; pōy – going to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); poliya upagāram koḷḷādu – not taking lot of favours; avan pugaḻē – his auspicious qualities; vāyupagāram koṇḍa vāyppu – this activity which is recognised as a beneficial act for the mouth; piḻaikka muyanṟōmō – did we miss out on it?; pēsāi – please tell

**Перевод:**

**taḻaikkum tuḻāy mārvan  tannai – even if a withered tuḷasi garland is applied on emperumān, it will sprout afresh once it comes into contact with his divine form. Such emperumān who has sprouted tuḷasi garland . . .**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Just as āḻvār had ordained it, his heart set out to speak about emperumān and this was very sweet for the heart. āḻvār tells it “Oh heart! Have you seen that there is nothing wrong in what I had told you!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aḻaittu oru nāḷ pōy upagāram poliyakkoḷḷādu** – Instead of saying “Once that emperumān comes after he is called, I will make you \[āḻvār’s heart\] reach śrīvaikuṇṭam with the grace of that emperumān and enjoy the unlimited bliss there”

**avan pugaḻē vāy upagāram koṇḍa vāyppu piḻaikka muyanṟōmō** – will this activity of praising his qualities as a result of having been provided with a mouth, be incorrect?

**nenjamē pēsāi** – āḻvār tells his heart “You please tell”. The meaning implied here is that once knowledge about going in the right path regarding matters related to emperumān is obtained, saying “I will carry out kainkaryam after reaching śrīvaikuṇṭam once this body is shed” is equivalent to not being involved with emperumān.

We will move on to the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 40. Vayppo idu oppa

*vāyppō idu oppa maṟṟillai vā nenjē pōyppōy vennaragil pūviyēl tīppāla pēyttāy uyir kalāyppāluṇḍu avaḷuyirai māyttānai vāḻttē vali*

**Пословный перевод:**

vā nenjē – Oh heart! Come \[and hear me\]; idu oppa – equivalent to this; vāyppu – opportunity; maṟṟu illai – there is none; pōyppōy – separating a great deal; vennaragil – in the cruel hell (of separation); pūviyēl – do not make me enter; tīppāla – one who had cruel nature; pēy tāy – the demon pūtanā, who had the form of a loving mother; uyir – (her) life; pāl kalāy uṇḍu – drinking after mixing it with milk; avaḷ uyirai māyttānai – one who destroyed her life; vāḻttē – praising; vali – is strength (for us)

**Перевод:**

**vāyppō idu oppa maṟṟillai vā nenjē– Oh heart! Come and hear me. There is no other opportunity like this to speak about him and spend the time purposefully.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In order to ensure that his heart does not step back again, thinking of its unsuitability, āḻvār tells his heart “Do not make an attempt at going away from emperumān again, when you have an opportunity to speak about him and spend your time purposefully”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pōyppōy vennargail pūviyēl** – In line with your nature, do not think of your unsuitability (for being with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and go away from emperumān which is equivalent to pushing me into cruel hell. Did not sītā pirātti too mercifully say in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 30-18 “*yas tvayā saha sa svargō nirayō yastvayā vinā* I *iti jānan parām prītim gacca rāma mayā saha* īī” (Oh śrī rāma, my benefactor! Being with you only is heaven for me. Being separated from you only is hell for me. Knowing (my) great love like this, come with me (to the forest))!

**tīppāla pēyttāy uyir kalāyppāluṇḍu aval uyirai māyttānai vāḻttuvadē vali** – our strength is only to praise him \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who mixed the life of cruel pūtanā with her milk, which she offered when she came in the form of his mother, and destroyed her life.

**vāḻttē vali** – we can also construe this to mean – be courageous enough to praise.

We will take up the 41<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 41. Valiyam ena ninaindu

*valiyam ena ninaindu vandedirnda mallar valiya mudi idiya vāngi valiya nin ponnāḻikkaiyyāl pudaittidudi kīḷādē pannāḷum niṟkum ippār*

**Пословный перевод:**

valiyam ena ninaindu – thinking that only we are strong; vandu – coming (to fight); edirnda – opposing; mallar – wrestlers (by the names of cāṇūra and muśtika); valiya mudi – powerful heads; idiya – falling as if struck by lightning; vāngi – seizing; pon āḻi – donning the beautiful disc; un – your; valiya kaiyāl – powerful divine hands; kīḷādē – instead of tearing; pudaittidudi – you beat them (till the hands started aching); ippār pal nāḷum niṟkum – this earth stabilised for a long time

**Перевод:**

**valiyam ena ninaindu vandu edirnda mallar valiya mudi idiya vāngi valiya nin pon āḻikkaiyāl pudaittidudi kīḷādē – The wrestlers cāṇūra and muśtika thought that they had sufficient strength to defeat kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) and came to wrestle with him. But you (emperumān) seized their powerful heads such that they fell down as if struck by lightning, with your powerful divine hand which dons the divine disc. Instead of tearing their mouths just like you did with the demon kēśi, you beat them up in line with wrestling bout.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Once āḻvār mentioned about pūtanā in the previous pāsuram, he continues about emperumān destroying the wrestlers in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pannāḷum niṟkum ippār** – This earth will bear witness to your victory over the rude wrestlers, for a long time. The word *niṟkum* refers to the earth remaining as a witness. The word *pār* refers to people on the earth. Alternative meaning: Is it not because of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s victor over the wrestlers that this earth is remaining stable! Otherwise, it would have got destroyed. ōr, we can rearrange the words to read *kīḷādē pannāḷum niṟkum ippār* which would mean – after seeing your wrestling with the rude wrestlers, with your soft, divine hands, śouldn’t those living on this earth have lost their lives? Since they are sustaining themselves even after seeing this, their longevity is indeed lengthy. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam 103-5 “*bhūmau stitasya rāmasya ratastasya ca rākśasa:* I *na samam yuddamityāhur dēva gandarva dānavā:* īī” (celestial entities and demonic entities said that the fight between rāma who is standing on earth and the demon who is on his chariot is not fair), the opinion here is that those who saw the fight between the rude wrestlers and the childish kaṇṇa without any fear, will never face death. Here, the word *pār* refers to people on earth. *kīḷādē niṟkum* refers to being stable without any destruction.

We will move on to the 42<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 42. Par undan

*pāruṇḍān pārumiḻndān pāridandān pāraḷandān pāridam mun padaittān enbarāl pāridam āvānum tānānāl āridamē maṟṟoruvrkku āvān pugāvā avai*

**Пословный перевод:**

pār uṇḍān – (emperumān) swallowed the earth; pār umiḻndān – he spat out the earth; pār idandān – he dug out the earth; pār aḷandān – he measured the entire worlds (with his divine feet); pār idam mun padaittān – he created all these worlds during the time of great deluge; enbar – (knowledgeable people) will say; pār idam āvānum tān – it is only he who becomes this expansive world; ānāl – if this were the case; Ar idamē – who do these places (in the worlds) belong to?; avai – those worlds; maṟṟu oruvarkku āvān pugā – will not be subservient to anyone else

**Перевод:**

**pār uṇḍān – during the time of deluge, he protected by keeping in his stomach so that the worlds will not suffer in the deluge.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that earth stabilised because of emperumān. In this pāsuram, he says “Is this the only one activity \[by which he showed that he is the protector\]? Are there not many ways by which he protected the earth? Who else can be the refuge for this earth other than such emperumān? ” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pār umiḻndān** – later, he let out the worlds which he had swallowed.

**pār idandān** – when hiraṇyākśan (a demon) hid the world under water, he took the form of a great boar and dug out the world from the walls of universe and protected it.

**pār aḷandān** – when mahābali seized all the three worlds from indra, he took the form of trivikrama and measured the worlds with his divine feet, redeeming the worlds from mahābali and giving them back to indra, thus protecting them.

**pār idam mun padaittān** – when the cētana (sentient) entities were without their physical forms and senses, and thus were ineligible to experience the worldly pleasures or to seek liberation, he created them in this world during the time of creation so that they would become eligible and thus protected them.

**enbarāl** – authentic texts, which do not have any faults in them, and the sages who accepted these texts say that it was only the supreme being who carried out such great tasks which could not have been carried out by anyone else.

**pār idam āvānum tān** – by protecting through all these activities, by having all the worlds as his physical form and being their indvelling soul, it is only he who is all the worlds. Since he is all the worlds, could one say that the worlds and he have become unified in their *svarūpa* (basic nature) by their communion? Since it is not possible to have unification due to faults in the worlds, since he is the protector starting with the activity *pār uṇḍān* (swallowed the world), since he is the cause, since  he is the one who pervades, since the world is protected by him, since the world is the effect of his activity, since it is pervaded by him, and since relationships such as protector – protected, cause – effect, one who pervades and one who is pervaded, *śarīra* – *śarīri* (body and soul) have been shown for saying that the world is one with him, it is not possible to have unification in *svarūpam.* Did not vēda (sacred text) also say that the entire world is only *brahmam* (supreme being) and that the reason for this is the cause-effect and *śarīra – śarīri* relationship, as mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-1 “*sarvam kalvidam brahma tajjalānidi śānta upāsīta*” (let him worship by remaining peaceful (thinking) that since it is generated from brahmam, it ends with brahmam and it sustains due to brahmam, all these are brahmam)?

**ānāl āridamē** – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is everything for the worlds, who is the refuge for the worlds?

**maṟṟoruvarkku āvān pugā avai** – the sentient entities in the world cannot become anyone else’s possession. The word *avai* refers to the sentient entities who are in the world. Alternatively, we can say that the activities referred to by words such as *uṇḍān, umiḻndān* etc cannot be applicable to anyone else. The word *Al* is filler without any significant meaning.

We will move on to the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.
