## 43. Avayam ena

*avayam ena ninaindu vanda surar pālē navaiyai naḷirvippān tannai kavaiyil manattuyarai vaittirundu vāḻttādārkkuṇḍō manattuyarai māykkum vagai*

**Пословный перевод:**

avayam ena ninaindu – thinking (of you) as the refuge; vanda surar pālē – with the dēvatas (celestial entities) who came; navaiyai – sorrows; naḷirvippān tannai – sarvēśvaran who made (the sorrows) to tremble; kavai il manattu – in the mind without any doubt; uyara vaittu – holding aloft; irundu – being stable; vāḻttādārkku – those who do not praise; manam tuyarai māykkum vagai uṇḍō – is there any way by which they can remove mental agonies?

**Перевод:**

**avayam ena ninaindu vanda surar pālē navayai naḷirvippān tannai – Just as it is said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-35 “yadāvat katitō dēvair brahmā prāha tata: surān I parāvarēśam śaraṇam vrajadvamasurārdanam īī” (brahmā, who was well informed by dēvas (celestial entities) of their troubles, told them “surrender to bhagavān who is the lord for all without any distinction between lofty and lowly people and who is the enemy for demons”), emperumān is one who will make the troubles of dēvas, who approach him saying abhayam (refuge,) to tremble and disappear. The word navai refers to troubles; this could also be taken to refer to sins which are the cause of troubles.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān is one who removes hurdles of cētanas (sentient entities). āḻvār says that for those who do not attain such emperumān, it is not possible to overcome the mental agonies which they suffer in this world. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kavaiyil manattu uyara vaittirundu vāḻttādārkku uṇḍo manattuyarai māykkum vagai** – Is there any way by which mental agonies could be removed for those who do not keep [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in their doubtless minds in a lofty place and who do not praise him? The word *kavai* refers to doubt – should we hold on to sensory perceptions such as *śabda* (sound) etc or should we hold on to emperumān? ōr it could refer to the doubt as to whether we should move away from emperumān since we do not have the necessary quality to be with him or should we not leave emperumān. Keeping emperumān in a lofty place in the mind would mean that we think of him as our means, our goal, our sustenance, our nourishment and our enjoyment.

We will take up the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 44. Vagai sernda

*vagai sērnda nannenjum nāvudaiya vāyum miga vāyndu vīḻāvenilum miga āyndu mālaittām vāḻttādiruppar iduvanṟē mēlaittām seyyum vinai*

**Пословный перевод:**

vagai sērnda – that which has come as the path (for manifesting knowledge); nal nenjum – superior mind; nāvu udaiya – having tongue; vāyum – mouth too; miga vāyndu – involving deeply; vīḻā enilum – even if they do not attain (emperumān); miga āyndu – taking lot of troubles; mālai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all); tām vāḻttādiruppār – samsāris will not praise emperumān; mēlai – since time immemorial; tām seyyum vinai – the result of their sins; idu anṟē – is it not this?

**Перевод:**

**For this pāsuram, bhattar (parāsara bhattar, son of śrī kūrattāḻvān) and piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar would give different interpretations.  bhattar’s interpretation is described first:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, there was a reference to troubles with which samsāris (dwellers of the materialistic world) toil without praising emperumān. As a corollary for that, āḻvār says in this pāsuram that this is the result of their sins. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vagai sērnda nal nenjum** – the superior mind which is the gate for *jñānam* (knowledge) to be manifested for the cētana (sentient entity) who is with his physical body.

**nāvudaiya vāyum** – the tongue which has been created along with the mouth in order to praise [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**miga vāyndu vīḻā enilum** – even though they (sentient entities) do not get much involved with emperumān since among the three qualities of *satva* (pure goodness), *rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance) which they are controlled by, *rajas* and *tamas* overshadow *satva.*

**miga āyndu mālaittām vāḻttādiruppar** – Just as it is mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 14-17](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/14-17/) “*satvāt sanjāyatē jñānam rajasō lōbha Eva ca* I *pramādamōhau tamasō bhavatō’jñāna mēvaca* īī” (jñānam (knowledge) results from satvam (purely good qualities); desire (in svargam/heaven etc) comes out of rajas; involvement in insentient deeds (not ordained for us), crooked knowledge and ignorance which arise out of carelessness, come from tamas), the knowledgeable sentient entities should push out qualities of rajas and tamas to the background and through their sātvika āhāram (proper food intake which is prescribed for us) and connection with sātvikas (those who have predominance of purely good qualities), praise sarvēśvaran (supreme being). But most of the cētanas do not do this.

**iduvanṟē mēlaittām seyyum vinai** – isn’t this the sinful deed which they carry out which makes them to remain as samsāris (dwellers of materialistic world) in the times to come? *mēlai* – times to come.

piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar’s interpretation is described next:

**vagai sērnda nal nenjum nāvudaiya vāyum** – the superior mind which engages with one specific quality of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s and the tongue which praises that quality.

**miga vāyndu vīḻā enilum** – even though the mind and tongue do not engage with other qualities of emperumān’s.

**miga āyndu mālait tām vāḻttādiruppar** – the knowledgeable cētana should analyse all the qualities of emperumān’s and praise him. But, for most, it is not possible to praise emperumān like this.

**iduvanṟē mēlait tām seyyum vinai** – it is due to the result of sins which we have committed in the previous births that the mind and tongue are not engaging with all the qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s.

In [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)’s interpretation, the term *vinai* refers to the sinful deed of not praising emperumān. In tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar’s interpretation, this term refers to the result of sins committed in earlier times.

We will move on to the 45<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 45. Vinaiyar tara muyalum

*vinaiyār tara muyalum vemmaiyai(yē) anjit tinaiyām siṟidaḷavum sella ninaiyādu vāsagattāl ēttinēn vānōr toḻudiṟainjum nāyagattān ponnadikkaḷ nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

vinaiyār – separation (from emperumān); tara muyalum – attempting to give; vemmaiyai anji – fearing, thinking of the sorrows; tinaiyām siṟidu aḷavum – even the shortest of moments; sella ninaiyādu – not thinking of spending time without him; nān – I; vānōr – nityasūris; toḻudu iṟainjum – vorśipping through the faculties of mind and speech; nāyagattān – svāmy’s (lord’s); pon adigaḷ – beautiful divine feet; vāsagattāḷ ēttinēn – vorśipped through mouth

**Перевод:**

**vinaiyār tara muyalum vemmayai anji tinaiyām siṟidaḷavum sella ninaiyādu vāsagattāl ēttinēn – separation from emperumān results from sins committed earlier. Fearing such separation, thinking of not separating from him even for the shortest of moments and spending that time \[purposelessly\], I vorśipped him with the sensory perception of speech. Since āḻvār has already said “ingillai” (pāsuram 30) and since he is going to say further (in pāsuram 54) “vānō maṟi kadalō” wherein he says that sins have already left him, the term vinayār here will not refer to sins. It will only refer to separation (from emperumān) resulting from previous deeds. The term sella ninaiyādu will refer to not thinking of spending time after separating from him. The term vāsagam would refer to the sense of speech.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that only due to the effect of their sins in previous births, do samsāris not engage with emperumān. When the question arose as to what he did, āḻvār says “thinking of the sorrow which entails separation from emperumān, I praised his divine feet”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

What did he worship? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) responds saying . . . .

**vānōr toḻudu iṟainjum nāyagattān ponnadikkaḷ nān** – I vorśipped the great, divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which are served constantly by nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) through their faculties of mind, speech and body, with the thought that I should never let go of them ever. The term *toḻudu* refers to vorśipping with the faculties of mind and body while the term *iṟainji* refers to vorśipping through the faculty of speech. The word *ponnadikkaḷ* refers to the divine feet \[of emperumān\] which are both the means and the goal.

We will move on to the 46<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 46. Nan kurum

*nān kūṟum kūṟṟāvadu ittanaiyē nāṇāḷum tēngōda nīruruvan sengaṇmāl nīngāda māgadiyām vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku nīgadiyā nenjē ninai*

**Пословный перевод:**

tēngu ōda nīr uruvan – one who has the colour of the stagnant ocean; sem kaṇ māl – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who has reddish eyes; nīngāda māgadiyām – is the final goal who does not (make us) return; vem naragil – in the cruel samsāram (materialistic realm); sērāmal – so as not to fall; kāppadaṟku – to protect; gadiyām – he is also the means; nenjē – Oh heart!; nī – you; ninai – think (about this); nān – I (who knew this); nāl nāḷum – every day; kūṟum kūṟṟāvadu – the words which are said; ittanaiyē – are only this much

**Перевод:**

**nān kūṟum kūṟṟāvadu ittanaiyē – All the words that I say are only these.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that he considered vorśipping emperumān as a way of spending his time. In this pāsuram, he tells his heart to consider emperumān as the means and the goal. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Are these said on one day, on one occasion?

**nāṇāḷum** – on every day; all the time, on all occasions, these are all that I say.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) describes further as to what he says every day.

**tēngu ōdam nīr uruvan sem kaṇ māl nīngāda māgadiyām** – he, who has the complexion of a stagnant ocean and who has the eyes which reveal that he is the lord of all, is the goal who does not send us back. The term *nīngāda* refers to the quality by which, unlike the benefits in samsāram, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) does not let us to return \[once we attain him\].

**vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku gadiyām** – He is also the means for protecting us from falling into the cruel hell called as samsāram.

**nenjē nī ninai** – Oh heart! You think about this. We can rearrange the words to form the verse *nīngāda māgadiyā vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku gadiyā nī ninai* to get the meaning – Oh heart! Think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as the end goal, who does not allow us to return \[to samsāram\] and who is the means for preventing us from falling into the cruel hell called as samsāram. In both interpretations, the considered meaning is that emperumān is both the means and the benefit.

**ninai** – For the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (soul) who has to attain emperumān, the only activity to be performed is to think of emperumān as both the means and the goal. The implied meaning is that just as it is mentioned in śrī rāma carama ślōkam (the ultimate ślōkam given by śrī rāma) *sakrudēva prapannāya* (for the sake of one who surrenders to me only once), beyond thinking once \[that he is the means and goal\], there is nothing further to be done.

We will take up the 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 47. Ninaittirainji

*ninaittiṟainji mānidavar onṟirappar enṟē ninaittidavum vēṇḍā nī nērē ninaittiṟainja evvaḷavar evvidattōr mālē adu tānum evvaḷavum uṇḍō emakku*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālē – Oh sarvēśvara! (who cannot be estimated); mānidar – manuśyas (cētanas or samsāris, dwellers of the materialistic realm); ninaittu – thinking; iṟainji – vorśipping; onṟu irappar – will seek a small benefit; enṟu – saying like this; nī – you; ninaittidavum vēṇḍā – do not think; nērē ninaittu – thinking (of you), as you are; iṟainja – to worship; evvaḷavar – how much knowledge do they (samsāris) have?; evvidattār – in which place are they?; adu tānum – that too (seeking other benefits); evvaḷavum – even a little bit; emakku uṇḍō – do we (who have your grace), have?

**Перевод:**

**ninaittiṟainji mānidavar onṟu irappar enṟē nī ninaittidavum vēṇḍā – \[āḻvār tells emperumān\] Do not think that other samsāris are “thinking of me, falling at my feet but unlike you \[āḻvār\] they do not ask for me as the benefit; they are asking for lowly benefits”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān tells āḻvār “You are considering me as the means and goal and keep praising me to spend your time. But the other people in samsāram are just the opposite of this” to which āḻvār responds “They are people without your grace. Do not feel bad about this”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nērē ninaittiṟainja evvaḷavar evvidattōr** – who can think clearly that you are the natural means and goal? From which place and with what level of knowledge could they think this way? The implied meaning here is that only nityars (permanent dwellers of paramapadam) and muktars (those who have got liberated from samsāram and have attained paramapadam) could think this way.

**mālē** – Oh the great entity! The opinion is that those with very low levels of knowledge cannot think of a great entity such as emperumān and arrive at a conclusion that emperumān is like this or that these are his qualities.

**adu tānum evvaḷavum uṇḍō emakku** – Do I have such lowly quality that these samsāris have, of seeking other benefits from you? For persons like me who have your grace, there is no connection with other lowly benefits. It is no wonder that other samsāris, who do not have your blessing, ask for other benefits from you. The opinion is that emperumān cannot dislike them because of this.

We will take up the 48<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 48. Emakkiyam vinnattukku

*emakkiyām viṇṇāttukku uccamadām vīttaiamaittirundōm ahtanrē yāmāṟu amaippolindamentōḷi kāraṇamā vengōttu ēṟēḻudanēkonṟānaiyē manattuk koṇḍu*

**Пословный перевод:**

amai polinda – being like a bamboo; mentōḷi kāraṇamā – nappinnai pirātti (śrī neeḷā dēvi) who has soft shoulders; vem kōdu – sharp horns; ēḻu ēṟu – seven bulls; udanē – simultaneously; konṟānaiyē – kriṣṇa, who killed; manattuk koṇḍu – keeping in the mind; nām – we; viṇṇāttukku uccamadām vīttai – paramapadam which is above svarga (heaven) etc; emakku amaittirundōm – we were desirous; ahdanṟē āmāṟu – isn’t that, the correct way?

**Перевод:**

**emakkuyām viṇṇāttukku uccamadām vīttai amaittirundōm – We had desired for us, paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is above the worlds occupied by brahmā, rudra, indra et al.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says “We were thinking that we will have emperumān, who is capable of removing our strong hurdles, as the means and will attain paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) through him. īsn’t this the way that we should be?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ahdanṟē āmāṟu** – īsn’t that the way that we should be?

When asked “There are many hurdles to attain paramapadam. How will you overcome these?”  
[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says further “He, who removed the hurdles which prevented him from attaining nappinnaip pirātti, will remove those hurdles”.

**amaippolinda mentōḷi kāraṇamā vengōttu ēṟēḻudanē konṟānaiyē manattukkoṇḍu** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that since he has kept in his mind kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who killed seven bulls which had sharp horns, simultaneously, for the sake of nappinnaip pirātti, who has soft shoulders resembling bamboo, his hurdles would suffer the same fate which those bulls suffered.

Here, the word *amaivu* \[as in the first verse *amaittirundōm*\] comes in the meaning of ‘desire’ with the variant *samaivu*. Since the word *amaiyum* would mean “not required”, the word *amaittirundōm* would mean ‘we did not need it’. This would convey the meaning that we did not require *kaivailya mōkśam* (a variant of liberation in which the ātmā (soul), instead of enjoying [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), enjoys itself) which is located in the outskirts of paramapadam and which is above the worlds of brahmā, rudra, indra et al. The implied meaning is that for us, who have thought of the consort of nappinnai, kaivalya mōkśam, which excludes experience of emperumān, is not required.

Instead of this, we could also interpret the meaning in another way – we can consider *viṇṇāttukku uccamadām vīttai* to refer to paramapadam itself. However, just as hanuman had said “Other than this physical form of a monkey which was embraced by śrī rāma, in whose auspicious qualities I had got engaged, with his divine form, I do not require the *aprākruta* (not related to primordial matter) form with which I have to enter paramapadam as there is nothing in paramapadam for me to enjoy”, we can consider that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says “For me who has kept firmly in the mind nappinnaip pirātti’s consort kaṇṇa, the experience of paramapadam is not required ”. Did not toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār too say in tirumalai 2 *“iccuvaitavira yān pōy indiralōgam āḷum accuvai perineum vēṇḍēn aranga mānagaruḷānē*” (I will not like if I have to leave this experience of enjoying tiruvarangam even if I were to get the opportunity of going to paramapadam)!

We will take up the 49<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 49. Kondaldan malvaraidan

*koṇḍaldān mālvaraidān mākadaldān kūriruḷdānvaṇḍaṟāp pūvaidān maṟṟuttān kaṇḍanāḷkāruruvam kāṇḍōṟu(m) nenjōdum kaṇṇanārpēruruvenṟu emmaip pirindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

koṇḍaldān – the clouds; mālvaraidān – the huge mountains; mākadaldān – the vast oceans; kūriruḷdān – the dense darkness; vaṇḍu aṟā pūvaidān – the pūvai tree (bilberry) from whose flowers the beetles will never leave; maṟṟum kār uruvam tān – other objects which are dark in complexion; kaṇḍa nāḷ – on days when they are seen; kāṇḍhōṟum – whenever they are seen; nenju – my heart; kaṇṇanār pēr uru enṟu – thinking (this is) kaṇṇa’s (kriṣṇa’s) huge physical form; emmai – us; pirindu ōdum vittu – will run (towards him), leaving \[us\]

**Перевод:**

**koṇḍaldān mālvaraidān mākadaldān kūriruḷdān vaṇḍaṟāp pūvaidān kāṇḍhōṟu – on seeing clouds, huge mountains, vast oceans, dense darkness, pūvai tree which has flowers from which beetles will not leave, all of which are similes \[for emperumān\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār ascertained that emperumān is the means who will remove our hurdles and grant us paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). In this pāsuram, he gives out the way by which those who are with the firm belief \[that emperumān is the means\] have to follow till their physical bodies fall, to spend their time. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**maṟṟu kāruruvamdān kaṇḍa nāḷ** – when looking at dark pūnguvaḷai (a type of dark coloured flower), neelamalar (blue coloured flower), kāyāmalar (purple coloured flower), which have not been mentioned earlier \[as similes for emperumān\]

**kāṇḍhōṟum** – every time that they are seen

**kāṇḍhōṟum nenjōdum** – whenever any dark coloured object like the above is seen, even when others tell “this is not emperumān” , my heart runs away from me, towards such objects

**kaṇṇanār pēruruvenṟu** – not realising that these are very small objects and cannot contain his huge form, my heart runs.

**emmaip pirindu nenjōdum** – leaving me, the heart runs towards them, thinking that they are all kaṇṇan’s divine form.

We will take up the 50<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 50. Pirindonru nokkadu

*pirindonṟu nōkkādu tammudaiya pinnē tirinduḻalum sindainaiyār tammaip purindorugāl āvā ena irangār andō validē kol māvāy piḷandār manam*

**Пословный перевод:**

pirindu – other than self \[emperumān\]; onṟu nōkkādu – not thinking of anything else; tammudaiya pinnē – going behind him; tirindu uḻalum – roaming, and tottering; sindanaiyār tammai – my mind; oru kāl – even once; purindu – showering mercy; A A \[āvā\] ena – saying “Oh my! How sad!”; irangār – he is not showing any consideration; andō – Oh my!; mā vāy piḷandār manam – the mind of emperumān who tore the mouth of demon kēśi, who came in the form of a horse; validē kol – how did it become so hardened?

**Перевод:**

**pirindu onṟu nōkkādu tammudaiya piṇṇe tirindu uḻalum – without thinking of anything else, following only him and tottering**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār loathes in this pāsuram saying “Looking at the similes (cloud, ocean, mountain etc), we are thinking that these are only emperumān. But he is not showering his grace on us ”. Just as āḻvār had mentioned in his tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-6 “edirsūḻal pukku enaittōr piṟappum”, āḻvār is not thinking of the efforts taken by emperumān to obtain āḻvār whenever he had taken birth in this samsāram. With the (physical) body given by emperumān and after developing a desire in emperumān due to the efforts put in by emperumān, even if there is a slight delay on the part of emperumān \[in coming to the cētana\],  that appears like a fault for āḻvār. He is not considering the toils of emperumān when he had come after āḻvār wondering if he would get involved with him during each of his earlier births. The saying in śāstras that when a cētana (sentient entity) gets involved with emperumān and if emperumān does not shower his mercy on the cētana, then it is emperumān’s fault, is explained here. However much troubles a child may give its mother, if the mother does not give food to her child when the child cries for food, then it is said that the mother is at fault. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sindanaiyār tammaip purindu oru kāl A A \[āvā\] ena irangār** – he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] does not show mercy on the heart \[[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s\], by looking at it with affection, thinking “There is none for this heart other than myself”,

**andō validē kol māvāi piḷandār manam** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had killed the demon kēśi for the sake of his followers, in order to rid them of their troubles. How such emperumān’s mind has hardened now! The mind of one, who tore the mouth of a horse for the sake of his followers then, has become like a stone now, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/).

We will take up the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 51. Manamalum oraivar

*manamāḷum ōraivar van kuṟumbar tammaic cinamāḷvittu ōridattē sērttup punamēya taṇ tuḻāyān adiyē tām kāṇum ahdanṟē vaṇṭhuḻām sīrārkku māṇbu*

**Пословный перевод:**

manam āḷum – those who take control of the mind; van kuṟumbar – being powerful and indulging in mischief; ōr aivar tammai – the unique five sensory perceptions; sinam āḷvittu – removing anger (towards them); ōr idatte sērttu – engaging with the incomparable bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to emperumān); punam mēya taṇ tuḻāyān – one who has the cool, comfortable tuḷasai, which blossoms as if it were on its own land, such emperumān’s; adiyē tām kāṇum – the activity of vorśipping the divine feet; ahdanṟē – isn’t that; vaṇ tuḻām sīrārkku – for śrīvaiṣṇavas (devotees of emperumān), who are magnanimous and who have great qualities; māṇbu – beautiful?

**Перевод:**

**manam āḷum –  it appeared as if the lowly people who indulge in mischief, rise in tumult. The ātmā (soul) is expected to be under the control of emperumān; mind is expected to be under the control of ātmā and the sensory perceptions are expected to be under the control of the mind. But this order is contravened and the mind is under the control of sensory perceptions.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār loathed, finding that emperumān had not graced him mercifully. In this pāsuram he says “the possession should accept whatever the possessor does, patiently. It is not correct to speak out in disgust”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**van kuṟumbar** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is referring to the five sensory perceptions as being very powerful and indulging in mischief. He is calling them powerful since they are able to lead the mind in their path.

**van kuṟumbar tammai** – these mischief mongers are like the wicked people who bribe government officials and keep them under their control.

**ōraivar varn kuṟumbar tammai sinam āḷvittu** – removing the anger which rises due to the effect of earlier bad deeds, against these five sensory perceptions. Has it not been said in śrī bhagavat gītā that anger results due to effects of virtuous and vicious deeds when one engages with worldly matters, as mentioned in [2-62](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-62/) “*dyāyatō viśayām pumsas sangas tēśupajāyatē* I *sangāt sanjāyatē kāma: kāmāt krōdō’pijāyatē* īī” (for a person who continuously indulges in worldly matters, a connectivity is created with them; this connectivity creates lust; anger results from such lust)! When sensory perceptions indulge in any matter other than that related to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), they do not get satisfaction however much they enjoy and this results in lust and anger. In the case of matters related to emperumān, even if all the sensory perceptions engage simultaneously, since the part to be enjoyed is huge, such enjoyment will never result in anger. Since indulging in matters related to emperumān reduces the tendency to engage with worldly matters, anger will lessen. Hasn’t [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mercifully mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-8-arivilenukku-arulay/) “*innam keduppāyō*”, in [6-9-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-9-avi-thigaikka/) “*pāviyēnaipala nī kāttip paduppāyō*” and again in [6-9-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-9-avi-thigaikka/) “*siṟṟinbam*” in all of which he says that indulging in worldly matters leads only to earthly pleasures, which lead to more and more of lust and anger, resulting in destruction of ātmā’s nature?

Unlike engagement with such worldly matters which only gives rise to dissatisfaction, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says further that if one engages with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with all sensory perceptions, it will lead to unlimited happiness….

**punamēya taṇ tuḻāyān adiyē** – the divine feet of one who dons the cool, comfortable tuḷasi garland, which blossoms as if it were on its own land.

**vaṇ tuḻām sīrārkku māṇbu – manam āḷum ōr aivar van kuṟumbar tammaic cinam āḷvittu ōr idattē cērttup punamēya taṇ tuḻāyānadiyē tān kāṇum ahdanrē** – the thing of beauty for śrīvaiṣṇavas who are magnanimous and who have expansive qualities is – winning over the wicked sensory perceptions which pull mind in their path, removing the anger which results from involvement with worldly matters, keeping the sensory perceptions with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and seeing the divine feet of such emperumān who dons the cool, comfortable tuḷasi garland. This is also the only correct way. The implied meaning is that loathing as in the previous pāsuram “*andē validē kol*” is not correct.

We will take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 52. Manbhavitta ajnjnanru

*māṇbāvitta ajñjñānṟu maṇṇirandān māyavaḷ nanju ūṇbāvittu uṇḍānadu ōr uruvam kāṇbān nam kaṇṇavā maṟṟonṟu kāṇuṟā sīr paravādu uṇṇavāy tān uṟumō onṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

ajñjñānṟu – during that time; māṇ bāvittu – in the guise of a bachelor; maṇ irandān – sought alms for three steps of land; māyavaḷ nanju – poison (present in the bosom) of the deceitful pūtanā; ūṇ bāvittu – acting as if he is drinking it (desirously); uṇḍānadu – emperumān who drank (both her milk and her life); ōr uruvam – his unique divine form; kāṇbān – only to worship; nam kaṇ avā – desire for our eye; maṟṟū onṟu – anything else; kāṇ uṟā – (it) will not desire to see; vāy tān – mouth; sīr paravādu – without praising (emperumān’s) auspicious qualities; onṟu uṇṇa uṟumō – will it desire to eat anything?

**Перевод:**

**māṇ bāvittu – taking very well, the guise of a bachelor. When emperumān, as vāmana, asked of mahābali to grant him land which would be equivalent to his three steps, mahābali told him “When I am granting you whatever you want, would you ask for such a miniscule object? Ask for more; I will grant you”. vāmana told him “This is all that I need; I do not want anything more than this”. Thus, emperumān did justice to the guise of bachelor that he had taken \[bachelors are expected to be satisfied with small things\]. The word bāvittu would mean assuming on a deceitful role. But this is not applicable here. emperumān donned the role of bachelor, very truly.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says in this pāsuram that whatever he had mentioned in the previous pāsuram that śrīvaṣṇavas should possess, in general, is very much present in him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māyavaḷ nanju ūṇ bāvittu uṇḍān** – Just as pūtanā assumed the guise of a mother and gave him her bosom with the firmness that “I  cannot sustain myself unless I nurture him” emperumān too thought “I cannot sustain myself unless I drink milk from her” and took her milk as his nourishment. nammāḻvār is mercifully mentioning the same meaning which poygai āḻvār had mercifully said in his [mudal tiruvandādi 11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-11/) “*pēymulai nanju ūṇāa uṇḍān*” (emperumān consumed the milk from the demon’s bosom as his nourishment).

**uṇḍānadu ōr uruvam kāṇbān nam kaṇṇavā** – the desire for our eye is to see the divine form of the one who drank milk from the demon’s bosom, in order to sustain himself.

**maṟṟonṟu kāṇuṟā** – When the eye sees his divine form it will see with the certainty “only that divine form should be seen and nothing else”. If it sees any object other than emperumān’s divine form, it will get destroyed, out of shame.

āḻvār says further on that this feature is applicable not only to the sensory perception of vision but also to the sensory perception of speech….

**sīr paravādu uṇṇa vāydān uṟumō onṟu** – Will the mouth attempt to enjoy anything other than speaking about emperumān’s auspicious qualities? No, it will not. If it attempts to enjoy anything else, it will get destroyed, is the implication here.

We will move on to the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 53. Onrundu senganmal

*onṟuṇḍu sengaṇmāl yānuraippa(du) unnadiyārkku enseyvan enṟē irutti nī nin pugaḻil vaigum tam sindaiyilum maṟṟinidō nīyavarkku vaigundam enṟaruḷum vān*

**Пословный перевод:**

sem kaṇ māl – Oh one who has reddish eyes and who is biased towards your followers!; yān uraippadu – what I have to tell (you); onṟu uṇḍu – there is a word; nī – you; un adiyārkku – for those who have love towards you; en seyvan enṟē irutti – you are constantly thinking as to what benefit you could do; maṟṟu avarkku – for them; nin pugaḻil vaigum – being engaged with your auspicious qualities; tam sindaiyilum – more than their mind \[thought\]; vaigundam enṟu nī aruḷum vān – the (huge) paramapadam which you bestow on them; inidō – is it sweeter?

**Перевод:**

**onṟuṇḍu sengaṇmāl yān uraippadu – Oh one who has reddish eyes because you are not satisfied with whatever you do for your followers! There is something which I have to tell you. emperumān tells him “I am supposed to be omniscient. Do you have to tell me anything?” to which āḻvār responds “You are totally perplexed due to your bias towards your followers and you do not know this. Hence I have to tell you this”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār told emperumān “All my sensory perceptions are able to enjoy you now”.  emperumān tells him back “It is not just that I have created an involvement in you \[towards me\], here itself. I have also decided to take you to śrīvaikuṇṭam and enable you to enjoy great services there” to which āḻvār responds “That is not greater than the pleasure that I have here of thinking about you. Thus, you have done everything in this samsāram itself”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

āḻvār explains through the rest of the pāsuram as to what it is . . . . .

**sengaṇmāl un adiyārkku en seyvan enṟē irutti nī** – Oh who has reddish eyes due to your motherly forbearance towards your followers! You are thinking constantly “What should I do for them?” due to your bewilderment, when your followers do not need anything from you. Just as it is mentioned in mahābhāratam uttara parvam 47-22 “*gōvindēti yatākrantat kriṣṇāmām dūravāsinam* I *ruṇam pravruttamiva mē hrudayān nāpasarpatē* īī” (the outcry which darupadi made from far away calling me “*gōvinda! gōvinda!”* does not leave my heart, just as credit keeps increasing (due to interest)), even after the pāṇḍavas got back their kingdom, draupadi had platted her hair \[as a result of the vow when she was insulted\], you felt indebted to draupadi since she called out to you by reciting your *gōvinda nāmam* even though her husbands were very much nearby to protect her! You are constantly thinking “What further could I do” towards such people who are devoted towards you.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) proves that this is due to the perplexity in his mind . . . .

**nin pugaḻil vaigum tam sindaiyilum maṟṟinidō nī avarkku vaigundam enṟu aruḷum vān** – Is the paramapadam which you grant your followers sweeter than their minds which are constantly involved with your auspicious qualities? The implication here is whether what they will get in future (paramapadam) will be a match for what they have now on their hands \[engaging with your auspicious qualities\].

We will take up the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 54. Vano marikadalo

*vānō maṟikadalō mārudamō tīyagamō kānō orungiṟṟuk kaṇḍilamāl ānīnṟa kanṟuyarat tāmeṟindu kāyudirttār tāḷ paṇindōm vantuyarai āvā marungu*

**Пословный перевод:**

An īnṟa kanṟu – vatsāsura who came in the form of a calf which appeared as if it had been given birth to on that day by a cow; tām uyara eṟindju – tossing him high, by emperumān himself; kāy udirttār – one who made kapittāsura, who was in the form of a wood apple, to tumble down \[as a consequence\], his; tāḷ paṇindōm – vorśipped divine feet; van tuyarai – sins which are very strong; marungu kaṇḍilam – we did not see anywhere near us, at all; Al – what a wonder!; vān odungiṟṟō – did they hide in the sky?; maṟi kadal odungiṟṟō – did they dissolve in the ocean with agitating waves?; mārudam orungiṟṟō – did they merge with wind?; tīyagam orungiṟṟō – did they melt in fire?; kān orungiṟṟō – did they hide in the forest?; A A \[āvā\] – Oh my!

**Перевод:**

**vānō maṟi kadalō mārudamō tīyagamō kānō orungiṟṟu – Did my sin merge with the sky? Did it dissolve in the ocean? Did it disappear in the wind? Did it get destroyed, entering fire? Did it proceed towards the forest in order to end its life by facing the northern direction, by the method called as mahāprastān (a procedure by which a person sits facing north, and ends his life, by observing fast)? Where did it go to, among these places?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān asks of āḻvār “You said that only this experience is great. What happened to the sins which were a hurdle for that experience?” āḻvār responds “I have not seen the place where they went to”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**van tuyarai marungum kaṇḍilamāl** – since the sin is not anywhere near us, we have to think of these possibilities.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) explains further on, as to why the sin ran away. . . .

**An īnṟa kanṟu uyarat tām eṟindu kāy udirttār tāḷ paṇindōm** – Manifesting his power of destroying powerful hurdles, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tossed a demon, vatsāsura, who came in the form of a calf as if it had been born on that day, at a wood apple, in which another demon, kapittāsura had pervaded, and brought down both. We vorśipped the divine feet of such emperumān. The implication is that the sins ran away because of this.

**van tuyarai kaṇḍilamāl A A** –  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says that he had not seen the sins anywhere. Where did they disappear? Oh my! He calls sins which are the reason for his *tuyar* (sorrow) as sorrow itself.

We will move on to the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 55. Marungodam modum

*marungōdam mōdum maṇi nāgaṇaiyār marungē vara ariyarēlum orungē emakkavaraik kāṇalām eppoḻudum uḷḷāl manakkavalai tīrppār varavu*

**Пословный перевод:**

varavu – due to (his) arrival; manam kavalai tīrppār – one who removes sorrow from the mind; marungu – nearby; ōdam mōdum – such that ocean will keep lapping; maṇi nāgaṇaiyār – emperumān who is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who has gems; marungē vara ariyarēlum – even if he is beyond approach (by us); emakku – for us; uḷḷāl – through mind; eppoḻudum – at all times; orungē – in the same manner; avarai kāṇalām – have direct vision of him

**Перевод:**

**varavu manakkavalai tīrppārai – he will remove sorrow from my mind due to his arrival \[at the place where I am present\]. This implies that emperumān, on his own, fell head over heels on āḻvār and made him his servitor.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that since his sins have disappeared, he is able to constantly see emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Next, the pāsuram says about the place where emperumān carried out sādanānuśtānam (performing activities as means to attain)

**marungu ōdam mōdum maṇi nāgaṇaiyār** – the opinion here is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), thinking of attaining [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), reclined on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān, in the milky ocean, with the waves gently massaging his feet.

**marungē vara ariyarēlum** – even if it is difficult for us to go near him

**namakku orungē uḷḷāl avarai eppoḻudum kāṇalām** – since he dwells in our mind, we can constantly see him in the same manner, through our mind. yajur vēdam 1.6 says “*prajāpatis tvam vēda prajāpatim tvō vēda yam prajāpatir vēda sa puṇyō bhavati*” (supreme being thinks of one (cētana, sentient entity); one thinks of supreme being. The one who is thought of by supreme being, undoubtedly, attains supreme being). Thus, it is clear from this that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has the greatness to be thought of by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

We will take up the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 56. Varavaru onrillaiyal

*varavāṟu onṟillaiyāl vāḻvu inidaḷ ellēoruvāṟu oruvan pugāvāṟu urumāṟumāyavardām sēyavardām anṟulagam tāyavardāmmāyavardām kāttum vaḻi*

**Пословный перевод:**

oru āṟu – in any means; oruvan – any jīvātmā (sentient entity); pugāvāṟu – not allowing to enter; uru māṟum – transforming his basic nature and qualities; āyavar tām – as kriṣṇa who was born in the clan of covherds; sēyavar tām – being at a far away distance (for duriyōdana et al); anṟu – at one point in time; ulagam tāyavar tām – one who measured all the worlds; māyavar tām – emperumān who is an amazing entity; kāttum vaḻi – the means that he showed; vara āṟu – the way it came; onṟu illaiyāl – nothing is manifesting; vāḻvu indāl – (our) life is very sweet; ellē – how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**varavāṟu onṟu illaiyāl – there is no means that I have for emperumān to come on his own and take residence in my mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked “How is it that one, who is very difficult to approach for everyone, is such simple for you to approach? “ āḻvār says “I too do not know \[the reason for that\]”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

If no activity had been performed as means, would the benefit be small? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) responds  . . .

**vāḻvu inidāl** – the benefit is very huge.

**ellē** – how amazing is this!

**oru āṟu oruvan pugāvāṟu urumāṟum** – the implication is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) keeps taking incarnations by transforming his nature, his divine form and his qualities so that one (jīvātmā) need not analyse whether he has any means for attaining the benefit and without the jīvātmā having to enter any means. Did emperumān not say in carama ślōkam ([śrī bhagavat gītā 18.66](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/)) to leave aside all means and to attain only him! The opinion here is that while his followers pāṇḍavas enjoyed the end benefits, emperumān observed the means of going \[to duriyōdana\] as their messenger, driving \[arjuna’s\] chariot, taking the arrows on his chest \[during the war\] etc.

**āyavar tām sēyavar tām** – kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who told arjuna to attain him, remained as his charioteer while at the same time he made duriyōdana et al fall as prey for arjuna’s arrows and remained very far from them.

**anṟu ulagam tāyavar tām** – while he could be very far from those who do not have affection for him, he could measure the worlds, when he decides, and become very close to everyone.

**māyavar tām kāttum vaḻi** – the means shown by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who has amazing nature, divine form, qualities etc. This should be added to the verse *varavāṟu onṟillaiyāl vāḻvinidāl* to get the apt meaning.

We will move on to the 57<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 57. Valittangu valvinayai

*vaḻittangu valvinaiyai māṟṟānō nenjē taḻīkkoṇḍu pōravuṇan tannai suḻittengum tāḻvidangaḷ paṟṟip pulāl veḷḷam tānugaḷa vāḻvadanga mārvidanda māl*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh mind!; pōr – in the battle; avuṇan tannai – the demon hiraṇya kaśyap; taḻīkkoṇḍu – hugging him tightly; tāḻvu idangaḷ engum paṟṟi – pervading all the low lying places; pulāl veḷḷam tān – the flood of blood; suḻittu ugaḷa – flowing with a swirl; vāḻvu adanga – ensuring that the demon’s arrogance of wealth would be suppressed; mārvu idanda – one who tore the chest; māl – the supreme being; vaḻi tangu – residing by other means; val vanaiyai – ignorance etc which have lot of strength; māṟṟānō – will he not remove?

**Перевод:**

**vaḻittangu valvinaiyai māṟṟānō nenjē – Oh heart! Will not emperumān remove ignorance, taste in worldly matters etc which have been staying with us since time immemorial and which came to us as a result of our deeds in earlier births? The implied meaning is that he will certainly remove all these.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār’s heart asked him “How to get rid of ignorance etc which have been intertwined with us from time immemorial and also taste in worldly matters?” āḻvār says “The one who killed hiraṇya kaśyap will remove these also”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) asks further that for one who annihilated the demon hiraṇya (hiraṇya kaśyap), is removing these an issue?

**taḻīkkoṇḍu pōravuṇan tannai suḻittengum tāḻvidangaḷ paṟṟi pulāl veḷḷam tān ugaḷa vāḻvadanga mārvidanda māl** – will the supreme being, who tightly hugged the demon hiraṇya kaśyap, for whom fighting is a habit, such that the demon’s blood flowed into all low lying areas like a flood with a swirl, who removed the arrogance of strength and wealth of the demon,  and who tore the chest of the demon, not remove our ignorance etc?

**suḻittengum tāḻvidangaḷ paṟṟip pulāl veḷḷam tān ugaḷa** – did not [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) too think of this same incident in his periya tirumoḻi 11-4-4 “*vaḷaiyugirālimoymbin maṟavōnadu āgam madiyādu senṟorugirāl piḷaveḻavitta kuttamadu vaiyamūdu perunīrin mummai peridē*” (the flow of blood from the demon when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tore his chest with his finger nails, unmindful of the strength of the demon, was three times the quantum of deluge)!

We will take up the 58<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 58. 58 Male padiccodi

*mālē padiccōdi māṟṟēl iniyunadu pālē pōl sīril paḻuttoḻindēn mēlāl piṟappinmai peṟṟu adikkīḻ kuṟṟēvalanṟu maṟappinmai yān vēṇḍum mādu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālē – Oh, the lord of all!; padi sōdi – one who has radiance of divine, auspicious, physical form; unadu – your; pāl pōl sīril – your auspicious qualities which are like sweet milk; paḻuttu oḻindēn – I have soaked myself in them fully; ini māṟṟēl – do not deny that experience of enjoying your auspicious qualities; mēlāl – in the times to come; yān vēṇḍum mādu – the wealth which I desire; piṟappu inmai peṟṟu – attaining the status of not being born; un adikkīḻ kuṟṟēval anṟu – not carrying out kainkaryam at your divine feet; maṟappu inmai – not forgetting (your divine feet)(is the wealth that I desire)

**Перевод:**

**mālē padiccōdi – Oh Lord of all! Oh one who has auspicious divine form which is not made of primordial matter! \[primordial matter is what is present in the physical forms of all those who are present in leelā vibhūti (samsāram or materialistic world); this includes entities from brahmā down to an ant\]. The term padiccōdi refers to the radiance (sōdi) emanating from his divine form which is called as padi. Alternatively, the term mālē would refer to one who is totally infatuated with followers who show just a little bit of affection towards him. padiccōdi could be considered as referring to the lustre coming from his divine, auspicious physical form. Another interpretation – mālē could be considered as referring to the qualities connected to his being a superior entity since he is the lord of all and it could also be considered as referring to the qualities connected to his simplicity since he is affectionate towards his followers, thus referring to all qualities of the ātmā \[paramātmā, the supreme being\] and padiccōdi could be considered as referring to qualities connected to his divine form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that he is not asking for attaining paramapadam. He requests emperumān, in this pāsuram, that he should always be immersed in engaging with emperumān’s auspicious qualities. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māṟṟēl ini** – do not change the experience that you gave me without any cause, just like one gives land without any taxes being levied on it.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) describes further about what that experience is. . .

**unadu pālē pōl sīril paḻuttoḻindēn** – I have soaked myself fully in your auspicious qualities which are like sweet milk. The opinion is that āḻvār is requesting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) not to change that experience which was granted to him causelessly.

emperumān then asks him whether [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) desires anything else further . . .

**mēlāl piṟappinmai peṟṟu adikkīḻ kuṟṟēval anṟu** – the wealth which I desire is not attaining parapapadam and carrying out service to your divine feet, after getting rid of being born repeatedly in this materialistic realm and tottering here.

**maṟappinmai yān vēṇḍum mādu** – The only wealth that I desire is not to forget the way you are sitting in paramapadam in the times to come. Just as sāvitri (a lady referred to in purāṇas) took a boon from yamadarmarāja to beget a son and recover her auspicious glories, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) too requests something from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which establishes his attaining paramapadam instead of asking for it directly from him since emperumān is a totally independent entity (*sarvasvatantran*) who could reject such a request from āḻvār.

The story of sāvitri as narrated in epics and purāṇas is as follows: Since the longevity of sāvitri’s husband \[satyavān\] was over, yamadarmarāja (deity for righteousness) started taking him. Since sāvitri was *paramapativratā* (totally devoted to her husband), due to the power of her chastity, she followed yama and asked him to return her husband. yama tells her “It is my duty to take the lives of people once their time is over. Ask me of any other boon, I will grant it to you”. She immediately requested yama “Grant me a child through my husband” and thus got her husband back.

In the same way, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is asking for non-forgetfulness and establishes his attaining paramapadam. āḻvār had sung in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/03/thiruvaimozhi-6-7-1-unnum-soru/) “*uṇṇum sōṟu parugum nīr tinnum veṟṟilai ellām kaṇṇan*” (the food eaten, the water drunk and the betel leaves consumed are all kriṣṇa). Will such an āḻvār have forgetfulness? He is afraid that forgetfulness which those who have connection to a physical form \[in this materialistic world\] suffer from, will affect him too and hence he is asking for this boon. When the entire village is burning in fire, could one remain unaffected saying “nothing will happen to my house”!

We will take up the 59<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 59. Made varapperuvaram

*mādē varappeṟuvarām enṟē val vinaiyār kādānum ādānum kaikkoḷḷār ūdēpōyp pērōdam sindutiraik kaṇ vaḷarum pērāḷan pērōdac cindikkap pērndu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēr ōdam – huge ocean; tirai – (agitating) waves; ūdē pōy sindu – scattering, close-by; kaṇvaḷarum – reclining, as if sleeping; pērāḷan – one who has greatness; pēr ōda – to recite his divine names; sindikka – the moment one thinks; valvinaiyār – sins who are very powerful; pērndu – leaving (us); kādānum – either forest; ādānum – or some other place; kaikkoḷḷār – they do not attain; mādē – near us (just like before); varap peṟuvarām enṟē – is it due to the desire to attain us?

**Перевод:**

**ūdē pōy pērōdam sindutiraik kaṇvaḷarum pērāḷan pēr ōdac cindikkap pērndu val vinaiyār kādānum ādānum kaikkoḷḷār, mādē varap peṟuvarām anṟē – in the huge ocean of tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean), emperumān is reclining such that the agitating waves come and scatter on his divine form. Once we decide to recite the divine names of that emperumān, who is reclining in the milky ocean with the ocean gently massaging him with its hands of waves, and who is biased towards his devotees, vouldn’t our sins leave us and attain some forest or mountain or ocean as their residence?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār has ascertained with emperumān that he would attain paramapadam, tells joyously “Could sins come anywhere near me!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

āḻvār is asking whether the reason for their delaying this process is in line with their desire of staying near us.

Let us take up the 60<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 60. Perndoru nokkadu

*pērndonṟu nōkkādu pinniṟpāy nillāppāy īntuḻāy māyanaiyē en nenjē pērndengum tollai mā vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku illai kāṇ maṟṟōr iṟai*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh my mind!; tollai – ancient; mā – expansive; vem – cruel; naragil – in the hell of samsāram; sērāmal – not to be trapped; kāppadaṟku – to protect; engum pērndu – even if one enters any other place; maṟṟu ōr iṟai illai kāṇ – you would see that there is no other protector; pērndu – leaving him; onṟum nōkkādu – not looking at anyone else; In tuḻāy māyanaiyē – only the amazing entity who is wearing tuḷasi garland; pin niṟpāy – follow him; nillāppāy – get destroyed, not following him

**Перевод:**

**pērndu onṟu nōkkādu In tuḻāy māyanaiyē pin niṟpāy nillāppāy – Either follow emperumān, who is donning the sweetly fragrant tuḷasi garland, without desiring anyone else, or in line with your nature of being independent, get trapped in the bottomless crevices of worldly pursuits and suffer.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, fearful that his heart, due to past impressions, would not engage with matters related to emperumān, despises it. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart further “Before you choose either of the two paths which I have just mentioned, listen carefully to the matter that I am going to tell you now”.

**en nenjē tollai mā vennaragil sērāmal kāppadaṟku engum pērndu maṟṟōr iṟai illai kān** – Wherever you go in this cruel hell of expansive samsāram which has been following \[us\] from ancient times, there is no refuge other than that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is donning the tuḷasi garland, to protect you; knowing this, either follow that emperumān or engage in worldly pursuits as per your wishes and get destroyed.

We will take up the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 61. Iraimuraiyan sevadimel

*iṟaimuṟaiyān sēvadimēl maṇṇaḷanda annāḷ maṟai muṟaiyāl vānādar kūdi muṟai muṟaiyin tādilagu pūtteḷittāl ovvādē tāḻ visumbin mīdilagit tān kidakkum mīn*

**Пословный перевод:**

iṟai muṟaiyān – one who has the relationship of being īśvaran (controller) for us; maṇṇalanda – when he measured the worlds (which are his possessions); annāḷ – during that time; tāḻ visumbin mīdu – on the expansive sky; ilagi kidakkum mīn – stars which are scattered over an expansive area, emitting lustre; vānādar – celestial entities; kūdi – together; muṟai muṟaiyin – standing in an orderly way; maṟai muṟaiyāl – in the method mentioned in the vēdas (sacred texts); sēvadi mēl – on those divine feet; tādu ilagu pū teḷittāl ovvādē – appear scatted like flowers which are lustrous due to their pollens

**Перевод:**

**iṟai muṟaiyān maṇṇaḷanda sēvadimēl – emperumān measured the worlds in order to establish the fact that he possesses these worlds. During that time, on his divine feet . . .**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since he had obtained what he had desired, in order to spend his time purposefully, āḻvār looks up at the expansive sky, which is the possession of emperumān. He saw the countless stars there and thinks “Are these \[the stars\] flowers which the celestial people scattered when emperumān measured the worlds with his divine foot? ” One conclusion that we can draw out of this is that even when āḻvārs see normal events, these would appear to them to be connected to emperumān always. periyavāccān piḷḷai, the commentator for this divya prabandam (divine composition) quotes an incident from the lives of our pūrvācāryas (preceptors): siṟiyāccān would tell nampiḷḷai “Just as for samsāris like ourselves, when we attempt to enjoy bhagavat viśayams (matters related to emperumān) they would appear to have some connection with śabdām etc (the five sensory perceptions), for āḻvārs, even whey look at events which happen in samsāram as a routine, these would appear to be connected to bhagavat viśayam and they cannot extricate themselves from such matters”. Since brahmā, rudra et al cannot go to paramapadam and enjoy emperumān there, āḻvār recounts an incident during one of his incarnations in which these celestial entities experienced emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

The rest of the pāsuram mentions about what kainkaryam the celestial entities carried out to those divine feet . . .

**annāl vānādar kūdi muṟai muṟaiyin maṟai muṟaiyāl tādilagu pūtteḷittāl ovvādē tāḻ visumbin mīdu ilagikkidakkum mīn tān** – stars which are scattered on the expansive sky, emitting lustre, appear like the flowers shining due to their pollens, which celestial entities such as brahmā, rudra, indra et al, standing in a line and following the way mentioned in the sacred texts, proffered to the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) when he measured the worlds

We will take up the 62<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 62. Min ennum kambil

*mīn ennum kambil veṟi ennum veḷḷi vēy vānennum kēdilā vānkudaikkut tānōr maṇikkāmbu pōl nimirndu maṇṇaḷandān nangaḷ piṇikkām peru marundu pin*

**Пословный перевод:**

mīn ennum kambil – in the rods of stars; veṟi veḷḷi ennum vēy – having moon, the king of stars and Venus as the spokes; vān ennum – having the name of sky; kēdilā vān kudaikku – for the indestructible, huge umbrella; tān – he (as trivikrama); ōr maṇi kāmbu pōl nimirndu – as a unique handle with blue gem; maṇ aḷandān – measured the worlds; pin – beyond that; nangaḷ piṇikku – for our disease (of samsāram); peru marundu Am – is a great medicine

**Перевод:**

**mīn ennum kambil – āḻvār is making the sky as a simile for a unique umbrella. An umbrella needs rods on the outer periphery; it needs a handle to hold it and spokes which will link the handle and the rods. The cluster of stars would be like the rods of the umbrella.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since in the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had spoken about emperumān’s incarnation as trivikrama, he continues to enjoy the same incarnation in this pāsuram too. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**veṟi ennum veḷḷi vēy** – The term *veṟi* refers to moon, who is the king of stars. īsn’t moon called as “udupati” (king of stars) and as “tārāpati” (king of stars)? The term *veḷḷi* refers to the luminescent planet Venus. These two are like the spokes for the umbrella. *vēy* – bamboo tube.

**vān ennum kēdilā vān kudaikku** – For the huge umbrella of sky which is stable until the time of deluge.

**tān ōr maṇikkāmbu pōl nimirndu maṇṇaḷandān** – For this umbrella of sky, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) became the handle, made of blue gems, grew to a huge form and measured the worlds.

**pin nangaḷ piṇikkup peru marundu Am** – Just as he measured the worlds and removed the hurdles of celestial entities in earlier times, he became the great medicine for our disease of samsāram (repeated cycle of births) in later times.

We will move on to the 63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 63. Pin turakkum

*pin turakkum kāṟṟiḻanda sūl koṇḍal pērndum pōy van tiraikkaṇ vandaṇainda vāymaittē anṟu tirucceyya nēmiyān tīyarakki mūkkum parucceviyum īrnda paran*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – during the time of śrī rāma’s incarnation; tiru seyya nēmiyān – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who has vīralakśmi (wealth of victory) and reddish divine disc; tī arakki mūkkum – the nose of cruel demon sūrpaṇakā; paru seviyum – stout ears; īrnda paran – having the greatness of severing them; pērndum pōy – leaving from this world; van tirai kaṇ vandu aṇainda (padi) – (the nature of) reaching tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) having strong waves; pin turakkum – pushing from behind; kāṟṟu iḻanda – leaving from air; sūl – having water in its womb; koṇḍal – cloud; pērndum pōy – going again; van tirai kaṇ – over ocean which has strong waves; vandu aṇainda vāymaittu – appears to be fitting well

**Перевод:**

**Our preceptors would give two different commentaries for this pāsuram. Since there was a shortcoming in each of these two commentaries, nanjīyar’s disciple nampiḷḷai would give a third interpretation which did not have either of these two shortcomings. These commentaries are described below:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about the way trivikrama controlled mahābali. In this pāsuram, he talks about how śrī rāma controlled the demons. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pin turakkum kāṟṟihanda sūl koṇḍal pērndum pōy van tiraikkaṇ vandaṇainda vāymaitte . . . . paran** – The first two lines are to be considered as the analogy and the last two lines as the subject. The meaning of the last two lines is – sarvēśvaran (lord of all), who has the wealth of victory and the reddish divine disc, has the greatness of having severed the cruel demon sūrpaṇakā’s nose and stout ears. The meaning of the first two lines is – it appears that water laden cloud, which has lost the air that was pushing it from behind, comes back over the ocean which has strong waves, in a fitting way. In the first interpretation, since the term *paran* which denotes a sentient entity has been used, the term *vāymaittē* has been changed to *vāymaiyyan* and the meaning for the pāsuram is considered as follows: [śrī rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who destroyed the demons and returned to tiruppāṟkadal has the analogy of cloud, which, after separating from air which pushes it from behind (as a prime mover), goes back to the ocean. The second interpretation involves adding the word *padi* (nature) to the last word *paran* and getting the meaning as given in the text – the nature of śrī rāma is similar to the nature of cloud.

In the first interpretation, the word *vāymaittē* is changed to *vāymaiyyan* and the meaning has been derived. In the second interpretation, the word *padi* has been added to the pāsuram and the meaning derived. Thus there is a shortcoming in each of these two interpretations. When [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) mercifully gave these two interpretations to his disciple [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/), the latter interpreted the meaning in a third way which did not call for any alteration to the pāsuram and presented that to nanjīyar. In this interpretation, the words *pin turakkum kāṟṟiḻanda sūl koṇḍal pērndum pōy* becomes the subject which is compared. This line also remains as the subject of the pāsuram. The nature of [śrī rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) which is described in the last two lines becomes the analogy for the cloud. Now the vyākhyānam for the pāsuram is as follows:

The water laden cloud (which reached the ocean) after losing the air which pushes it from behind is similar to śrī rāma, who, after severing the nose and stout ears of the cruel demon sūrpaṇakā, reached tiruppāṟkadal again (at the end of his incarnation). Just as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had linked the stars to trivikrama’s incarnation in the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram, he links the cloud, which reaches ocean after losing its prime-mover air, to śrī rāma’s incarnation. The words *van tiraikkaṇ vandaṇainda* are common to both the water laden cloud and rāmāvatāram (incarnation of [śri rāma](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) since both reach the ocean at the end. Air, which pushes the cloud from behind, helps it to roam around in the skies. In the same way, the prayers of celestial entities enable the incarnation of śrī rāma. Once their prayers have been answered, śrī rāma too, just like the cloud which has lost air, returns to tiruppāṟkadal. Just as the cloud is laden with water (for raining), śrī rāma is laden with mercy (which he showers on all). Thus, we can see that both the subject and the analogy are congruent in all aspects. The reference to severing of sūrpaṇakā’s nose and ears is indicative of śrī rāma’s annihilating all the demons, down to rāvaṇa.

We will take up the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 64. Paranam avan

*paranām avan ādal pāvipparāgil uranāl oru mūnṟu pōdum maram ēḻanṟu eydānaip puḷḷinvāy kīṇḍānaiyē amarar kaidān toḻāvē kalandu*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – at an earlier point of time; ēḻ maram eydānai – one who shot a single arrow at and felled seven trees; puḷḷin vāy kīṇḍānai – one who tore the mouth of a demon who came in the form of a stork; avan – that emperumān; paran ādal – is the supreme entity; amarargaḷ – celestial entities; oru mūnṟu pōdum – at all times (past, present and future); uranāl pāvipparāgil – if they think, with their minds; kaidān – (their) hands; kalandu toḻāvē – will they not join together and worship him?

**Перевод:**

**oru mūnṟu pōdum uranāl avan paranādal pāvipparāgil maram ēḻu anṟu eydānaip puḷḷin vāy kīṇḍānaiyē amarar kaidān kalandu toḻāvē – at an earlier point of time emperumān had incarnated for the sake of celestial entities,  as śrī rāma and carried out amazing activities such as shot down seven trees with a single arrow etc, and as kriṣṇa and carried out amazing activities such as tearing the mouth of a demon, bakāsura, who came in the form of a stork etc. If these celestial entities such as brahmā, rudra, indra et al think of these activities through their minds, at all times, will their hands not vorśsip emperumān? āḻvār feels sad that these celestial entities, due to their pride, think of emperumān as one among themselves and are going the wrong way.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār touched upon the incarnations of emperumān in the previous pāsurams, he says in this pāsuram that celestial entities such as indra et al should have saluted emperumān when he incarnated as śrī rāma and kriṣṇa, by continuously meditating on him. This would have removed their deficiency that they could not go to paramapadam \[to see emperumān\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

We will take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 65. Kalandu naliyum

*kalandu  naliyum kadum tuyarai nenjē malanga adittu madippān vilangal pōl tonmālaik kēsavanai nāraṇanai mādavanaic conmālai eppoḻudum sūttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh mind!; kalandu naliyum – being together with us and troubling us; kadum tuyarai – cruel sin; malanga adittu – hitting it so well that it trembles; madippān – to drive it away; vilangal pōl tol mālai – being hard like a mountain which cannot be cracked, being ancient and great; kēsavanai – having beautiful bundles of divine locks; mādavanai – consort of śrī mahālakśmi; nāraṇanai – for nārāyanan; eppoḻudum – at all times; sol mālai – garland made of words; sūttu – bedeck

**Перевод:**

**nenjē! kalandu naliyum kadum tuyarai malanga adittu madippān – āḻvār tells his mind that all that it has to do is this, in order to drive away the cruel sin, which is troubling us by being together with us, so that we do not have to experience it. The sin has to be battered in such a way that it runs away, trembling.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “Let the celestial entities not serve him. You serve him, without any break”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

What is that, which has to be done?

**vilangal pōl tol mālaik kēsavanai nāraṇanai mādavanaic col mālai eppoḻudum sūttu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is like a mountain which cannot be cracked; he is ancient \[existing from time immemorial\]; he is great and cannot be estimated by anyone; he has beautiful locks; he has the quality of motherly forbearance towards his devotees; he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi. Bedeck such emperumān with garland made of words, which will not droop ever and which will always retain its fragrance. Can we, who are very low, approach such a great emperumān and bedeck him with a garland? Since he is mādavan, who has [śrī mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) always on his chest and since he is nāraṇan who has lot of affection towards his devotees, he will lower himself so that we can decorate him with the garland. Since he has the cluster of sentient entities as his dwelling place, it is clear that nārāyaṇan (one who has all the souls as his dwelling place) has motherly forbearance towards those entities.

We will take up the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 66. Suttaya nemiyan

*sūttāya nēmiyān tollarakkan innuyirai māttē tuyar iḻaitta māyavanai ītta veṟi koṇḍa taṇ tuḻāy vēdiyanai nenjē aṟi kaṇḍāy sonnēn adu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjē – Oh my mind!; sūdu āya nēmiyān – one who has the divine disc which can be worn as an ornament; tol arakkan – \[the demon\] rāvaṇa, who has been living for a long time, his; in uyirai – sweet life; māttē – standing in close proximity; tuyar iḻaitta – one who created trouble; māyavanai – one who has amazing power; ītta – abundant; veṟi koṇḍa – having lot of fragrance; taṇ tuḻāy – wearing the cool garland of tuḷasi; vēdiyanai – one who is spoken of in the vēdam (sacred texts); aṟi kaṇḍāy – know about him; adu sonnēn – I have instructed (you) this previous meaning

**Перевод:**

**sūttāya nēmiyān – emperumān has the divine disc, which not only annihilates enemies but is also suitable for being worn as an ornament.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār told his heart to carry out service to emperumān. In this pāsuram, he tells his heart that emperumān is such that he destroys enemies, he is sweet and he is the ordained entity for us and is the most apt entity for us to carry out kainkaryam. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tol arakkan innuyirai māttē tuyar iḻaitta māyavanai** – one who has the amazing power of standing in close proximity to rāvaṇa, the demon, and troubled his life which rāvaṇa was protecting very carefully for a very long time. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 60-3 “*brahmadaṇḍa prakāśānām vidyut sadruśa varcasam* I *smaran rāghavabāṇānām vivyatē rākśasēśvara:*  īī ” (the kind of demons, rāvaṇa, felt distressed thinking about the arrows of śrī rāma which have the lustre of the sceptre of supreme entity and the radiance of lightning), did not rāvaṇa suffer so much, thinking about the powerful, divine arrows of śrī rāma which carried his name, that he felt suffocated by the weapons and arrows that he was carrying!

**ītta veṟi koṇḍa taṇ tuḻāy vēdiyanai** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who wears the garland of tuḷasi which has so much of fragrance that one would think whether it is the repository of all the fragrance available, and who is spoken of in the vēdas (sacred texts).

**nenjē aṟi kaṇḍāy sonnēn adu** – Oh dear heart! I have told you this. Know about it. Since [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) destroyed rāvaṇa, he has the nature of annihilating enemies; since he is wearing tuḷasi garland, he is sweet; since he is spoken of in the vēdas, he is the ordained lord. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his heart that for these reasons, know that he is the one for whom kainkaryam is to be carried out. This meaning is not easily available to anyone; know this.

We will take up the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 67. Aduvo nanrenru

*aduvō nanṟenṟu angu amarulagō vēṇḍil aduvō poruḷillai anṟē adu oḻindu maṇṇinṟu āḷvēn enilum kūdu(m) mada nenjē kaṇṇan tāḷ vāḻttuvadē kal*

**Пословный перевод:**

angu adu nanṟu enṟu – knowing that enjoying emperumān at paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) is good; amar ulagu vēṇḍil – if (you ) desire śrīvaikuṇṭam, the dwelling place of nityasūris; adu – that paramapadam; poruḷ illai anṟē – is not a significant material (which emperumān cannot grant us); adu oḻindu – leaving aside desiring that paramapadam; maṇ ninṟu āḷvēn enilum – if one desires to remain in this world and enjoy its pleasures; kūdum – it is possible (for us to get that); mada nenjē – Oh mind, which is obedient towards me!; kaṇṇan tāḷ – (leaving aside all these things) only the divine feet of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa); vāḻttuvadē – praising; kal – learn

**Перевод:**

**aduvō nanṟu enṟu angu amarulagō vēṇḍil aduvō poruḷ illai anṟē – if you (āḻvār tells his heart) desire paramapadam, the place where nityasūris dwell, thinking that it is apt to enjoy emperumān in paramapadam, it is not something which emperumān cannot grant us. The term amarulagu is a modification of the term amarar ulagu (word of nityasūris). Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-9 “vānavar nādu” (the country of nityasūris), since emperumān created śrīvaikuṇṭam only for the sake of nityasūris, āḻvār is referring to it as amarulagu.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār tells his heart “When emperumān is sweet and is also the ordained lord, instead of asking for paramapadam or any other wish, keep praising his divine feet”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēṇḍil** – while [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) could be seen and enjoyed in this world itself, it is rare for someone to leave this aside and desire paramapadam as mentioned in periya tiruvandādi [pāsuram 53](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/01/periya-thiruvandhadhi-59/) “*nin pugaḻil vaigum than sindahiyilum maṟṟinidō nī avarkku vaigundam enṟaruḷum vān*” (is your granting of śrīvaikuṇṭam to your devotees any sweeter than their minds in which they are constantly praising your auspicious qualities). The opinion here is that someone could think that enjoying emperumān in this word is lowly since that experience is not permanent. Hence someone could desire to enjoy him in śrīvaikuṇṭam.

**aduvō poruḷ illai** – it is not beyond [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to grant us paramapadam. Just as it has been mentioned by [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 53 “*ninnadiyārkku enseyvan enṟē irutti nī*” (you are constantly thinking of what else you could do for your devotees), it is not great for emperumān to grant śrīvaikuṇṭam for his devotees since he desires to grant it to them.

**anṟu adu oḻindu maṇ ninṟu āḷvēn enilum kūdum mada nenjē** – instead of that paramapadam, if one leaves it aside and wants to stay in this world and enjoy its pleasures, emperumān would grant that too.

**mada nenjē** – for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), granting either liberation \[to reach śrīvaikuṇṭam\] or pleasures \[to enjoy in this world\] is not difficult. More difficult is for us to have an attachment towards him. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells his mind “Since you are an obedient mind, you have that inclination (of engaging with emperumān)”

When his heart asks him as to what it should do, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) tells it . . . .

**kaṇṇan tāḷ vāḻttuvadē kal** – try to learn and sing praises on the divine feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is very simple  towards his devotees. The opinion is that even if emperumān is willing to give both the vibhūtis (the spiritual realm, śrīvaikuṇṭam and the materialistic realm, samsāram), shun both and learn to praise him.

We will move on to the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 68. Kallum kanaikadalum

*kallum kanaikadalum vaigunda vānādum pullenṟoḻindanakol ēpāvam vella nediyān niṟam kariyān uḷpugundu nīngān adiyēn uḷḷattu agam*

**Пословный перевод:**

vella nediyān – being very great; niṟam kariyān – emperumān who is black in complexion; uḷ pugundu – entering me; adiyēnadu uḷḷattu agam – from my heart; nīngān – will not separate and go; kallum – tiruvēngadamalai (hills of tirumala); kanai kadalum – roaring tiruppāṟdakal (milky ocean); vaigunda vānādum – śrīvaikuṇṭam, also known as paramapadam; pul enṟu oḻin dana kol – have they become deserted (such that grass has grown tall)?; E pāvam – Oh, how sad!

**Перевод:**

**kallum – āḻvār refers to tirumalai as kal (stone). Is it correct to refer to tirumalai as kal? The relationship between āḻvārs and emperumān is such that they would refer to tirumalai as kal, tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśeśan) as tam pāmbu (his snake) (as in nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-3), and in verses such as māṇiyāy maṇṇaḷandāy (measured the worlds as a bachelor), they refer to emperumān as a bachelor.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “Other than singing praises on emperumān’s divine feet, I do not want either liberation or pleasures”. This pāsuram says that emperumān mingled completely with āḻvār saying “Other than this āḻvār I do not want other dwellings such as para (śrīvaikuṇṭam), vyūha (tiruppāṟkadal /milky ocean), arccai (deity form) etc” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kallum kanai kadalum vaigunda vānādum pul enṟu oḻindana kol ēpāvam** –  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) does not appear to believe that there are certain divine locations such as tirumalai or that there is a vyūha location such as tiruppāṟkadal or that there is a paramapadam and that he has inclination towards these locations.

**pul enṟu oḻindana kol** – When looking at the way he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] is engaged with my heart, it appears that those locations have become bushes (due to non-inhabitation). We could also consider the meaning of this to be – these divine dwelling places have become insignificant after [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) entered [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s heart. [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/), the commentator for this prabandam says that the attitude of āḻvār while saying this is similar to the attitude of the girls who married the sage saubhari.

The reference is to a narration regarding a sage saubhari who took fifty human forms to marry fifty daughters of a king and lived in a palace with each of the fifty daughters. When the king went around his daughters’ palaces to check if they had any grievance, all of them said the same line “I have no problem, but my husband does not go to any other sister’s house”. In the same way [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has forgotten all other residences and is dwelling only in his heart.

**ēpāvam** – this appears to be the result of some sin; however, since there is no sin in this, we can consider this to be an expression of wonder.

**vella nediyān** – being great in the quality of boundlessness, he cannot be defeated by anyone. Alternatively, the term *vella* could be taken to mean “very” (a lot) and thus the meaning of *vella nediyān* could be taken to mean that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is one who is very great and there is none who is greater than he. Here, the term greatness refers to the greatness of his qualities.

**niṟam kariyān** – even if he does not have qualities, he cannot be given up due to the beauty of his form.

**uḷpugundu nīngān adiyēnadu uḷḷattu agam** – entering my heart, he does not appear to consider leaving it. The opinion is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) appears to think that if he were to leave [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)’s heart, then āḻvār cannot sustain himself.

We will take up the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
