## 69. Agam sivanda

*agam sivanda kaṇṇinarāy val vinaiyārāvār mugam sidaivarām anṟē mukki migum tirumāḷ sīrkkadalai uḷpodinda sindanaiyēn tannai ārkkadalām sevvē adarttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

valvinaiyar āvār – cruel sins; agam sivanda kaṇṇinarāy – having reddish eyes, inside; mukki – feeling sorrowful; mugam sidaivarām anṟē – vouldn’t the face droop?; migum tirumāḷ – the great consort of śrī mahālakśmi; sīr kadalai – ocean of auspicious qualities; uḷ podinda – having taken in; sindanaiyēn tannai – me, who has such mind; sevvē adarttu – pressing tightly; aṟkku adalām – who can trouble?

**Перевод:**

**valvinaiyar āvār agam sivanda kaṇṇinarāy mukki kugam sidaivarām anṟē – āren’t the cruel sins, who were ruling over us all along, forced to vacate their places, with their eyes red and their faces in sorrowful state, with an expression of “Having ruled here for such a long, we are being forced out”?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that because of the involvement that emperumān had towards him, the cruel sins, which were the cause of taking another birth, got driven out. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**migum tirumāḷ sīrkkadalai uḷpodinda sindanaiyēn tannai ārkkadalām sevvē adarttu** – Just as tārā had mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkintā kāṇḍam 15-21 “*dātunāmiva śailēndrō guṇānāmākarō mahān*” (Just as a mountain is the repository of minerals, śrī rāma is a huge repository of auspicious qualities), who can get close enough to me and trouble me who has taken in the auspicious qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the greatest amongst everyone?

**sīrkkadalai uḷpodinda** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says that he has been able to consume the auspicious qualities of emperumān which cannot be estimated at all.

**sindanaiyēn tannai ārkku adalām sevvē adarttu** – can anyone, having limited capabilities, control me who has taken in the inestimable qualities of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? The word *mukki* refers to mumbling something, without having the courage to say out loudly or without remaining quiet. Referring to sins as having sentience (*vinaiy****Ar***) and making them to have a face and eyes is indicative of the joy of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), having successfully defeated the sins.

We will take up the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 70. Adarpon mudiyanai

*adar pon mudiyānai āyiram pērānai sudar koḷ sudar āḻiyānai idar kadiyum mātā pituvāga vaittēn enaduḷḷē yādāgil yādē ini*

**Пословный перевод:**

adar pon mudiyānai – having a divine crown which can destroy (enemies on merely sighting them); āyiram pērānai – one who has thousands of divine names; sudar koḷ sudar āḻiyānai – sarvēśvara (lord of all) who has the divine chakra (disc) which has within it, all the objects which emit radiance; idar kadiyum – removing (my) hurdles; mātā pituvāga – as mother and father; enadu uḷḷē vaittēn – established in my mind; ini – from now onvards; yādu āgil yādu – what does it matter, whatever happens?

**Перевод:**

**adar pon mudiyānai – one who has a divine crown, which is beautiful, which will destroy enemies the moment it sees them and which displays the fact that emperumān is the emperor for all the worlds,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about how he was able to defeat his sins. In this pāsuram, he says that once emperumān became his mother and father, what can happen even if he were to suffer the effects of those sins. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**āyiram pērānai** – having many divine names which reveal such beauty, greatness etc.

**sudar koḷ sudar āḻiyānai** – sarvēśvara who has divine disc which contains within it, all the objects which are radiant. Alternatively, the term *sudar koḷ sudar āḻi* could be taken to mean that the radiant divine disc is capable of defeating all the other radiant objects. Due to this distinction, it is implied here that the divine disc, apart from destroying enemies, is also an ornament to be worn for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**idar kadiyum mātā pitāvāga vaittēn enaduḷḷē** – I retained inside my mind [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as  mother and father who will remove all my hurdles. The parents whom we see in the world are those who will observe fasts and other rituals in order to beget offspring and will nurture the offspring, thus creating sorrows for him. In order to highlight that emperumān removes those sorrows, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) qualifies emperumān with the words *idar kadiyum*.

**yādāgil yādē ini** – what does it matter whether I suffer due to the sins or not? The implied meaning is that just as prahlāda did not mind the hurdles put in his path by hiraṇya kaśyap, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) also will not mind if he were to suffer the effects of sins.

We will take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 71. Ini ninru nin perumai

*ini ninṟu nin perumai yān uraippadu ennē tani ninṟa sārvilā mūrtti pani nīr agattulavu senjadaiyān ninnāgattān nāngu mugattān ninnundi mudal*

**Пословный перевод:**

tani ninṟa – standing as a unique, causative factor, during the time of mahāpraḷayam (great deluge); sārvu ilā – not having anyone as a refuge for him; mūrtti – Oh my lord!; pani nīr agattulavu – having the cool gangā (river Ganges) which is flowing inside; sem sadaiyān – siva who has reddish matted hair; āgattān – resides in a corner of your divine form; nāngu mugattān – brahmā, who has four faces; nin undi mudal – has your divine navel as the cause for his existence; ini – when things are like these; yān ninṟu – I, taking efforts; nin perumai – your greatness; uraippadu ennē – how to talk about?

**Перевод:**

**ini ninṟu nin perumāi yān uraippadu ennē – we can consider this to mean either “Is there any need for me to talk about your greatness?” or “Is there any need for me to talk about your simplicity?”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār talks about the supreme nature of emperumān since he, who is the mother and father for āḻvār, is the causative factor for all the worlds, including brahmā, rudra et al. Alternatively, we can say that this pāsuram talks about such simplicity of emperumān that he gave place, in his divine form, for even brahmā, rudra et al. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tani ninṟa sārvilā mūrtti** – Oh [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)! During the time of great deluge, you stood with all the resultant entities (which are created by causative entities) as part of yourself without any name or form, having integrated themselves into their causative entities; you did not have anyone in whom you could take refuge \[unlike these resultant entities who took refuge in you, along with their causative entities, all of them without any name or any form\]. The term *sārvu* refers to being related to something or leaning against something; thus in this context it refers to emperumān being without anyone on whom he could lean against. Did not vēda (sacred text) also glorify emperumān, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli “*patim viśvasyātmēśvaram śāśvatam śivamacyutam*” (being the lord of all the worlds, being *īśvaran* himself (one who controls everything), being intransient, being auspicious and being one who will never let down his followers), saying that he is the lord for all the worlds and that he does not have one who will control him! Alternatively the term *sārvu* would also mean “comparable” and hence *sārvilā mūrtti* could be said that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is incomparable. Just as kriṣṇa says in mahābhāratam āśramavāśika parvam 118-37 “*rudram samāśritā dēvā rudrō brahmāṇamāśrita:* I *brahmā mamāśritō rājan nāham kincidupāśrita:* īī” (Oh king! The celestial entities are taking refuge of rudra; rudra is taking (his father) brahmā’s refuge; brahmā has taken refuge under me; I have not taken refuge under anyone), since emperumān is the head for all, there is none who could be said to be his equal.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) further says that the two entities who are thought to be equal to emperumān are under his control. . .

**pani nīr agattulavu senjadaiyān** – rudra, who has reddish matted hair and has gangā inside that hair, is residing in a corner on your divine form, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/07/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-9-valaththanan-thiripuram/) “*valattanan tirupruam erittavan*” (the one who burnt down the three towns has taken residence on your right side).

**nāngu mugattān nin undi mudal** – brahmā, who has four faces, has your divine navel as his root.

How can I talk about your greatness or your simplicity?

We will take up the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 72. Mudalam tiruvuruvam

*mudalām tiruvuruvam mūnṟenbar onṟē mudalāgum mūnṟukkum enbar mudalvā nigarilagu kāruruvā ninnagattadanṟē pugarilagu tāmaraiyin pū*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiru uruvam mūnṟu – the three entities (brahmā, viṣṇu andn rudra) who have beautiful forms; mudalām enbar – are the causative factors for the origin of earth, (some people) will say; mūnṟukkum – for these three entities; onṟē mudalāgum enbar – an entity (superior) is the primary entity, (some other people) will say; mudalvā – Oh one who is the causative factor for the universe!; kār nigar ilagu uruvā – Oh one who has a divine form matching the dark clouds!; pugar ilagu – shining brightly (being the cause for universe); tāmaraiyin pū – lotus flower; nin agattadu anṟē – is it not forming from your divine form?

**Перевод:**

**mudalām tiruvuruvam mūnṟenbar – Some people will say that brahmā, viṣṇu and rudra are all responsible for the creation of this world. These people, who say that all the three mūrttys are equal, are called as trimūrti sāmyavādis.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that it is only emperumān who is apt to be attained by everyone including brahmā, rudra et al. When the question arose as to why āḻvār is saying that emperumān is the only supreme entity to be attained when some people say that brahmā and rudra are also fit to be attained, āḻvār negates the philosophy of those who speak against the supreme nature of emperumān by saying that the concept of equality among the trimūrtis (brahmā, viṣṇu and rudra) is ill-fitting and that it is only emperumān who is the supreme being, thus establishing his philosophy in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**onṟē mudalāgum mūnṟukkum enbar** – Some others will say that the reason for the creation of these three mūrtis is another entity, superior to these three.  Those who say that there is a fourth entity which is superior to the trimūrttys are called as *trimūrti uttīrṇavādis*. Since āḻvār refers to these people as “*enbar*” (will say like this), he affirms that his philosophy is different from that of these two types of people. Taking these two examples here is representative of similar arguments which talk about the oneness of trimūrtis.

āḻvār further explains that these arguments are not proper. . . .

**mudalvā** – Oh one who is the causative factor for all the worlds, including brahmā, rudra et al!

**nigar ilagu kār uruvā** – the words should be rearranged as *kār nigar ilagu uruvā* to get the correct meaning – one who has a divine form which matches rain bearing cloud. āḻvār hints that since emperumān has bluish form which removes the ennui of those who see him and has the complexion of cloud which gives rain without expecting anything in return, only emperumān is the supreme entity. Hasn’t [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) also established the supreme nature of emperumān in his [tiruneduntāṇḍagam pāsuram 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thirunedunthandakam-2/) “*ōruruvam ponnurugam onṟu sendī onṟu mākadal uruvam ottuninṟa mūvuruvum kaṇḍapōdu onṟu ānjōdi mugiluruvam emmadigaluruvandānē* ” (one form has a golden hue (brahmā), one has reddish fire like hue (rudra) and one has the hue of huge ocean; when the three forms are analysed together, the form of one with radiant, rain bearing cloud is that of my emperumān)!.

āḻvār clearly establishes next that it is emperumān who is causative factor for the other two. . . .

**ninagattadanṟē pugar ilagu tāmaraiyin pū** – īsn’t the lotus flower, which has the distinction of being the place of birth for brahmā, located in your divine navel? This clearly establishes that brahmā who was created on the lotus emanating from his divine navel  and rudra who was created by that brahmā, cannot be emperumān’s equal ; it is only emperumān, being the leader of the other two, who is the reason for creation of the worlds; since there is no mention about a fourth entity, different from these three, who has created brahmā on a lotus, it is not possible for a fourth entity, different  from these three, who can be the cause for the creation of all the worlds. This also proves that brahmā, who was created on a lotus and rudra, who was created by brahmā, cannot be equal to emperumān who created brahmā in his divine navel, thus negating the *trimūrtyaikya vādam* (oneness of trimūrtis). Has it not been shown in śri viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-66 that it is janārdana (emperumān) who remains as the indvelling soul of brahmā and rudra and carries out the functions of creation and annihilation? “*sruśtistityantakaraṇīm brahmā viṣṇu śivātmikām* I *sa samjñānām yāti bhagavānēna Eva janārdana:* īī” (it is janārdana, that bhagavān, who attains the names of brahmā, viṣṇu and Siva who carry out the functions of creation, sustenance and annihilation respectively). One could show the atarvaśika ślōkam 1-2-15 “*brahmā viṣṇu rudrēndrās tē sarvē samprasūyantē*” (brahmā, viṣṇu, rudra, indra are all born) and say that the trimūrtis are born \[in other words they are created\]. But since this only reflects the famous vēda vākhyams (lines from sacred texts) it is clear that the births of brahmā and Siva are due to their karmas (past deeds) and birth of viṣṇu is due to his necessity to incarnate. [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) (son of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/)) also showed the same meaning in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-51 “*vidyē virincagiriśam pratamāvatāra: tat sāmyata: stagayitum tava cēt  svarūpam* I *kim tē paratva piśunairiha rangadāman satva pravartana krupāparipālanādyai:* īī” (Oh śrī ranganāta! If the purpose of your first incarnation is to hide your basic nature, between brahmā and rudra, then what is the benefit for you by exhibiting your superior qualities of being the controller of satva guṇam (purely good qualities) and protecting (brahmā, rudra et al) through your mercy?) It is pertinent to recall here the 116<sup>th</sup> ślōkam of śrī rangarājastavam pūrvaśatakam by parāsara bhattar “*trayō dēvastulyā: tritayamidamadvaitam adikam trikādasmāt tatvam paramiti vitarkān vikatayan* I *vibōrnābhīpadmō vidiśiva nidānam bhagavata: tadanyat brūbhangīparavaditi siddāntayati n:*īī” (emperumān’s divine navel which negates hypotheses such as (1) All the three mūrtis are equal; (2) All the three mūrtis are one; (3) there is another entity which is superior to the three mūrtis and which is the root for brahmā and Siva, establishes for us clearly that the entire world is under the control of emperumān’s knitting of the eyebrows).

Let us move on to the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 73. Puvaiyum kayavum

*pūvaiyum kāyāvum neelamum pūkkinṟa kāvi malarenṟum kāṇḍōṟum pāviyēn mellāvi meymigavē pūrikkum avvavai ellām pirān uruvē enṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pūvaiyum – the flower of pūvai tree (bilberry); kāyāvum – the purple coloured flower of kāyā (a kind of shrub); neelamum – blue lily; pūkkinṟa – just then blossoming; kāvi malar – blue lotus; enṟum – at all times; kāṇ dōṟum – whenever seen; avvavaiyellām – all those flowers; pirān uruvē enṟu – thinking that they are all only the form of emperumān (my lord); pāviyēn – one who has the sins (of not being able to enjoy emperumān directly), my; mel āvi – soft mind; mey – physical body; migavē pūrikkum – will greatly puff up \[due to pride\]

**Перевод:**

**pūvaiyum kāyāvum neelamum pūkkinṟa kāvi malar enṟum kāṇ dōṟum – Whenever I look at the dark coloured flowers of pūvaippū (bilberry flower), kāyāmbū (purple coloured flower), neelam pū (blue water-lily) and just then blossomed kāvippū (blue lotus), during all those times**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār established that only emperumān is the apt entity to be attained. Having established this, he enjoys that emperumān, who is the ordained lord, in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pāviyēn mellāvi mey migavē pūrikkum avvavai ellām pirān uruvē enṟu** – my soft mind and my body will puff up with pride, thinking that all these flowers are \[indicative of\] [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine form only. The word *pāviyēn* would refer to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) thinking that he has lot of sins in him due to which he is unable to see directly the divine form of emperumān. Alternatively in an ironical way, the word *pāviyēn* could mean one who is fortunate. The pāsuram would then mean that āḻvār feels that he is fortunate that looking at the dark coloured flowers which are similes for emperumān, his mind and body would puff up due to pride \[as if they had seen emperumān himself\]. The word *āvi* refers to mind. Since the pāsuram says *enṟum kāṇḍōṟum mellāvi pūrikkum* it shows that his mind will lose consciousness out of being puffed up to a very high extent and that once consciousness is regained, it will again puff up and lose consciousness, thus repeating this process, indicating the softness of his mind. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is amazed that not only his mind, which is the gateway for knowledge, gets puffed up but his body, which is without any knowledge, also puffs up.

We will take up the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 74. Enrum orunal

*enṟum orunāḷ oḻiyāmai yānirandāl onṟum irangār urukkāttār kunṟu kudaiyāga ākātta kōvalanār nenjē pudaidān peridē puvi*

**Пословный перевод:**

orunāḷ oḻiyāmai – without missing even one day; enṟum – at all times; yān irandāl – even if I beseech; kunṟu kudaiyāga – taking the hill gōvardana like an umbrella; ākātta – one who protected cows; kōvalanār – one who tends to cows; onṟum irangār – does not show even a little bit of mercy; uru kāttār – does not show his divine form too; nenjē – Oh my mind; puvidān – this earth; pudai peridē – appears to be on an elevated place (such that his mercy will not flow towards us)

**Перевод:**

**enṟum orunāḷ oḻiyāmai nān irandāl onṟum irangār urukkāttār – I have been begging him from time immemorial, to see him; he (emperumān) is not showing any mercy towards me, from wherever he is staying, thinking “Oh, he is beseeching me”; he is not showing me his divine form either.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*This āḻvār, who is called as dīrga cintayantī (one who alternates between happiness and sadness repeatedly, just like cintayanti, the covherd girl in gōkulam), alternates between happiness when he gets to experience emperumān through his mind and sadness when he does not unite with emperumān externally \[physically\]. In the previous pāsuram, he went the way of happiness and in this pāsuram, finding that emperumān is not showing his divine form to him, suffers. Even though his desire for emperumān developed only after taking his present physical form, it appears to āḻvār that he had been having this desire to see emperumān from time immemorial. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kunṟu kudaiyāga ākātta kōvalanār** – īsn’t he the one who protected cows by holding the hill gōvardana like an umbrella? If he had not manifested himself to anyone, I could have comforted myself saying that he has not shown himself to me because of his nature. īsn’t the person, who showed himself up to cows who cannot even realise the great benefit that he had shown them, not showing himself to me and helping me?

**nenjē pudaidān peridē puvi** – Oh heart! Since he had protected cows, we cannot say that he does not have simplicity. It appears that we are located in a place where his simplicity does not flow (since we are on an elevated surface).By the word *puvi,* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) hints  the place where he is staying and by the words *pudai peridē* he hints that this place is much above the ground level.

We will take up the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 75. Puviyum iruvisumbum

*puviyum iruvisumbum  ninnagatta nīyen seviyin vaḻipugundu ennuḷḷāy avivinṟi yān periyan nī periyai enbadanai yār aṟivār ūn parugu nēmiyāy uḷḷu*

**Пословный перевод:**

Un parugu nēmiyāy – Oh one who has the chakra (disc) which drinks up the blood of enemies!; puviyum – this world (which is made of primordial matter); iruvisumbum – the expansive land of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is not made of primordial matter; nin agatta – are under your control; nī – you (who are as mentioned above); en seivyin vaḻi pugundu – entering through my ear; avivinṟi – without any interruption; en uḷḷāy – exist inside me; nān periyan – am I greater (between the two of us)?; nī periyai – Are you the greater?; enbadanai – this matter; yār aṟivār – who could know?; uḷḷu – think (yourself)

**Перевод:**

**puviyum iruvisumbum ninnagatta – both this leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) and nitya vibhūti (the spiritual realm) exist due to your will. The word puvi refers to the entire materialistic realm (created by emperumān for his pastime) and the word iruvisumbu refers to the ethereal layer of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār was sad that emperumān was not showing any mercy on him and that emperumān was not showing himself to him.  Hearing this, emperumān tells him “Could you feel sad like this? Did I not create a craving in you, making you to yearn to see me? ” which makes āḻvār feel very proud. As a result, he says in this pāsuram “There is none comparable to me”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nī en seviyin vaḻi pugundu avivinṟi ennuḷḷāy** – you take your existence in my heart permanently, by entering through my ears.

**yān periyān nī periyai enbadanai yār aṟivār** – Are you, the controller of these two realms, the greater between the two of us or am I, who had now taken you, the controller of the two realms, inside of me, the greater between the two of us? Who will know this?

**Un parugu nēmiyāy uḷḷu** – Since you are having the disc, which drinks up the blood of all enemies, in your hand, you have unbridled power of knowledge. Hence, you please give an answer to this query.

For the second part of this pāsuram, another interpretation is provided by knowledgeable persons.

**avivinṟi yān periyan** – Since I have kept you, who control both the realms, inside me, without any doubt, I am the greater between the two of us.

**nī periyai enbadanai yār aṟivār** – When this is the fact, who will know that you are greater between us?

Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) has mercifully called himself as great (*periyan*) in this pāsuram, āḻvār is known by the name *periyān* in [*āḻvārtirunagari*](http://azhwarthirunagari.koyil.org/), the place of his birth. Even though this prabandam has only 87 pāsurams, people will say that this is the reason why it is called as *periya tiruvandādi* (the great tiruvandādi).

We will move on to the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 76. Ullilum ullam

*uḷḷilum uḷḷam tadikkum vinaippadalam viḷḷa viḻittu unnai meyyuṟṟāl uḷḷa ulagaḷavum yānum uḷanāvan enkolō ulagaḷanda mūrtty urai*

**Пословный перевод:**

ulagu aḷanda mūrtty – Oh emperumān who mercifully measured all the worlds!; uḷḷilum – even if you are thought of; uḷḷam tadikkum – mind puffs up \[with pride\]; vinaippadalam – the bundle of sins; viḷḷa – to go away (from me); viḻittu – bestowing with your grace; unnai meyyuṟṟāl – if (I am) able to envision you; yānum – I too; uḷḷa ulagaḷavum uḷanāvan – it appears that I will permeate all over the worlds; en kolō – how is this?; urai – please tell (yourself)

**Перевод:**

**uḷḷilum uḷḷam tadikkum – if you are thought of, just as the physical body, which has a form, puffs up, the mind too, which does not have a form, puffs up.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about “entering through my ears” thus speaking about knowledge through hearing. In this pāsuram which begins with the words uḷḷilum (even if one thinks of), he speaks about knowledge through one’s mind, as a complement to the knowledge through hearing. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

āḻvār says further “if my heart will grow just by thinking of you \[emperumān\], when I reach paramapadam after getting rid of my *karma* (results of past deeds, both good and bad), and I am able to see you with your grace, it appears that I too would pervade all over the world just like you”..

**vinaippadalam viḷḷa viḻittu unnai meyyuṟṟāl** – If am able to see you, after my bundle of sins has been eliminated with your grace. Alternatively the words could be rearranged to read *unnai viḻittu meyyuṟṟāl* to give the meaning – if I am able to envision you through my mind here and through my eyes in parapapadam.

**uḷḷa ulagaḷavum yānum uḷanāvan enkolō** – it appears that I too would pervade all the worlds present. It should be understood here that while it may appear that āḻvār is asking whether he too will have universal pervasiveness just like emperumān, pervasiveness comes out of his *svarūpam* (basic nature) for emperumān who is unlimited while it comes out of *darmabhūta jñānam* (sensory perception) for āḻvār who is atomic in nature.

**ulagaḷanda mūrtty urai** – you, who pervaded all the worlds with your dwarfish form, should speak about this pervasive ability of mine.

We will move on to the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 77. Uraikkilor surrattar

*uraikkilōr suṟṟattār uṟṟārenṟārē iraikkum kadal kidanda endāy uraippellām ninnanṟi maṟṟilēn kaṇḍāy enadu uyirkkōr sol nanṟi āgum tuṇai*

**Пословный перевод:**

uraikkil – if one were to mention; ōr suṟṟattār – agnates \[people who are males and who are related from father’s side\]; uṟṟār – other relatives; enṟu – who are spoken of; ārē – who else is there (for me) apart from you?; iraikkum – being uproarious; kadal kidanda – reclining on tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); endāy – my svāmy (lord)!; enadu uyirkku – for my ātmā (soul); nanṟiyāgum – being beneficial; ōr sol – as a unique word; tuṇai – as companion; uraippu ellām – as all types of relationships; nin anṟi – other than you; maṟṟu ilēn kaṇḍāy – I am without anyone, please consider

**Перевод:**

**uraikkil ōr suṟṟattār uṟṟār enṟu ārē – if one were to speak about it, who is apt to be referred to as agnates (male relatives from father’s side) or other relatives? Alternatively, we can consider suṟṟattār as relatives and uṟṟār as friends. Instead of saying “There is none who is an agnate or relative” since āḻvār is asking emperumān a query “Who is there as an agnate or relative” it appears that āḻvār wants emperumān to respond saying “There is none; only I am there”,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār, by mentioning ulagaḷanda mūrtty, recalled emperumān’s quality of being a relative in all ways to all souls, when he manifested his activity of measuring the worlds. In this pāsuram, he is asking whether there is any relative for him other than emperumān. He says that others are considered as relatives due to our bewilderment but the only one who protects us at all the times and in all troublesome situations is emperumān alone and no one else. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iraikkum kadal kidanda endāy** – Oh one who is reclining in the milky ocean with the expectation “Wouldn’t someone from leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) desire and attain us?” even though nityasūris are always experiencing you in paramapadam! By using the word *endāy,* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) hints that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is observing the means to attain āḻvār at tiruppāṟkadal \[by carrying out a penance\]. The term *iraikkum kadal* indicates that the ocean becomes uproarious on seeing emperumān.

**uraippellām ninnanṟi maṟṟilēn kandāy** – other than you, there is none who could be counted as a helpful relative for me.

**enadu uyirkkōr sol nanṟi āgum tuṇai ninnanṟi maṟṟilēn kaṇḍāy** – Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had said in [carama ślōkam 18-66 in śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-66/) “*mā suca:*” (do not grieve), there is none who could say the good word “Do not grieve” other than you, when the ātmā is in any trouble.

We will take up the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 78. Tunainal perungilaiyum

*tuṇai nāḷ perungiḷaiyum tol kulamum suṟṟattu iṇai nāḷum inbudaittām ēlum kaṇai nāṇil ōvāttoḻil sārngan tol sīrai nannenjē ōvāda ūṇāga uṇ*

**Пословный перевод:**

tuṇai nāḷ – companionship (from friends) and longevity; perum kiḷaiyum – relatives in large numbers; tol kulamum – birth in an ancient clan; suṟṟattu iṇai – togetherness of relatives; nāḷum – at all times; inbudaittām ēlum – even if they are sweet; kaṇai nāṇil ovāttoḻil – having arrows constantly on the drawn bow (in order to protect followers), ready for action; sārngan – one who controls the bow called as sārngam; tol sīrai – ancient, auspicious qualities; nal nenjē – Oh my heart, which is favourable to me; ōvāda – without any hurdle; ūṇāga – as a food to be enjoyed; uṇ – enjoy

**Перевод:**

**tuṇai nāḷ perungiḷaiyum tol kulamum suṟṟattu iṇai nāḷum inbudaittāmēlum – even if  companionship of friends, longevity, relatives who gather in large numbers, birth in a long standing clan, being together with relatives, remain forever and are sweet**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that apart from emperumān, there is no other relative. In this pāsuram, he tells emperumān, as if he is instructing his heart, that even if there are relatives who are dear and even if samsāram (life in the materialistic realm) is sweet, there is nothing to enjoy other than emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kaṇai nāṇil oVattoḻil sārngan tol sīrai** – the auspicious and ancient qualities of the one who has a drawn bow in which arrows are placed, ready for combat, in order to protect his followers

**nal nenjē** – Oh my good heart which knows to distinguish between genuine and false relatives. Alternatively, we can consider this to mean the heart which is ready to listen to my instruction of attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)

**ōvāda ūṇāga uṇ** – enjoy \[those auspicious qualities of emperumān\] as sweet food which is taken regularly.

We will take up the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 79. Unnattut tesanre

*uṇṇāttut tēsanṟē ūḻvinaiyai anjumē viṇṇāttai onṟāga meccumē maṇṇāttil ārāgi evviḻi viṟṟānāgilum āḻiyangaip perāyaṟkāḷām piṟappu*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇṇāttil – on this earth; ārāgi – even if one were in lowly birth; evviḻi viṟṟānāgilum – even if (he were) engaged in lowly profession \[for a livelihood\]; āḻi am kai pēr āyaṟku – to kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who holds the beautiful divine disc on his divine hand; āḷ Am piṟappu – birth which is apt to carry out servitude; uḷ nāttu tēsu anṟē – is it not sacred similar to the resplendence of paramapadam which is very intimate (to emperumān)?; ūḻ vinaiyai – thinking of sins carried over since time immemorial; anjumē – will it be fearful?; viṇṇāttai – svarga (heaven); onṟāga meccumē – will it consider that as an entity?

**Перевод:**

**āḻiyangaip pērāyaṟku āḷām piṟappu uḷ nāttut tēsanṟē – isn’t the birth which stays focussed on carrying out kainkaryam to other human beings considered to have the resplendence of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) even if one were the lord of all, who carries the divine disc in his divine hand? āḻvār, though remaining in this leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), calls paramapadam as uḷ nādu in line with emperumān’s thinking. Since in the vyākhyānam (commentary) for this prabandam, this meaning has been given as the second interpretation, it is clear that āḻvār considers leelā vibhūti  as uḷ nādu and the birth in this realm to have resplendence. This will be clarified at the end of the commentary. It is pertinent to recall here what śrī maṇavāḷā māmunigaḷ wrote in his commentary for the 81st cūrṇika (aphorism) of ācārya hrudayam  for the portion āḻiyangaip pērāyaṟkāḷām piṟappu – uṇṇattut tēsanṟē  “Unlike the outer realm leelā vibhūti which is full of people who are highly ill disposed towards emperumān, the physical form taken by ātmā (soul) has resplendence in paramapadam which is very close to emperumān and which is favourable to him being full of great sweetness. The opinion is that just like taking the physical form which is apt for carrying out kainkaryam (service) to emperumān in paramapadam, even for ātmā, which is the object of servitude, this will create resplendence”. The term pērāyan denotes the maturity which kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) has in the qualities of being a covherd, when compared to other covherds who could be thought to be nityasūris.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had instructed his heart to stay focussed on experiencing the auspicious qualities of emperumān. In this pāsuram, he mentions about the glory of those who are focussed like that. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ūḻvinaiyai anjumē** – will those who have such a birth, be fearful of sins?

**viṇṇāttai onṟāga meccumē** – will such people consider the worlds of dēvas such as brahmā et al, as significant? Has it not been said in śrī guṇaratnakōsam 21 that going to heaven is considered lowly by these people “*yat dūrē manasō yadēvatamasa: pārē yadyadatbhutam yat kālāt apacēnimam  surapurī yadgaccatō durggati* I *sāyujyasya yadēva sūdiratavā yaddurgraham madgirām tadviṣṇō: paramam padam tava krutē māta: samāmnāsiśu:* īī” (Oh mother! vēdas state that viṣṇu’s paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)  which is (a) beyond anyone’s mind, (b) beyond primordial matter, (c) wondrous, (d) unchanged by time, (e) making those who desire to attain it to consider even heaven as being like hell, (f) granting equality with the supreme being in eight specific characteristics and (g)  beyond my speech, exists only for you)! ḍidn’t lakśmaṇa say in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 3-5 that going to heaven is similar to going to an inappropriate place “*na dēvalōkākramaṇam nāmaratvamaham vruṇē* I *aiśvaryam vā’pi lōkānām kāmayē na tvayā vinā* īī” (I,  separated from you, will not like living in śrīvaikuṇṭam which is inhabited by nityasūris, kaivalyam (soul enjoying itself) and the opulence in all the worlds)! śrī parāśara maharishi (author of śrī viṣṇu purāṇam) also said that for one who is involved with emperumān, even the position of indra is a hurdle, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-6-41 “*vāsudēvē manō yasya japahōmārcanādiśu* I *tasyāntarāyō maitrēya! dēvēndratvātikam pada(la)m* īī” (Oh maitrēya! For one whose mind is involved with vāsudēva through prayer, rituals and worship, benefits such as being the head of celestial entities etc become hindrances).

**viṇṇāttai onṟāga meccumē** – when the term *uḷ nādu* is considered to be leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) *viṇṇādu* should be considered as referring to paramapadam. These people will not think that attaining paramapadam is greater than carrying out kainkaryam to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in this world.

**maṇṇāttil ārāgi evviḻi viṟṟānālum . . . . piṟappu** – this earth, which is materialistic realm, as it is, is very lowly; in this realm, even if one were born in a lowly birth, have lowliness in knowledge and deeds etc, one should desire for enjoying emperumān and carrying out kainkaryam to him. If these are not there, even if one has greatness in birth \[being born in a great clan\], carries out great deeds and has knowledge, these will be considered to be without any purpose.

Let us take up the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 80. Pirappirappu

*piṟappiṟappu mūppup piṇi tuṟandu pinnum iṟakkavum inbudaittāmēlum maṟappellām ēdamē enṟallāl eṇṇuvanē maṇṇaḷanda pādamē ēttāp pagal*

**Пословный перевод:**

piṟappu iṟappu mūppu piṇi tuṟandu – getting rid of birth, death, old age and disease; pinnum – beyond that; iṟakkavum inbu udaittāmēlum – even if the immeasurable blissful kaivalya mōkśam (a state in which ātmā enjoys itself) occurs; maṇ aḷandān – sarvēśvara (lord of all) who measured the worlds; pādam – divine feet; ēttā – not vorśipped; pagal – that which occurs during that time; maṟappu ellām – being forgetful \[of emperumān\]; ēdamē enṟallāl eṇṇuvanē – will I think of it as anything other than being the embodiment of sorrow?

**Перевод:**

**piṟappu iṟappu mūppu piṇi tuṟandu pinnum iṟakkavum inbu udaittāmēlum– After getting rid of birth, death, old age and disease in samsāram (materialistic realm), even if immeasurable happiness results. The opinion here is that even if kaivalyam, which is the immeasurable experience of ātmā about itself occurs, as mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 7-29 “jara maraṇa mōkśāya” (the liberation which follows old age and death), tiruvāimoḻi 1-7-1 “piṟavittuyaraṟa” (to get rid of the sorrow of repeated birth and death), tiruvāimoḻi 8-3-2 “maraṇam tōṟṟam vānpiṇi mūppenṟivai māyttōm ” (we have achieved nothing other than getting rid of birth, death, terrible diseases and old age), tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-10 “kuṟugā neeḷā iṟudi kūdā enai uZhi siṟugā perugā aḷavailinbam sērndālum maṟugālinṟi māyōn! unakkēyāḷāgum siṟū kālattaiyuṟumō? andō! teriyilē” (Oh amazing entity! Even if we get the pleasure if kaivalyam in which there is no change in the basic nature of ātmā, there is no contraction or expansion etc over very long period of time, if we analyse, will it equal the pleasure of being your servitor even if it is for a very short time? Alas, it will not).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had said in the previous pāsuram that even if one were born in a lowly birth in this world, that birth in which a cētana engages in servitude to emperumān is the greatest. In this pāsuram he says that he considers even mōkśam (liberation) as a suffering if that mōkśam makes him forget emperumān. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**iṟakkavum inbudaittāmēlum** – even if immeasurable happiness results…..

**maṇṇaḷandān pādamē ēttāppagal maṟappellām ēdamē enṟallāl eṇṇuvanē** – will I consider that state \[of *kaivalyam*\] as one of happiness instead of one of sorrow if I do not praise the divine feet of emperumān who measured all the worlds and who is the epitome of simplicity, but forget about him during that time of enjoying that happiness \[resulting from that *kaivalyam*\]?

We will take up the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram next.

## 81. Pagalira enbaduvum

*pagalirā enbaduvum pāviyādu emmai igal seydu irupoḻudum āḷvar tagavāt toḻumbar ivar sīrkkum tuṇaiyilar enṟu ōrār seḻum paravai mēyār terindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

terindu seḻu paravai mēyār – one who analyses and goes to tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) to recline; ivar – this āḻvār; tagavā toḻumbar – has lowliness and not qualified (for his mercy); sīrkkum – to meditate on his (superior) qualities; tuṇai ilar – does not have support; enṟu ōrār – not analysing; pagal irā enbaduvum – that it is day time or it is night time (having or not having the qualification for experiencing emperumān’s auspicious qualities); pāviyādu – not bothered about it; emmai – this servitor; igal seydu – combating (ruling over); iru poḻudum – during both times; āḷvar – will engage (in his qualities)

**Перевод:**

**pagal irā enbaduvum pāviyādu emmai igal seydu iru poḻudum āḷvar – Without thinking that day time is for carrying out deeds and night time is for taking rest, emperumān, waging a battle with āḻvār, keeps attracting āḻvār with his auspicious qualities constantly, during both times \[day and night\] in such a way that āḻvār would feel very happy when he gets to experience those qualities and feel distressed when not experiencing them enough. There is cure for a hurt caused by an arrow, but is there a cure for a hurt caused by qualities!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that the time when he forgets emperumān, even if he is in a state of liberation, is sorrowful. In this pāsuram, he describes the way emperumān rules over him without making any distinction between day and night. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tagavāt toḻumbar ivar sīrkkum tuṇaiyilar enṟu ōrār** – he is remaining without analysing whether [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is qualified to receive his mercy due to his lowliness or that he does not have anyone with whom he could discuss about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s qualities and pass his time purposefully  just as it is mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 10-9](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/10-9/) “*maccittā madgataprāṇā bōdayanta: parasparam* I *katayantaśca mām nityam tuśyanti ca ramanti ca* īī” (having a focussed mind towards me, keeping their lives in me, informing each other (about my qualities) they \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s devotees\] keep conversing with each other and keep remaining happy).

Who is that entity?

**terindu seḻum paravai mēyār** – one who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal after analysing that it is the apt place to get followers and to experience them.

We will take up the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

## 82. Terindu unarvu

*terindu uṇarvu onṟinmaiyāl tīvinaiyēn vāḷā irundoḻindēn kīḻnāḷgaḷellām karanduruvin ammānai ajñānṟu pin todarndu āḻiyangai ammānai ēttādu ayarttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

terindu uṇarvu – knowledge which can be qualified as knowing truly; onṟu inmaiyāl – lacking in it totally; tīvinaiyēn – I, having cruel sins; ajñānṟu – during that time; karanda uruvin – hiding the form (of a demon); ammānai – that deceitful deer; pin todarnda – one who followed; āḻi am kai ammānai – emperumān who had in his beautiful hand, the signet ring; ēttādu – without praising; ayarttu – being ignorant; kīḻ nāḷgaḷellām – in the days gone by; vāḷā irundoḻindēn – I had spent purposelessly

**Перевод:**

**terindu uṇarvu onṟu inmaiyāl – not having knowledge which can be truly called as knowing. The opinion is that āḻvār feels that he did not have knowledge about matters relating to emperumān but only had useless knowledge which enabled him to know “this is eight; this in ten”. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-87 says “samjñāyatē yēna tatasdōśam śuddam param nirmalamēkarūpam I sandruśyatē vāpyadigam yatē vā tat jñānam ajñānamatō’nya dugtam īī” (only that knowledge through which one is able to know, see or attain that entity which is without any fault, pure and shining is called as knowledge; anything other than that is ignorance). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-41 says “tat karma yanna bandāya sā vidyā yā vimuktayē I āyāsāparam karma vidyānyā śilpanaipuṇam  īī ” (only that deed which is not a cause for bondage (in this materialistic realm) is called as deed; only that which is the reason for getting liberated is called as knowledge. The rest of the karmas are only for suffering; other knowledge is only apt for sculpting). Thus it was considered that only knowledge about emperumān is to be considered as knowledge and that the rest of the knowledge is only equivalent to ignorance.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār mentioned as to how he received the experience of enjoying emperumān, without any distinction between day and night, in the previous pāsuram. He feels sorrowful in this pāsuram as to how he lost that enjoyment. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tīvinaiyēn** – I, who had committed the sin of spending time purposelessly instead of experiencing enjoyment of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**vāḷā irundoḻindēn kīḻ nāḷgal ellām** – I spent time purposelessly in earlier times instead of being a little bit favourable to emperumān and receiving his mercy, just as one could pick up a few grains of paddy in a field which is fully matured with paddy cultivation.

**karandurivin ammānai ēttādu ayarttu** – I should have praised and carried out service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who was wearing the signet ring in his beautiful hand, during that time when he followed mārica who came in the form of a deer, hiding his demonic form, at a time when iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) was also not around to help him. Instead of doing that, during the time of emperumān’s vibhavāvatāra (incarnations) I spent my time purposelessly due to my ignorance. Since the reference is to cakravartty tirumagan (divine son of emperor daśarata), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is mentioning about the signet ring that śrī rāmā was having in his beautiful hand. As per the boon sought by kaikēyi, śrī rāma had to remove all the regal paraphernalia (dress, ornaments etc); however, this signet ring alone escaped that boon and went with śrī rāma to the forest. Did it not get the fortune of being shown as the identification to sītāp pirātti through hanumān! Considering that greatness, āḻvār is referring to it as *āḻiyangai ammān*.

**ēttādu ayarttu** – the term *ēttudal* here refers to praising [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and as a consequence, carrying out service to him. It is pertinent here to recall that just as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had desired to carry out service to emperumān when he went in exile to forest, emperumānār (śrī rāmānuja) too desired to carry out service to śrī rāma, as could be known from the following incident in his lifetime: A poet was composing poem to describe the action of śrī rāma who, while returning after killing the deceitful deer, kept his feet on tender leaves which he plucked from plants nearly and strew them on the path since his feet were hurting due to the scorching heat of the noon. On hearing this, [emperumānār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/) mercifully said “I was not fortunate to keep my head repeatedly in his path so that he could have walked over my head”

We will move on to the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.

## 83. Ayarppay ayarappay

*ayarppāy ayarāppāy nenjamē sonnēn uyappōm neṟi iduvē kaṇḍāy seyaṟpāla allavē seygiṟudi nenjamē anjinēn mallarnāḷ vavvinanai vāḻttu*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjamē – Oh heart!; seyaṟpāla alla – whatever has been proscribed; seygiṟudi – you are doing; anjinēn – (I am) scared; mallar nāḷ – longevity of the wrestlers; vavvinanai – one who removed; vāḻttu – please praise; uyappōm neṟi – the way to uplift; iduvē kaṇḍāy – is it not this?; nenjamē – Oh my heart!; ayarppāy – seek ways to destroy yourself, forgetting (emperumān); ayarāppāy – uplift yourself, by not forgetting (emperumān); sonnēn – I have told (you, that which is beneficial for you)

**Перевод:**

**ayarppāy ayarāppāy – destroy yourself by continuing as you had done in the past, forgetting emperumān or, seek uplifting of yourself by constantly thinking about him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Since āḻvār recalled the state in which his heart was lying from time immemorial, he faults his heart to ensure that this state does not recur. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nenjamē sonnēn** – when a person is about to fall into a well, śouldn’t a person who is standing at a distance shout out to him “Do not fall in to the well”? In the same way, I too warned you.

**uyappōm neṟī iduvē kaṇḍāy** – what I am going to tell you now is the way to uplift yourself; all other ways are only for your destruction.

When his heart asked him back “What mistake did I do”, āḻvār tells it . . . . .

**seyaṟpāla allavē seygiṟudi** – “Is there any limit for all the improper deeds which you carried out since time immemorial? Have you not carried out such improper deeds as vorśipping other deities and engaging with other matters? Even now, you are often carrying out the improper deed of moving away from emperumān saying ‘I am not qualified’ ”

**nenjamē anjinēn** – I have been scared, seeing you move away from emperumān saying “I am not suitable for you” starting with the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram *pugaḻvōm paḻippōm*.

When asked “There are many faults in us which make us become unsuitable. What do we do?” āḻvār says . . .

**mallar nāḷ vavvinanai  vāḻtu** – praise him who destroyed the wrestlers muśtika and cāṇūra. Are our faults stronger than those of the wrestlers? In order to get rid of these faults, praise emperumān who destroyed the lives of those wrestlers.

We will move on to the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 84. Vaztti avan adiyai

*vāḻtti avan adiyaip pūppunaindu nin talaiyait tāḻtti iru kai kūppu enṟāl kūppāda pāḻtta vidi enguṟṟāyenṟu avanai ēttāda en nenjamē tangattānāmēlum tangu*

**Пословный перевод:**

avan adiyai – the divine feet of that sarvēśvara (lord of all); vāḻtti – praising; pū punaindu – offering flowers; nin talaiyai tāḻttu – bow down your head (at his divine feet); iru kai kūppu enṟāl – if asked to fold your hands together \[in the gesture of offering anjali\]; kūppāda en nenjamē – Oh my mind which will not fold the hands!; avanai – that sarvēśvara; enguṟṟāy enṟu – saying “where have you gone”; ēttādu – without praying (with affection); tangattānāmēlum – if it is possible to sustain; tangu – sustain; pāḻtta vidi – how terrible is the weight of the sins?

**Перевод:**

**The pāsuram should be rephrased to vāḻtti avan adiyaip pūppunaindu nin talaiyait tāḻttu iru kai kūppu enṟāl kūppāda en nenjamē! enguṟṟāy enṟu avanai ēttādu tangattānāmēlum tangu, pāḻtta vidi in order to get cogent meaning.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār continues to fault his heart, in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**en nenjamē** – Oh my heart which does not do anything in matters regarding emperumān! āḻvār, who had praised his heart in the 1<sup>st</sup> pāsuram saying *munduṟṟa* nenjē (Oh my mind which is preceding me) is saying this now, considering its nature of not involving with matters relating to emperumān since time immemorial, before he sang the first pāsuram.

**vāḻtti avan adiyaip pūppunaindu nin talaiyait tāḻttu, iru kai kūppu enṟāl kūppāda en nenjamē** – āḻvār tells his heart “Oh heart who is not cupping your palms when I ask you to praise emperumān, offer flowers to his divine feet, bow down your head to his divine feet and cup your palms in *anajali* posture!” Since his head and hands were under the control of his heart for a very long time, āḻvār is referring to them as the head and hands of his heart.

**avanai enguṟṟāy enṟu ēttādu tangattānāmēlum tangu** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-8-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/05/thiruvaimozhi-6-8-5-nungatku/) “*enguc cenṟāgilum kaṇḍu*” (seeking and seeing wherever emperumān goes), āḻvār tells his heart, “If you can sustain yourself without seeking emperumān with fervour and praising him, do remain in one place and sustain yourself”

**pāḻtta vidi** – how terrible that such a destiny is befalling us! Through this , we can either take it that āḻvār is disgusted with himself since he keeps leaving emperumān’s presence saying “I am unsuitable” or that he is disgusted with his twin deeds (of pāpa and puṇya) which are a stumbling block for him in attaining emperumān. Since āḻvār is saying *vāḻtti* and *ēttādu*, we can consider these to belong to the faculty of speech and since he is saying *pūppunaindu* and *talaiyait tāḻttu* we can consider these to belong to the faculty of action and that āḻvār is instructing his mind that it is impossible not to involve with emperumān with all the three faculties (of thought, speech and action).

We will move on to the 85<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 85. Tanga muyarriyavay

*tangā muraṟṟiyavāyt tāḻ visumbin mīdu pāyndu engē pugundu ettavam seydittana kol pongōdat taṇṇambāl vēlai vāyk kaṇ vaḷarum ennudaiya kaṇṇan pāl nanniṟam koḷ kār*

**Пословный перевод:**

pongu ōdam – having agitating waves; taṇ ambāl vēlai vāy – in the cool, beautiful tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kaṇ vaḷarum – one who is reclining; ennudaiya – one who is my lord; kaṇṇan pāl – towards kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa); nal niṟam koḷ – having beautiful complexion; kār – clouds; tangā muyaṟṟiyavāy – having continuous efforts (to get that complexion); tāḻ visumbin mīdu – in the expansive sky; pāyndu – roaming; engē pukku – going to which place; ettavam seydittana kol – what type of penance did they carry out?

**Перевод:**

**tangā muyaṟṟīyavāyt tāḻ visumbin mīdu pāyndu engē pukku ettavam seydittana kol – without giving up the efforts which they had taken up, roaming all over the skies, which sacred place did the clouds enter and what type of penance did they carry out?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār, who had been alternating between satisfaction and dissatisfaction in enjoying emperumān, looked at the world at large. Looking at the clouds which were roaming in the sky, āḻvār involves himself with emperumān’s matter and wonders “By carrying out which penance did these clouds get the divine complexion of emperumān?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) shows the reason for having a doubt like this, in the latter half of the pāsuram . . . . .

**pongu ōdam taṇ ambāl vēlai vāyk kaṇ vaḷarum ennudaiya kaṇṇan pāl nanniṟam koḷ kār** – where did these clouds, which have the dark bluish complexion of my lord kaṇṇan, who is reclining on the cool, beautiful tiruppāṟkadal which has agitating waves, enter and what type of penance did they perform \[in order to get that complexion\]?

**engē pukku ettavam** – It is pertinent to recall that the same meaning has been conveyed in the 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of tiruviruttam \[composed by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)\] “*mēgangaḷō uraiyīr tirumāl tirumēni okkum yōgangaḷ ungaḷukku evvāṟu peṟṟīr*” (Oh clouds, please explain! How did you get the complexion of tirumāl’s \[consort of śrī mahālakśmi\] divine form?) and his response in the same pāsuram “*uyiraḷippān māgangaḷellām tirindu nannīrgaḷ sumandu nundam āgangaḷ nōva varuttum tavamām aruḷ peṟṟadē* ” (is this the result of your action of going all over the world, carrying water notwithstanding the pain in your physical forms, and showering rains, thus protecting all lives, without expecting anything in return?)

We will take up the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

## 86. Kar kalanda

*kār kalanda mēniyān kai kalanda āḻiyān pār kalanda valvayiṟṟān pāmbaṇaiyān sīr kalanda sol ninaindu pōkkārēl sūḻ vinaiyin āḻ tuyarai en ninaindu pōkkuvar ippōdu*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār kalanda mēniyān – one who has the divine form congruent with cloud; kai kalanda āḻiyān – one who has the divine disc fitting well with his divine hand; pār kalanda – (during the time of deluge) keeping the earth fittingly; val vayiṟṟān – having strong divine stomach; pāmbu aṇaiyān – emperumān who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan, his; sīr kalanda – being with superior qualities; sol ninaindu – meditating on divine names; sūḻ vinaiyin – due to the cruel sins which surround (us); āḻ tuyarai – deep sorrows; pōkkārēl – if they do not get rid of; ippōdu – during this time; en ninaindu – thinking of what (else); pōkkuvar – will they get rid of

**Перевод:**

**kār kalanda mēniyān – as mentioned in the previous pāsuram, emperumān has his divine form congruent with cloud in all manner. The word kalanda refers to the fact that he is congruent in all ways.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 84th pāsuram, āḻvār had said that it is not possible to live without involving with emperumān with all three faculties \[of thought, speech and action\]. In the 85th pāsuram, looking at the normal events in life, he said that he is reminded of the divine beauty of emperumān’s physical form which has the complexion of rain bearing clouds. In this pāsuram, he says “Leave aside the fact that it is difficult to sustain oneself without involving with that beautiful emperumān, it is not even possible to spend one’s time without that”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kai kalanda āḻiyān** – one who is having the firm disc in his divine hand. Here again, the word *kalanda* refers to the disc fitting so well with the hand that it would appear that it has taken roots in the ground called as divine hand or that it is like the flower which has blossomed out of the branch called as divine hand.

**pār kalanda val vayiṟṟān** – one who is having well fitting earth inside his divine stomach, during the time of deluge. Usage of the term *kalanda* signifies that the earth has got fully mingled with his stomach such that it cannot be detected by the waters of deluge should they enter. The term *val vayiṟṟān* signifies, just as it did in [tiruvāsairiyam 7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/thiruvasiriyam-7/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/thiruvasiriyam-7/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/thiruvasiriyam-7/) “*naḷir madic cadaiyanum*” that his divine stomach is capable of offering protection to any number of entities which may enter.

**pāmbaṇaiyān** – on who has tiruvantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) as his mattress

**sīr kalanda sol** – the sweet words which describe the beauty of his divine form and his divine qualities. Just like a garland made with different types of flowers and just like a dish made with different types of vegetables, this implies the itihāsa purāṇas (epics and historic narratives) as well as *aruḷicceyal* (4000 divya prabandam) which are strung with his variegated divine qualities. People who have cultivated taste in arts, literature etc will say that the phrase *kār kalanda mēniyān sol* refers to śrī rāmāyaṇam which describes śrī rāma as “*mēgaśyāmam mahābāhum*” (one who has a dark complexion similar to cloud and who has lengthy hands); the phrase *kai kalanda āḻiyān sol* refers to śrī bhāgavatam, mahābhāratam and śrī viṣṇu purāṇam which refer to kriṣṇa who was born with divine conch and divine disc even at the time of his birth; the phrase *pār kalanda valvayiṟṟān siṟ kalanda sol* refers to purāṇas which refer to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protecting all the worlds inside his divine stomach and the phrase *pāmbaṇaiyān sīr kalanda sol* refers to tiruvāimoḻi and other works of aruḷicceyal which describe the greatness of  divine qualities of emperumān’s as he reclines on the mattress of ādiśēśan. The term *sol* refers to emperumān’s divine names.

**sol ninaindu sūḻ vinaiyin āḻ tuyaraip pōkkārēl** – it is proper that one should think of and recite the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in order to get rid of deep sorrows which result from the sins which have to be necessarily experienced. Let them \[samsāris\] not get rid of such sins in this way.

**en ninanidu pōkkuvar ippōdu** – even if they do not have the earnestness in getting rid of sins, how will they spend their time?

Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is similar to yudistra who thinks of others as just like himself, he is mercifully stating this.

We will next move on to the last (87<sup>th</sup>) pāsuram in this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 87. Ippodum innum

*ippōdum innum inicciṟidu ninṟālum eppōdum īdē sol en nenjē eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān nammēl vinai kadivān moy kaḻalē ētta muyal*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjē – Oh mind which is well disposed towards me!; nam mēl vinai kadivān – in order to remove sins from us; eppōdum – at all times; kai kaḻalā nēmiyān – emperumān from whose hands the divine disc will not separate, his; moy kaḻalē – beautiful divine feet; ētta muyal – try and praise; ippōdum – during this time; ini – beyond this; innum siṟidu ninṟālum – even if some more time passes; eppōdum – at all times; īdē sol – keep reciting these divine names

**Перевод:**

**ippōdum innum inicciṟidu ninṟālum eppōdum – during this time, in the times to come and at all times, without losing even a moment.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When āḻvār said in the previous pāsuram “en ninaindu pōkkuvar ippōdu” (thinking of what, will they spend their time now), his heart told him “We are seeing many people in the world spending their time in many ways”. āḻvār tells his heart “Let anyone in the world spend his / her time in any way. There is no benefit for us because of that. You keep meditating on emperumān’s divine names first and foremost, all the time”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**īdē sol** – keep spending your time reciting the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**en nenjē** – Oh my heart which is in a position to hear what I am saying, after overcoming the earlier stance of not folding your hands when I had asked you to fold your hands and not praising emperumān.

**eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān** – one who always has the divine *cakkarattāḻvān* (disc) which will not get off his divine hand. Why is it so?

**nam mēl vinai kadivān** – the opinion is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is manifesting himself with the divine disc in order to rid us of our sins and hurdles.

**moy kaḻalē ētta muyal** – The word *moy* refers to beauty. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) is telling his heart to praise [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s beautiful, divine feet; *moy* would also refer to strength and āḻvār is telling his heart to praise the powerful divine feet of emperumān which will not let down those who attain them.

*īdē sol* could also be construed to praise the divine qualities of emperumān which had been mentioned in the previous pāsuram. This could also be construed to refer to praising as seen in the words *moy kaḻalaiyē ētta muyal*.

This brings us to the end of this prabandam.

periyapirātti samēda periya perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
nammāḻvār tivadigaḷē śaraṇam  
bhagavat rāmānujar tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
jīyar tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
